\displaystyle U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t_{0})\Big[U_{\hbar,0}(-t_{0})+\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{k=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{T}(\iota,k,\bm{x},t_{0};\hbar)\Big]

(3.14)

\displaystyle=U_{\hbar,0}(-2t_{0})+\mathcal{D}_{1}^{2}(t_{0})+\mathcal{D}_{2}^{2}(t_{0})+\mathcal{D}_{3}^{2}(t_{0})+\mathcal{D}_{4}^{2}(t_{0}),

where the terms
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{2}(t_{0})
,
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{2}(t_{0})
,
\mathcal{D}_{3}^{2}(t_{0})
and
\mathcal{D}_{4}^{2}(t_{0})
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}^{2}(t_{0})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{k=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}^{1},\bm{x}^{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{1}}}\sum_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\left(\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}\right)^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\times\int_{[0,t_{0}]_{\leq}^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{1}}\prod_{i=\beta_{m-1}+1}^{\beta_{m}}V^{t_{i+\tilde{\alpha}}}_{\hbar,x_{\iota(m)}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}}V^{t_{i}}_{\hbar,x^{2}_{\iota(k_{2})}}\,d\bm{t}_{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha},1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t_{0})\mathcal{T}(\iota,k_{2},\bm{x}^{2},t_{0};\hbar),

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{2}^{2}(t_{0})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},i,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-i}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{1}}}\sum_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\left(\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}\right)^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\times\int_{[0,t_{0}]_{\leq}^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{1}}\prod_{i=\beta_{m-1}+1}^{\beta_{m}}V^{t_{i+\tilde{\alpha}}}_{\hbar,x_{\iota(m)}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}}V^{t_{i}}_{\hbar,x^{2}_{\iota(k_{2})}}\,d\bm{t}_{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha},1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t_{0})\mathcal{T}(\iota,k_{2},\bm{x}^{2},t_{0};\hbar),

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{3}^{2}(t_{0})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+1+k_{2},i,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}^{1},\bm{x}^{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}-i+1}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{0}}\times\{1\}}\sum_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\left(\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}\right)^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}\int_{[0,t_{0}]_{\leq}^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t_{|\alpha|})

\displaystyle\times U_{\hbar,0}(t_{|\alpha|})\prod_{m=1}^{k_{0}}\prod_{i=\beta_{m-1}+1}^{\beta_{m}}V^{t_{i+\tilde{\alpha}}}_{\hbar,x_{\iota(m)}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}}V^{t_{i}}_{\hbar,x^{2}_{\iota(k_{2})}}\,d\bm{t}_{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha},1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t_{0})\mathcal{T}(\iota,k_{2},\bm{x}^{2},t_{0};\hbar),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{4}^{2}(t_{0})=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{0}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 6-j\}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}^{1},\bm{x}^{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\sum_{i=1,i\neq\iota(k)}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{1}}}\sum_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\left(\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}\right)^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}+1}

\displaystyle\times\int_{[0,t_{0}]_{\leq}^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t_{|\alpha|})U_{\hbar,0}(t_{|\alpha|})V^{t_{|\alpha|}}_{\hbar,x_{i}}\prod_{i=\beta_{m-1}+1}^{\beta_{m}}V^{t_{i+\tilde{\alpha}}}_{\hbar,x_{\iota(m)}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}}V^{t_{i}}_{\hbar,x^{2}_{\iota(k_{2})}}\,d\bm{t}_{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha},1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t_{0})\mathcal{T}(\iota,k_{2},\bm{x}^{2},t_{0};\hbar),

where we have sorted the fully expanded terms in those such that
k_{1}+k_{2}\leq k_{0}
and
k_{1}+k_{2}>k_{0}
. For the term
\mathcal{D}_{1}^{2}(t_{0})
we make the change of variables
t_{i}\mapsto t_{i}+t_{0}
for all
i
in the integrals in the function
\mathcal{T}
. This gives us

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{1}^{2}(t_{0})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{1}}}\sum_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\left(\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}\right)^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}\int_{[0,t_{0}]_{\leq}^{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha}}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{1}}\prod_{i=\beta_{m-1}+1}^{\beta_{m}}V^{t_{i+\tilde{\alpha}}}_{\hbar,x_{\iota(m)}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}}V^{t_{i}}_{\hbar,x^{2}_{\iota(k_{2})}}\,d\bm{t}_{|\alpha|+\tilde{\alpha},1}

(3.15)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{2}}}\left(\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}\right)^{|\alpha|}\int_{[t_{0},2t_{0}]_{\leq}^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}}\prod_{i=\beta_{m-1}+1}^{\beta_{m}}V^{t_{i}}_{\hbar,x^{2}_{\iota(m)}}\,d\bm{t}_{|\alpha|,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-2t_{0})

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\left(\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}\right)^{|\alpha|}\int_{[0,2t_{0}]_{\leq}^{|\alpha|}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}}\prod_{i=\beta_{m-1}+1}^{\beta_{m}}V^{t_{i}}_{\hbar,x^{2}_{\iota(m)}}\,d\bm{t}_{|\alpha|,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-2t_{0})=\mathcal{T}(\iota,k,(\bm{x}^{1},\bm{x}^{2}),2t_{0};\hbar),

where we have sorted the terms in a particular way before writing the product of the integrals as one integral and again we have that
\mathcal{T}(\iota,k,(\bm{x}^{1},\bm{x}^{2}),2t_{0};\hbar)
is given by (
3.9
).

For the last three terms
\mathcal{D}_{2}^{2}(t_{0})
,
\mathcal{D}_{3}^{2}(t_{0})
and
\mathcal{D}_{4}^{2}(t_{0})
we make the following type of change of variables
t_{\beta_{m}}\mapsto s_{m,\beta_{m}}
and
t_{i}-t_{i+1}\mapsto s_{m,i}
for
i\in\{\beta_{m-1}+1,\dots,\beta_{m}-1\}

m\in\{1,\dots,k\}
, where the indices will be diffrent if
\tilde{\alpha}\neq 0
. We do this for both the integrals in
\mathcal{T}
and the ones obtained from the recent Duhamel expansion. Then by recognising different terms using the definitions made above, (
3.14
) and (
3.15
) we obtain that

\displaystyle U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)={}

\displaystyle U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-\tfrac{t(\tau_{0}-2)}{\tau_{0}})\Big[U_{\hbar,0}(-2t_{0})+\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{k=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{T}(\iota,k,\bm{x},2t_{0};\hbar)U_{\hbar,0}(-2t_{0})\Big]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\tau=1}^{2}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-\tfrac{t(\tau_{0}-\tau)}{\tau_{0}})\big[\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)+\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\mathcal{R}_{\tau,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)\big].

By iterating the above arguments we obtain the following equality

\displaystyle U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)={}

\displaystyle U_{\hbar,0}(-t)+\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{k=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{T}(\iota,k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\tau=1}^{\tau_{0}}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-\tfrac{t(\tau_{0}-\tau)}{\tau_{0}})\big[\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)+\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\mathcal{R}_{\tau,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)\big].

To obtain the stated form we again make the change of variables
t_{\beta_{m}}\mapsto s_{m,\beta_{m}}
and
t_{i}-t_{i+1}\mapsto s_{m,i}
for
i\in\{\beta_{m-1}+1,\dots,\beta_{m}-1\}

m\in\{1,\dots,k\}
for the integrals in
\mathcal{T}(\iota,k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)U_{\hbar,0}(-t)
. This concludes the proof.
∎

4
Terms without recollisions

We will in this section give the first of a number of technical estimates which gives that the Duhamel expansion do converge in appropriate sense. We will here focus on the terms where we have not observed a recollision.

4.1
Estimates for fully expanded terms

Lemma 4.1
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.6
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k}}{k!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar kC^{k}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular,
\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Observation 4.2
.

The function
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)
can be expressed in two different ways firstly by Definition
3.4
and Definition
3.6
we have an explicit formula for the kernel of the operator
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
. Hence we get that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)d}}e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k}\rangle}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\hbar^{-1}(s_{m-1}-s_{m});V)e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{m}^{2}-p_{m-1}^{2})}\,\Big\}e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{p}_{0,k}d\bm{s}_{k,1}.

Secondly we can consider the original expression for the operator
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
obtained from the Duhamel expansion before any change of variables in time. Instead of preforming the change of variables in time as in the proof of the Lemma we can preform the change of variables
t_{n}\mapsto t_{n}
and
t_{i}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}(t_{i}-t_{i+1})
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
. From this change of variables and a relabelling of the the variables we obtain the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{j}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(j+1)d}}\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{m}\hbar^{-1}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}_{1,a_{k}}d\bm{p}_{0,k}d\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}d\bm{s}_{1,k},

where the functions
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}
is defined in (
3.2
) and the numbers
a_{m}
is given by

a_{m}=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\alpha_{i}-m.

The depends on the variables
\bm{\eta}
of functions
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}
is only
(\eta_{a_{m-1}+1},\dots,\eta_{a_{m}})
. This other way of expressing the function
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)
can also be used when we act with the other operators defined in the previous section. Observe that by preforming the right change of variables we can easily switch between the to ways of writing the operator.

Proof.

When we take the
L^{2}
-norm we will get two sums over the set
\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}
. In order to control this double sum we will divide into cases depending on how many points
\bm{x}
and
\bm{\tilde{x}}
have in common and where they are placed. This yields

\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|\tilde{\alpha}|}\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa),

(4.1)

where the numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)
are given by

\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})}{\rho}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{I}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}(k,\bm{\tilde{x}},\tilde{\alpha},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx,

(4.2)

where
\mathcal{I}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)\varphi
is
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi
for a fixed
\alpha
.
We will divide into the two different cases
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
and
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. For both cases we will use the second expression for the kernel given in Observation
4.2
. We start with the first case. Here we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{2k}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{i}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2j}}\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we have evaluated the integral in
x
. We observe that this has the form as the expressions considered in Lemma
2.9
and applying this Lemma yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]=\frac{\left(\rho(2\pi)^{d}\right)^{2k-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Big\{e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}.

Recalling that the function
\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})
is given by the expression

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}}-q_{\sigma^{2}_{i-1}}-p_{\sigma^{1}_{n}}+q_{\sigma^{2}_{n}})\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}}).

We get by evaluating all integrals in
\bm{q}
and
\bm{p}
except
p_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}
for all
i
the bound

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]|\leq\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{2k-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})

(4.3)

\displaystyle\times\Big|\int e^{i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q(\bm{t})\bm{\eta},\bm{\eta}\rangle}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q(\bm{\tilde{t}})\bm{\xi},\bm{\xi}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{i(\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{i}^{2},\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{a_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}+1,a_{\sigma_{i+1}^{1}}}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}+1,\tilde{a}_{\sigma^{2}_{i+1}}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})e^{-i(\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{1},k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{1}^{2},k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{\sigma_{1}^{1}}}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{\sigma^{2}_{1}}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{p}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}\Big|d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where the numbers
a_{m}
,
\tilde{a}_{m}
are the ones associated to
\alpha
and
\tilde{\alpha}
respectively as introduced above. The function
\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})

(4.4)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}(p_{i},p_{i-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}(p_{i},p_{i-1},\bm{\xi})}\prod_{i=0}^{n}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{i},p_{i},\bm{\eta})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(p_{i},p_{i},\bm{\xi})}.

The matrix
Q(\bm{t})
is given by

Q(\bm{t})=\begin{pmatrix}t_{1}I_{d}&0&0&0\\
0&t_{2}I_{d}&0&0\\
\vdots&0&\ddots&0\\
0&\cdots&0&t_{a_{k}}I_{d}\end{pmatrix}.

We will now divide into different cases depending on the absolute value of
t
’s and
\tilde{t}
’s. This is the same type of division done in the proof of Lemma
2.2
. Again we divide into the cases where
|t_{i}|\geq 1
and
|t_{i}|<1
and similar for
\tilde{t}
. This gives us
2^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}
different cases to consider and we denote each corresponding set by
B_{l}
. For each case the indices for which
|t_{i}|\geq 1
will be denoted by
J_{1}
and the remaining will be in
J_{2}
the indices for which
|\tilde{t}_{i}|\geq 1
will be denoted by
\tilde{J}_{1}
and the remaining will be in
\tilde{J}_{2}
. As in the proof of Lemma
2.2
we will write the parts of the quadratic exponential with indices in
J_{1}
and
\tilde{J}_{1}
as a inverse Fourier transform of its Fourier transform. To ensure integrability we define certain differential operators and preform integration by parts. After this division and preforming this integration by parts we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]|\leq\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\hbar\rho)^{2k-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{l=1}^{2^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}}\sum_{|\epsilon_{1}|,\dots,|\epsilon_{j_{1}}|\leq d+1}\sum_{|\nu_{1}|,\dots,|\nu_{\tilde{j}_{1}}|\leq d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\bm{1}_{B_{l}}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})

(4.5)

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{C(\epsilon_{i})}{(2\pi t_{i}))^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{C(\nu_{i})}{(2\pi\tilde{t}_{i})^{\frac{d}{2}}}|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where we have used the notation
C(\nu)=\binom{d+1}{(\nu,d+1-\lvert\nu\rvert)}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})=\int e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q_{k_{1},J_{1}}^{-1}(\bm{t})\bm{x}_{J_{1}}-2\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}},\bm{x}_{J_{1}}\rangle}e^{i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q_{k_{1},J_{2}}(\bm{t})\bm{\eta}_{J_{2}},\bm{\eta}_{J_{2}}\rangle}e^{i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q_{k_{2},\tilde{J}_{1}}^{-1}(\bm{\tilde{t}})\bm{y}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}+2\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}},\bm{y}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}\rangle}e^{i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q_{k_{2},\tilde{J}_{2}}(\bm{\tilde{t}})\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{2}},\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{2}}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{|y_{i}^{\nu_{i}}|}{(1+|y_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{|x_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}|}{(1+|x_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{i(\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{i}^{2},\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{a_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}+1,a_{\sigma_{i+1}^{1}}}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}+1,\tilde{a}_{\sigma^{2}_{i+1}}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\big[\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})\big]e^{-i(\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{1},k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{1}^{2},k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{\sigma_{1}^{1}}}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{\sigma^{2}_{1}}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{x}d\bm{y}d\bm{p}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}.

We will first estimate
|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|
. To do this we start by for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
preforming the following change of variables

\displaystyle\eta_{j}\mapsto\eta_{j}-p_{i}\quad\text{for all $j\in\{a_{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}+1,\dots,a_{\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}\}$}

\displaystyle\xi_{j}\mapsto\xi_{j}-p_{i}\quad\text{for all $j\in\{\tilde{a}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}+1,\dots,\tilde{a}_{\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}\}$}.

This yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})=\int e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q_{k_{1},J_{1}}^{-1}(\bm{t})\bm{x}_{J_{1}}-2\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}},\bm{x}_{J_{1}}\rangle}e^{i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q_{k_{2},\tilde{J}_{1}}^{-1}(\bm{\tilde{t}})\bm{y}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}+2\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}},\bm{y}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}\rangle}e^{i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q_{k_{1},J_{2}}(\bm{t})\bm{\eta}_{J_{2}},\bm{\eta}_{J_{2}}\rangle}e^{i\frac{1}{2}\langle Q_{k_{2},\tilde{J}_{2}}(\bm{\tilde{t}})\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{2}},\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{2}}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{|x_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}|}{(1+|x_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{|y_{i}^{\nu_{i}}|}{(1+|y_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{i(\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{i}^{2},\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}^{+}+\hbar l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}}))\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{i}^{2}}e^{i\langle p_{i},l^{2}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}},\bm{x},\bm{y},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi})\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\big[\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})\big]e^{-i(\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{1},k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{1}^{2},k}^{+}+\hbar l^{1}_{0}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}}))\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{x}d\bm{y}d\bm{p}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi},

where
l_{i}^{j}
is a linear function for all
i
and
j
. By applying Lemma
2.2
in the variables
p_{1},\dots,p_{n}
we get the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|\leq\tilde{C}_{d}^{n}\int\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{|y_{i}^{\nu_{i}}|}{(1+|y_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{y}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{i}^{2},\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}^{+}+\hbar l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}}))|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

(4.6)

\displaystyle\times\int\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{|x_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}|}{(1+|x_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{x}\sup_{|\gamma_{1}|,\dots,|\gamma_{n}|\leq d+1}\int\big|\partial_{p_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}\cdots\partial_{p_{n}}^{\gamma_{n}}\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\big|\,d\bm{p}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}.

We will now estimate each pice of this expression separately. Firstly we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2k}_{+}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{i}^{2},\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}^{+}+\hbar l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}}))|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}

(4.7)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\hbar^{n}t^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|s|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,ds\right)^{n}=\left(\frac{2d}{d-2}\right)^{n}\frac{\hbar^{n}t^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}.

By arguing as in the proof of Lemma
2.2
we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{4^{a_{k}}}\sum_{|\epsilon_{1}|,\dots,|\epsilon_{j_{1}}|\leq d+1}\sum_{|\nu_{1}|,\dots,|\nu_{\tilde{j}_{1}}|\leq d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}_{+}}\bm{1}_{B_{l}}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{C(\epsilon_{i})}{(2\pi t_{i}))^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{C(\nu_{i})}{(2\pi\tilde{t}_{i})^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}

(4.8)

\displaystyle\times\int\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{|x_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}|}{(1+|x_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{x}\int\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{|y_{i}^{\nu_{i}}|}{(1+|y_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{y}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\left(\frac{d}{d-2}\right)^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}.

Lastly using the definition of the functions
\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})
, equation (
4.4
), and our assumptions on the single site potential
V
we get that

\displaystyle\sup_{|\gamma_{1}|,\dots,|\gamma_{n}|\leq d+1}\int\big|\partial_{p_{1}}^{\gamma_{1}}\cdots\partial_{p_{n}}^{\gamma_{n}}\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\big|\,d\bm{p}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle 2^{(d+1)(j-k)}4^{(d+1)n}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(\frac{\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|},

where the numbers
2^{(d+1)(j-k)}4^{(d+1)n}
comes from multiple application of the product rule. From combining
eqs.

4.5
,
4.6
,
4.7
,
4.8
and
4.9
we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]|\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!},

(4.10)

where the constant
C_{d}
is only depending on the dimension. Next we turn to the case where
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. For this case there exists a smallest index
i
such that
\kappa(i)\neq i
. We will in the following denote this index by
i^{*}
. Again we use the second expression for the kernel given in Observation
4.2
, but this time we insert the function
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
defined by

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\neq i^{*}}^{k}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma^{2}}^{k}\tilde{s}_{i}\big).

We then get that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we also have made a rescaling in some of the time integrals compared to before. We note the we can freely insert
f
due to the two characteristic functions already there. We now introduce the variables
s_{0}
and
\tilde{s}_{0}
and get that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}}\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Big\{e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}e^{is_{0}\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{0}\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}.

We define the functions
\zeta(t)
for
t\geq 0
to be

\zeta(t)=\frac{1}{\max(1,t)}.

Using this function we insert the two functions
e^{(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\zeta(\hbar^{-1}t)}
and e
{}^{(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\zeta(\hbar^{-1}t)}
as they are identical
1
on our domain of integration. We will in the following use the convention
\zeta(\hbar^{-1}t)=\zeta
. So from inserting the functions we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\frac{e^{2\hbar^{-1}t\zeta}}{(2\pi)^{2}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2(j+1)}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Big\{e^{is_{m}(\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}(\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}e^{is_{0}(\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{0}(\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t\tilde{\nu}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t\nu}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we have also written the two delta functions
\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})
and
\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})
as Fourier transforms of
1
. We observe that this has the form as the expressions considered in Lemma
2.9
and applying this Lemma yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)]=\frac{\left(\rho\hbar(2\pi)^{d}\right)^{2k-n}e^{2\hbar^{-1}t\zeta}}{(2\pi)^{2}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2(j+1)}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t(\nu+\tilde{\nu})}

(4.11)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Big\{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})e^{is_{m}(\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}(\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}e^{is_{0}(\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{0}(\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}.

We will again divide into different cases depending on the absolute value of
t
’s and
\tilde{t}
’s as we did above. We do the same type of argument and obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)]\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\rho\hbar(2\pi)^{d}\right)^{2k-n}e^{2\hbar^{-1}t\zeta}}{(2\pi)^{2}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{l=1}^{2^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}}\sum_{|\epsilon_{1}|,\dots,|\epsilon_{j_{1}}|\leq d+1}\sum_{|\nu_{1}|,\dots,|\nu_{\tilde{j}_{1}}|\leq d+1}

(4.12)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}_{+}}\bm{1}_{B_{l}}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{C(\epsilon_{i})}{(2\pi t_{i})^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{C(\nu_{i})}{(2\pi\tilde{t}_{i})^{\frac{d}{2}}}|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2(k+1)}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t(\nu+\tilde{\nu})}\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)\prod_{m=0}^{k}e^{is_{m}(\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}(\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{e^{i\langle y_{i},\xi_{i}\rangle}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}^{-1}\frac{1}{2}y_{i}^{2}}e^{i\tilde{t}_{i}(\tilde{\nu}+i\zeta)}y_{i}^{\nu_{i}}}{(1+|y_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{e^{i\langle x_{i},\eta_{i}\rangle}e^{it_{i}^{-1}\frac{1}{2}x_{i}^{2}}e^{it_{i}(\nu+i\zeta)}x_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}}{(1+|x_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\prod_{i\in J_{2}}e^{it_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{2}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Big\{\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{x}d\bm{y}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}.

Recall that
i^{*}
was the first index such that
\kappa(i^{*})\neq i^{*}
and that the function
\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)
is given by

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}}-q_{\sigma^{2}_{\kappa(i)-1}}-p_{\sigma^{1}_{n}}+q_{\sigma^{2}_{n}}-l^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}_{\sigma^{2}}))\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}}).

We now do the integrals in
s_{0}
,
s_{\sigma^{1}_{i^{*}}}
,
\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}
for all
i\in\{0,\dots,n\}
all
p
and all
q
for which the index is not in
\sigma^{2}
. Note that the integrals we do in
s
and
\tilde{s}
variables are exactly the variables the
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
does not depend on. After evaluating these integrals and moving the absolute value in under the integrals we get the estimate

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})\big|\leq{}

\displaystyle\int\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{|x_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}|}{(1+|x_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{x}\int\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{|y_{i}^{\nu_{i}}|}{(1+|y_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{y}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-1}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}

(4.13)

\displaystyle\times\int|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\Big|\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})\Big|\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu},

where we have used that
l_{i^{*}}^{\kappa}(\bm{q})=q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}
and
q_{\kappa(1)-1}+l_{1}^{\kappa}(\bm{q})=q_{0}
and the notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})={}

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}(q_{\kappa(i)}+l_{i}^{\kappa}(\bm{q}),q_{\kappa(i)-1}+l_{i}^{\kappa}(\bm{q}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}(q_{i},q_{i-1},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=0}^{n}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(q_{\kappa(i)}+l_{i}^{\kappa}(\bm{q}),q_{\kappa(i)}+l_{i}^{\kappa}(\bm{q}),\bm{\eta})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{i},q_{i},\bm{\xi})}.

Again we wil consider each of the integrals separately. The integrals over
\bm{x}
and
\bm{y}
we can estimate with (
4.8
). For the integrals over
\bm{s}
and
\bm{\tilde{s}}
we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-1}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-1}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\bm{s}_{1,k-1}^{+}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k-n}^{+}\big)d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}=\frac{(\hbar^{-1}t)^{2k-n-1}}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}.

(4.14)

In the estimates of the remaining integrals we will introduce the term

\frac{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}}{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}}\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}}{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{5d+5}}{\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{5d+5}}.

Firstly we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{q}}\int\langle q_{{\kappa(i^{*})}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{5d+5}\Big|\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})\Big|\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}

(4.15)

\displaystyle\leq C^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}.

Next using that

\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}}{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{4d+4}}\leq C.

We only have to consider the integral

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\langle{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}

(4.16)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle{\nu}\rangle}{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{d+1}}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\,d\bm{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}.

Applying the estimate
\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{-d-1}\leq 1
we can use Lemma
2.6
with
q_{\kappa(i^{*})}
as “
p
” and
q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}-q_{i^{*}-1}
as “
q
”. This yields the estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nd+2}}\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\langle{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}

(4.17)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle{\nu}\rangle}{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{d+1}}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\,d\bm{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\log(\zeta)^{2}\int\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\langle{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}-q_{i^{*}-1}|}\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\prod_{m=\kappa(i^{*})+2}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\,d\bm{q}_{1,\kappa(i^{*})-1}d\bm{q}_{\kappa(i^{*})+1,n}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}.

Next we do the integral in
q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}
.
By using the estimate
\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-2}\rangle^{-d-1}\leq 1
and Lemma
2.5
we get that

\displaystyle\int\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}-q_{i^{*}-1}|}\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-2}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\,dq_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\leq C|\log(\zeta)|.

(4.18)

For the remaining integrals we apply Lemma
2.5
repeatedly. This combined with (
4.17
) and (
4.18
) gives us the estimate

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\langle{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}

(4.19)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle{\nu}\rangle}{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{d+1}}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\,d\bm{q}_{1,n}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}\leq C^{n}|\log(\zeta)|^{n+3}.

Moreover, we have that

\int\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,dq_{0}\leq\hbar^{d}C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(4.20)

From combining
eqs.

4.8
,
4.13
,
4.12
,
4.20
,
4.19
,
4.15
and
4.14
we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)]\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a_{k}}+n}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+3}}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

(4.21)

where the constant
C_{d}
only depends on the dimension. Moreover, we have used that by definition of
\zeta
we have the estimate
e^{2\hbar^{-1}t\zeta}\leq\penalty\ C
, where the constant is independent of
t
and
\hbar
. Combining (
4.1
), (
4.10
) and (
4.21
) we get the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}C_{d}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|-k}\left(\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\Big[\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+3}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}n!}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big],

where we have used that the number of elements in
\mathcal{A}(k,n)
is bounded by
2^{k}
and that the number of elements in
\mathcal{S}_{n}
is
n!
. Using our assumptions on the potential and the coupling constant we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{k}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\Big[\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+3}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}n!}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big].

Using that
\frac{n!}{(k-1)!}\leq k
,
\zeta\geq\frac{\hbar}{t}
and evaluating the sum in
n
we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k}}{k!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar kC^{k}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

As mentioned in the introduction the methodology of the proof just given follows the methodology of Eng and Erdős
[
5
]
but is not identical. Firstly we write the expressions different and use other notation. But the main difference in the proofs is that we use a simultaneous Fourier based argument to ensure that all integrals in the internal time variables is finite. That is in the notation in the above proof the
t
and
\tilde{t}
variables. For these variable a successive type of the same argument is used in
[
5
]
.

In the cases, where
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
we get from this method the factor
k
instead of
k!
as in
[
5
]
in the second term in our error estimate.

For the case where
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
we connect the techniques used to obtain the necessary integrability in the
s
and
\tilde{s}
variables with the integrability argument for the
t
and
\tilde{t}
variables. This ensures that we here do not need any derivatives of our initial data in this case. Moreover, from making this coupling we get the combinatorial factor of size
4^{n(d+1)}
in most cases. This combinatorial factor comes from our use of integration by parts as each
p_{i}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
is in four places when we have connected the two arguments. If we do not make this connection the the combinatorial factor will be of the size

4^{n(d+1)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\sigma_{i+1}^{1}-\sigma_{i}^{1})^{d+1}(\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-\sigma_{i}^{2})^{d+1},

for most cases. Hence for small values of
n
this will make a difference. Since we use uniform estimate over all cases we have obtained a smaller constant from connecting the arguments.

One disadvantage from making this “coupling” between the estimates is that we then need to preform the Duhamel expansion to higher order in recollisions.
This is also the reason that we later will use a different argument to prove that the recollision error indeed converges to zero in the limit.

Lemma 4.4
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{3}}{\tau_{0}}\frac{C^{k_{0}}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{5}\hbar}{\tau_{0}}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{2k_{0}+4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

Firstly we observe that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(4.22)

\displaystyle\leq k_{0}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle=k_{0}^{2}\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{2k_{0}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

We now fix
k=k_{1}+k_{2}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we get two sums over the set
\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}
when we take the
L^{2}
-norm. Again we will divide this double sum into cases depending on how many points they have in common. However this time we also need to keep track of how these points is chosen in relation to the two operators. With this in mind we have that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{\alpha}(-i\lambda)^{\tilde{\alpha}}

(4.23)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}+n_{2}=n\\
n_{1}\leq k_{1},n_{2}\leq k_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},n_{1},k_{2},n_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{n}_{1}+\tilde{n}_{2}=n\\
\tilde{n}_{1}\leq k_{1},\tilde{n}_{2}\leq k_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},\tilde{n}_{1},k_{2},\tilde{n}_{2})}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa),

where the sets
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},n_{1},k_{2},n_{2})
are defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},n_{1},k_{2},n_{2})=\{\bm{\sigma}\in\{1,\dots,k_{1}+k_{2}\}^{n}\,|\,\sigma_{1}<\cdots<\sigma_{n_{2}}\leq k_{2}<\sigma_{n_{2}+1}<\cdots<\sigma_{n_{1}+n_{2}}\}.

(4.24)

The numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int\mathcal{I}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}(k_{1},\bm{\tilde{x}}_{1},\tilde{\alpha}\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}(k_{2},\bm{\tilde{x}}_{2},\tilde{\alpha},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx,

where again
\mathcal{I}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)
are defined as
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)
for
\alpha
fixed. As in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we consider the case where
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
and
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
separately. The proof for the first case is analogous to the proof of the same case in Lemma
4.1
. To see this first note that from the definition of the operators
\mathcal{I}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\int_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]_{\leq}^{k_{1}}}\prod_{m=k_{2}+1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}})

\displaystyle\times\int_{[0,\frac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t]_{\leq}^{k_{2}}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{[\frac{t}{\tau_{0}},\frac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}t]_{\leq}^{k_{2}}}\int_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]_{\leq}^{k_{1}}}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}t),

where we for the last equality we have done the change of variables
s_{m}\mapsto s_{m}+\frac{t}{\tau}
for all
m\in\{1,\dots,k_{2}\}
. We see that this is almost the same form as the original operator
\mathcal{I}
expect the split time integral. Hence the proofs will be almost the same with the difference is in the estimate done in (
4.7
). For this current case this estimate will be

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2k}_{+}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]}(\bm{s}_{1,k_{1}}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{1}}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k_{1}}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{1}}}^{+})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{i}^{2},\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}^{+}+\hbar l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}}))|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[\frac{t}{\tau_{0}},\frac{\tau t}{\tau_{0}}]}(\bm{s}_{k_{1}+1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{a_{k_{1}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[\frac{t}{\tau_{0}},\frac{\tau t}{\tau_{0}}]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{k_{1}+1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{1}+1},\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{\hbar^{n}\left(\frac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t\right)^{2k_{2}-n_{2}}\left(\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}\right)^{2k_{1}-n_{1}}}{k_{1}!(k_{1}-n_{1})!k_{2}!(k_{2}-n_{2})!}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|s|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,ds\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2d}{d-2}\right)^{n}\frac{\hbar^{n}t^{2k-n}\tau_{0}^{n_{1}-2k_{1}}}{k_{1}!(k_{1}-n_{1})!k_{2}!(k_{2}-n_{2})!},

where we have used the estimate
\frac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}\leq 1
. With this modification one arrives at the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}\tau_{0}^{n_{2}-2k_{2}}}{k_{1}!(k_{1}-n_{1})!k_{2}!(k_{2}-n_{2})!}.

(4.25)

We now turn to the case when
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. The proof will be analogous to that of the same case in Lemma
4.1
. Again we will here also denote the smallest
i
such that
\kappa(i)\neq i
by
i^{*}
and we will use the second expressions for the kernels of the operators
\mathcal{I}
from Observation
4.2
. Using these we get that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k_{2}}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{2}}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k_{2}}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{k_{2}+1,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{a_{k_{2}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{k_{2}+1,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}+1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})e^{i((\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{k_{2}+1,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{a_{k_{2}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{k_{2}}^{2}}e^{-i((\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{k_{2}+1,k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}+1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}q_{k_{2}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}e^{i((\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k_{2}}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{2}}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i((\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k_{2}}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we have used the notation
\tau_{1}^{-1}=\frac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}
. The numbers
a_{m}
and
\tilde{a}_{m}
are the numbers associated to
\alpha
and
\tilde{\alpha}
respectively. Next we introduce three new variables
\tilde{s}_{0}
,
\tilde{s}_{k+1}
and depending on
i^{*}
we introduce
s_{0}
or
s_{k+1}
. If
i^{*}\leq k_{2}
we introduce
s_{0}
and
s_{k+1}
otherwise. We will assume
i^{*}\leq k_{2}
. The other case is analogous with some change of indices. By introducing these variables we get the following expression

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+3}}\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})

\displaystyle\times\delta(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{1}\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k_{2}}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{2}}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{1}\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k_{2}}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{k_{2}+1,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{a_{k_{2}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times\delta(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{k_{2}+1,k+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}+1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})e^{i((\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{k_{2}+1,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{a_{k_{2}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{k_{2}}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k+1}\frac{1}{2}q_{k_{2}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}e^{is_{0}\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{0}\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we had inserted the function
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
before introducing the new variables. The function
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
is defined by

\displaystyle f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})={}

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\neq i^{*}}^{k_{2}}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma^{2}}^{k_{2}}\tilde{s}_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=k_{2}+1}^{k}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=k_{2}+1,i\notin\sigma^{2}}^{k}\tilde{s}_{i}\big).

We define the functions
\zeta(t)
for
t\geq 0
to be

\zeta(t)=\frac{1}{\max(1,t)}.

We will set
\zeta_{i}=\zeta((\tau_{i}\hbar)^{-1}t)
for
i=0,1
. Using these numbers function we insert the three functions
e^{(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{1}\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k_{2}}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{2}}}^{+})\zeta_{1}}
,
e^{(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{1}\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k_{2}}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}}^{+})\zeta_{1}}
and e
{}^{(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{k_{2}+1,k+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}+1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\zeta_{0}}
as they are identical
1
on our domain of integration.

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\frac{e^{2\frac{t}{\tau_{1}\hbar}\zeta_{1}+\frac{t}{\tau_{0}\hbar}\zeta_{0}}}{(2\pi)^{3}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+3}}\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\tilde{\nu}_{1}(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{1}\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}}^{+})}\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{k_{2}+1,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{a_{k_{2}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{-(\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{2}}}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}}^{+})\zeta_{1}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}+1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+}\zeta_{0}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\nu(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{1}\hbar}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{2}}}^{+})}e^{-i\tilde{\nu}_{0}(\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}+1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})}e^{i((\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{k_{2}+1,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{a_{k_{2}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{k_{2}}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k+1}(\frac{1}{2}q_{k_{2}}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{0}-i\zeta_{0})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=0}^{k_{2}}e^{is_{m}(\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta_{1})}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}(\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{1}-i\zeta_{1})}\prod_{m=k_{2}+1}^{k}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}(\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu_{0}}-i\zeta_{0})}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}

\displaystyle\times f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\nu d\tilde{\nu}_{1}d\tilde{\nu}_{0}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we have also written the three delta functions as Fourier transforms of
1
. From here the proof will proceed analogous to the proof of the similar case in Lemma
4.1
. We first apply Lemma
2.9
, then we do the argument where we divide into cases depending on the size of the
t
’s and
\tilde{t}
’s. After this we evaluate the integrals in
s_{0}
,
s_{\sigma^{1}_{i^{*}}}
, all
\tilde{s}
with an index in
\sigma^{2}
, all
p
and all
q
that does not have an index in
\sigma^{2}
. After these operations we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)]\leq{}

\displaystyle C\frac{\left(\rho\hbar(2\pi)^{d}\right)^{2k-n}}{(2\pi)^{3}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{l=1}^{2^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}}\sum_{|\epsilon_{1}|,\dots,|\epsilon_{j_{1}}|\leq d+1}\sum_{|\nu_{1}|,\dots,|\nu_{\tilde{j}_{1}}|\leq d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}_{+}}\bm{1}_{B_{l}}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})

(4.26)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{C(\epsilon_{i})}{(2\pi t_{i}))^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{C(\nu_{i})}{(2\pi\tilde{t}_{i})^{\frac{d}{2}}}|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}},

where we have used that
e^{2\frac{t}{\tau_{1}\hbar}\zeta_{1}+\frac{t}{\tau_{0}\hbar}\zeta_{0}}\leq C
and the numbers
\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})
can be estimated by

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})\big|\leq\int\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{|x_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}|}{(1+|x_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{x}\int\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{|y_{i}^{\nu_{i}}|}{(1+|y_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{y}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-1}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}

(4.27)

\displaystyle\times\int|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{1}-i\zeta_{1}|}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta_{1}|}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{k_{1}}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{2}-i\zeta_{0}|}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\prod_{m=1}^{n_{2}}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{1}-i\zeta_{1}|}\prod_{m=n_{2}+1}^{n}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{2}-i\zeta_{0}|}

\displaystyle\times\Big|\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})\Big|\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}_{1}d\tilde{\nu}_{0},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})={}

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}(q_{\kappa(i)}+l_{i}^{\kappa}(\bm{q}),q_{\kappa(i)-1}+l_{i}^{\kappa}(\bm{q}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}(q_{i},q_{i-1},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=0}^{n}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{i},q_{i},\bm{\xi})}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(q_{\kappa(i)}+l_{i}^{\kappa}(\bm{q}),q_{\kappa(i)}+l_{i}^{\kappa}(\bm{q}),\bm{\eta}).

Using the definition of the function
f
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-1}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}_{1,k-1}d\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k-n}=\frac{(\hbar^{-1}t)^{2k-n-1}\tau_{0}^{n_{1}-2k_{1}}}{(k_{1}-1)!(k_{1}-n_{1})!k_{2}!(k_{2}-n_{2})!}.

Since we in general could have that
i^{*}>k_{2}
we will instead use the estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-1}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}_{1,k-1}d\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k-n}\leq\frac{(\hbar^{-1}t)^{2k-n-1}\tau_{0}^{n_{1}+1-2k_{1}}}{(k_{1}-1)!(k_{1}-n_{1})!(k_{2}-1)!(k_{2}-n_{2})!},

(4.28)

as it cover both cases. In order to estimate the integrals in
q
,
\eta
and
\xi
we insert the fraction

\frac{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{5d+5}}{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{5d+5}}\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}}{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{6d+6}}{\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{6d+6}}.

Note here that we have additional
d+1
of
\langle q_{0}\rangle
and
\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle
. This is due to the additional
\tilde{\nu}_{2}
compared to the the proof of Lemma
4.1
. We have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{q}}\int\langle q_{{\kappa(i^{*})}}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{6d+6}\Big|\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})\Big|\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}

(4.29)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,4d+4}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}.

Next we have that

\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{k^{\prime}}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{d+1}}{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{0}\rangle^{5d+5}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{5d+5}}\leq C,

where the index
k^{\prime}
is chosen to be
1
if
\kappa(i^{*})>k_{1}
and
k_{1}+1
if
\kappa(i^{*})\leq k_{1}
. We only have to consider the integral

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}_{1}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{1}-i\zeta_{1}|}\frac{1}{\langle{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta_{1}|}\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}_{2}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{k_{1}}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{2}-i\zeta_{0}|}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle{\nu}\rangle\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta_{1}|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}_{1}\rangle\langle\tilde{\nu}_{2}\rangle}{\langle q_{k^{\prime}}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{d+1}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{n_{2}}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{1}-i\zeta_{1}|}\prod_{m=n_{2}+1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{2}-i\zeta_{0}|}\,d\bm{q}_{1,n}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}_{1}d\tilde{\nu}_{2}.

Applying the estimate
\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-2}\rangle^{-d-1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{-d-1}\leq 1
we can use Lemma
2.6
with
q_{\kappa(i^{*})}
as “
p
” and
q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}-q_{i^{*}-1}
as “
q
”. After applying this lemma we integrate
q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}
by using Lemma
2.5
. For all the remaining integrals we also apply Lemma
2.5
repeatedly. This gives us the estimate

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}_{1}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{1}-i\zeta_{1}|}\frac{1}{\langle{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta_{1}|}\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}_{2}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{k_{1}}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{2}-i\zeta_{0}|}

(4.30)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle{\nu}\rangle\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta_{1}|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}_{1}\rangle\langle\tilde{\nu}_{2}\rangle}{\langle q_{k^{\prime}}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{d+1}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{n_{2}}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{1}-i\zeta_{1}|}\prod_{m=n_{2}+1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}_{2}-i\zeta_{0}|}\,d\bm{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}_{1}d\tilde{\nu}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{n}|\log(\zeta_{1})|^{n_{2}+3}|\log(\zeta_{0})|^{n_{1}+1}.

Moreover we have that

\int\langle q_{0}\rangle^{5d+5}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,dq_{0}\leq\hbar^{d}C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(4.31)

Combing the estimates in
eqs.

4.26
,
4.27
,
4.28
,
4.29
,
4.30
and
4.31
we get the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)]

(4.32)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,4d+4}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\tau_{0}^{n_{1}+1-2k_{1}}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}\hbar|\log(\zeta_{1})|^{n_{2}+3}|\log(\zeta_{0})|^{n_{1}+1}}{(k_{1}-1)!(k_{1}-n_{1})!(k_{2}-1)!(k_{2}-n_{2})!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

We observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}+n_{2}=n\\
n_{1}\leq k_{1},n_{2}\leq k_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},n_{1},k_{2},n_{2})}1\leq 2^{k}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}+n_{2}=n\\
n_{1}\leq k_{1},n_{2}\leq k_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},n_{1},k_{2},n_{2})}\frac{1}{(k_{1}-n_{1})!(k_{2}-n_{2})!}\leq\frac{2^{k}}{(k_{1}+k_{2}-n)!}.

Using these observations in combination with
eqs.

4.23
,
4.25
and
4.32
we get that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(4.33)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(\lambda C_{d}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,4d+4})^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\Big[\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{1}}k_{1}!k_{2}!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar\tau_{0}}{t})|^{n+4}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}n!}{\tau_{0}^{k_{1}}(k_{1}-1)!(k_{2}-1)!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big].

Combining this with (
4.22
) and using our assumptions on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

(4.34)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{2}}{\tau_{0}}\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{2k_{0}}C^{k}\sum_{n=0}^{k}\Big[\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar\tau_{0}}{t})|^{n+4}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}n!}{(k-2)!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big],

where we have used that

\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{2k_{0}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\frac{1}{k_{1}!k_{2}!}+\frac{1}{(k_{1}-1)!(k_{2}-1)!}=\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{2k_{0}}\frac{2^{k}}{k!}+\frac{2^{k-2}}{(k-2)!},

(4.35)

since we assume
k_{0}>1
. Using that
\frac{n!}{(k-2)!}\leq k^{2}
, evaluating the sum over
n
, maximising each term in the sum over
k
by plugging in
k_{0}+1
or
2k_{0}
and then estimating the sum over
k
by
k_{0}
(the number of terms in the sum) we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

What remains to estimate is the terms we get when we start the second expansion with the same point we ended with. Here we have the following result

Lemma 4.5
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{3}}{\tau_{0}}\frac{C^{k_{0}}}{(k_{0}+1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{5}\hbar}{\tau_{0}}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar\tau_{0}}{t})|^{2k_{0}+4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

As In the proof of Lemma
4.4
we have that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(4.36)

\displaystyle\leq k_{0}^{2}\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{2k_{0}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

We now fix
k=k_{1}+k_{2}
. As in the other proofs we get two sums over the set
\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}
when we take the
L^{2}
-norm. Again we will divide this double sum into cases depending on how many points they have in common. As before we also need to keep track of how these points is chosen in relation to the two operators. With this in mind we have that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+1}}(i\lambda)^{\alpha}(-i\lambda)^{\tilde{\alpha}}

(4.37)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}+n_{2}=n\\
n_{1}\leq k_{1}+1,n_{2}\leq k_{2}-1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{n}_{1}+\tilde{n}_{2}=n\\
\tilde{n}_{1}\leq k_{1}+1,\tilde{n}_{2}\leq k_{2}-1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\sigma^{1}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1}+1,n_{1},k_{2}-1,n_{2})\\
\sigma^{2}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1}+1,\tilde{n}_{1},k_{2}-1,\tilde{n}_{2})\end{subarray}}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa),

where the sets
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},n_{1},k_{2},n_{2})
is defined in(
4.24
).
The numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})\int\mathcal{I}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}(k_{1}+1,(\tilde{x}_{2,k_{2}},\bm{\tilde{x}}_{1}),\tilde{\alpha},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}(k_{2},\bm{\tilde{x}}_{2},\tilde{\alpha},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx,

where again
\mathcal{I}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)
are defined as
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)
for
\alpha
fixed. Again we consider the case where
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
and
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
separately. The proof for the first case is again almost analogous to the proof of the same case in Lemma
4.1
. To see this first note that from the definition of the operators
\mathcal{I}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\tilde{\alpha}\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}(k_{2},\bm{\tilde{x}}_{2},\tilde{\alpha},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]_{\leq}^{k_{1}+1}}\prod_{m=k_{2}+2}^{k+1}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m-1};V,\hbar)\Theta_{\alpha_{k_{2}+1}}(s_{k_{2}},{s}_{k_{2}+1},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}})

\displaystyle\times\int_{[0,\frac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t]_{\leq}^{k_{2}}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{[\frac{t}{\tau_{0}},\frac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}t]_{\leq}^{k_{2}}}\int_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]_{\leq}^{k_{1}+1}}\prod_{m=k_{2}+2}^{k+1}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m-1};V,\hbar)\Theta_{\alpha_{k_{2}+1}}(s_{k_{2}},{s}_{k_{2}+1},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)

\displaystyle\times\Theta_{\alpha_{k_{2}}}(s_{k_{2}-1},{s}_{k_{2}},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}-1}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}t),

where we for the last equality have done the change of variables
s_{m}\mapsto s_{m}+\frac{t}{\tau}
for all
m\in\{1,\dots,k_{2}\}
