. We see that this is almost the same form as the original operator
\mathcal{I}
expect the split time integral and we have two operators depending on the same position next to each other. Since all “dependence” of
s_{k_{2}}
is contained in the kernel of
\Theta_{\alpha_{k_{2}+1}}(s_{k_{2}},{s}_{k_{2}+1},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)\Theta_{\alpha_{k_{2}}}(s_{k_{2}-1},{s}_{k_{2}},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)
we make the change of variables
s_{k_{2}}\mapsto s_{k_{2}}-s_{k_{2}+1}
this yields

\displaystyle\int_{[\frac{t}{\tau_{0}},\frac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}t]_{\leq}^{k_{2}}}\int_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]_{\leq}^{k_{1}+1}}\prod_{m=k_{2}+2}^{k+1}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m-1};V,\hbar)\Theta_{\alpha_{k_{2}+1}}(s_{k_{2}},{s}_{k_{2}+1},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)

\displaystyle\times\Theta_{\alpha_{k_{2}}}(s_{k_{2}-1},{s}_{k_{2}},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}-1}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}t)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k+1}}\bm{1}_{[\frac{t}{\tau_{0}},\frac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}t]_{\leq}}(s_{k_{2}}+s_{k_{2}+1},s_{k_{2}-1},\dots,s_{1})\bm{1}_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]_{\leq}}(s_{k+1},s_{k},\dots,s_{k_{2}+1})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=k_{2}+2}^{k+1}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m-1};V,\hbar)\tilde{\Theta}_{\alpha_{k_{2}+1}+\alpha_{k_{2}}}(s_{k_{2}-1},s_{k_{2}},{s}_{k_{2}+1},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}-1}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{\tau}{\tau_{0}}t),

where the kernel of
\tilde{\Theta}_{\alpha_{k_{2}+1}+\alpha_{k_{2}}}(s_{k_{2}-1},s_{k_{2}},{s}_{k_{2}+1},x_{k_{2}};V,\hbar)
is given by

\displaystyle(p_{k_{2}+1},p_{k_{2}-1})\mapsto{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\hbar^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1/d}\langle x_{k_{2}},p_{k_{2}+1}-p_{k_{2}-1}\rangle}e^{is_{k_{2}+1}\hbar^{-1}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k_{2}+1}^{2}-p_{k_{2}-1}^{2})}\int e^{is_{k_{2}}\hbar^{-1}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k_{2}}^{2}-p_{k_{2}-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times\Psi_{\alpha_{k_{2}+1}}(p_{k_{2}+1},p_{k_{2}},\hbar^{-1}s_{k_{2}};V)\Psi_{\alpha_{k_{2}}}(p_{k_{2}},p_{k_{2}-1},\hbar^{-1}(s_{k_{2}-1}-s_{k_{2}+1}-s_{k_{2}});V)\,dp_{k_{2}}.

With this form we observe that this is the same type of integrals as we considered in the proof of Lemma
4.1
, where one of the operators is slightly different.
To be precise the difference is in that we in this proof treats
p_{k_{2}}
as an “
\eta
”-variable and thereby also will treat
s_{k_{2}}
as a “
t
”-variable.
Hence with an argument analogous to that used in the proof of Lemma
4.1
for the case where
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]\leq\frac{C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}(\rho t)^{2k-n}\tau_{0}^{n_{2}-2k_{2}}}{(k_{1}+1)!(k_{1}+1-n_{1})!(k_{2}-1)!(k_{2}-1-n_{2})!}.

(4.38)

Since we have treaded
s_{k_{2}}
as a “
t
” variable in the notation used in the proof of Lemma
4.1
the main difference in the arguments is in the estimate obtained in (
4.7
) in the proof of Lemma
4.1
for this case we use the estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2k+2}_{+}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]}(\bm{s}_{1,k_{1}+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{1}+1}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k_{1}+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{1}+1}}^{+})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma_{i}^{2},\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}^{+}+\hbar l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}}))|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\bm{1}_{[\frac{t}{\tau_{0}},\frac{\tau t}{\tau_{0}}]}(\bm{s}_{k_{1}+2,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{a_{k_{1}}+2,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[\frac{t}{\tau_{0}},\frac{\tau t}{\tau_{0}}]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{k_{1}+2,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{1}+2},\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{k_{2}}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\tilde{s}_{k_{2}}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{\hbar^{n+2}\left(\frac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t\right)^{2k_{2}-2-n_{2}}\left(\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}\right)^{2k_{1}+2-n_{1}}}{(k_{1}+1)!(k_{1}+1-n_{1})!(k_{2}-1)!(k_{2}-1-n_{2})!}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|s|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,ds\right)^{n+2}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2d}{d-2}\right)^{n+2}\frac{\hbar^{n+2}t^{2k-n}\tau_{0}^{n_{1}-2k_{1}-2}}{(k_{1}+1)!(k_{1}+1-n_{1})!(k_{2}-1)!(k_{2}-1-n_{2})!}.

We now turn to the case when
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. Again this part of the proof will be analogous to that of the same case in Lemma
4.1
. Again we will here also denote the smallest
i
such that
\kappa(i)\neq i
by
i^{*}
and we will use the second expressions for the kernels of the operators
\mathcal{I}
from Observation
4.2
. Using these we get that

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\int\delta(p_{k}-q_{k})

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k_{2}}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{2}}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k_{2}}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{k_{2}+1,k+1}^{+}+\bm{t}_{a_{k_{2}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,(\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{k_{2}+1,k+1}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}+1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})e^{i((\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{k_{2}+1,k+1}^{+}-\bm{t}_{a_{k_{2}}+1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{k_{2}}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i((\tau_{0}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{k_{2}+1,k+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}+1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}q_{k_{2}}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}-1}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}\Big\}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k_{2}},p_{k_{2}+1}-p_{k_{2}-1}\rangle}e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{k_{2}},q_{k_{2}+1}-q_{k_{2}-1}\rangle}\Big\{\prod_{m=k_{2}+1}^{k+1}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m-1},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}\Big\}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}e^{i((\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k_{2}}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k_{2}}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i((\tau_{1}\hbar)^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k_{2}}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k_{2}}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}.

We observe that this is the same form as the same in case in the proof of Lemma
4.4
with more complicated relations between the indices. Keeping this in mind the argument is analogous to that made in the proof of Lemma
4.4
. We then obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)]

(4.39)

\displaystyle\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,4d+4}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\tau_{0}^{n_{1}+2-2k_{1}}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}\hbar|\log(\zeta_{1})|^{n_{2}+3}|\log(\zeta_{0})|^{n_{1}+1}}{k_{1}!(k_{1}+1-n_{1})!(k_{2}-2)!(k_{2}-1-n_{2})!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

From combining (
4.36
), (
4.37
), (
4.38
), (
4.39
) and arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.4
we obtain the desired estimate.
This concludes the proof.
∎

4.2
Estimates for truncated terms

We now turn to estimating the truncated terms without recollisions. Our first estimate is given in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.6
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.6
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+1}}\mathcal{E}^{k_{0}}_{0,0}(\bm{x},\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k_{0}}}{\hbar k_{0}!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-1}\hbar}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3}C^{k_{0}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular,
\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+1}}\mathcal{E}^{k_{0}}_{0,0}(\bm{x},\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

From definition of the operator
\mathcal{E}^{k_{0}}_{0,0}(\bm{x},t;\hbar)
we get that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+1}}\mathcal{E}^{k_{0}}_{0,0}(\bm{x},\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}}:\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(4.40)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}}\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+1}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{0}}\times\{1\}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{0}}_{0,0}(\bm{x},s_{k_{0}+1},\alpha,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,ds_{k_{0}+1},

where we have used the unitarity of
U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k_{0}+1})
and
U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k_{0}+1})
, Jensen’s inequality and the operator
\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{0}}_{0,0}(\bm{x},s_{k_{0}+1},\alpha,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)
is given by

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{0}}_{0,0}(\bm{x},s_{k_{0}+1},\alpha,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)=\int_{[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]_{\leq}^{k_{0}}}\bm{1}_{[s_{k_{0}+1},t]}(s_{k_{0}})\prod_{m=1}^{k_{0}+1}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k_{0},1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t).

We observe that the right hand side in (
4.40
) is essentially the same expression as the one considered in the proof of Lemma
4.1
, where the “last” time integral is outside the
L^{2}
-norm. From arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+1}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{0}}\times\{1\}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}^{k_{0}}_{0,0}(\bm{x},s_{k_{0}+1},\alpha,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\,ds_{k_{0}+1}

(4.41)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{k_{0}}}{\hbar k_{0}!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-1}\hbar}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3}C^{k_{0}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where we for the estimate corresponding to in the estimate in (
4.14
) from the proof of Lemma
4.1
for this case will be integratig
k_{0}-1
variables all in belonging to the interval
[0,\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}]
. By combining (
4.40
) and (
4.41
) we obtain the state estimate and this concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}+1}}\mathcal{E}_{0,0}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{k_{0}}k_{0}^{3}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar k_{0}!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{6}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{2k_{0}+3}C^{k_{0}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}+1}}\mathcal{E}_{0,0}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{k_{0}}k_{0}^{3}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar k_{0}!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{6}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{2k_{0}+3}C^{k_{0}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

Firstly by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.6
we obtain an expression similar to the one estimated in Lemma
4.4
, but with the last time integral outside of the
L^{2}
-norm. Then using an argument analogous to that used in the proof of Lemma
4.4
or Lemma
4.5
we obtain an estimate that put together with the estimate obtained by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.6
gives us the stated estimate.
∎

5
Terms with one recollisions

We will in this section continue with the technical estimates which gives that the Duhamel expansion do converge in appropriate sense. We will here focus on the terms where we have observed a single recollision.

5.1
Estimates for fully expanded terms

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.12
. Let
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k}}{(k-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar k|\log(\zeta)|^{k+4}C^{k}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we get two sums over the set
\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}
, when we take the
L^{2}
-norm. Again we divide into different cases depending on how many points
x_{m}
and
\tilde{x}_{m}
they have in common and where they are placed. This yields

\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(k-1,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|\tilde{\alpha}|}\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa),

(5.1)

where the numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{x},\tilde{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})\int\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{\tilde{x}},\tilde{\alpha},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx,

where the operators
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)
is defined as
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)
for a fixed
\alpha
. We will again divide into the two different cases depending on if
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
or
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. We start with the case where
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
. For this case we divide further into the two cases depending on the relation between the map
\iota
and the two vectors
\sigma^{1}
and
\sigma^{2}
. First case is if there exist
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
in
\{1,n+1\}
such that following condition is satisfied

\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{1}\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\sigma_{i_{1}}^{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma_{i_{2}-1}^{2}\leq m_{1}<m_{2}<\sigma_{i_{2}}^{2}.

(5.2)

We will in this case use the following expression for the function
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{is_{m}\hbar^{-1}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\iota(m)},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}.

We start by making the change of variables
p_{m_{2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{2}-1}-p_{m_{2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-2\}
, where
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
are the numbers associated to the map
\iota
. Moreover we also do a relabelling by changing
p_{m_{2}-1}
into
\tilde{p}
and
p_{m}
into
p_{m-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{2},\dots,k\}
.This yields the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m_{2}}\hbar^{-1}\frac{1}{2}p_{m_{2}-1}^{2}}

(5.3)

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{m_{2}},\tilde{p}+p_{m_{2}},\bm{\eta})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k-1}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k-1}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\hbar^{-1}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}),p_{m-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where
\iota^{*}
is the function associated to
\iota
and

\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}&\text{if $m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-1\}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}&\text{if $m\in\{m_{1}+1,\dots,m_{2}-1\}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(5.4)

With this expression we can write down
\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})
and use Lemma
2.9
to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]=\frac{(\rho(2\pi)^{d})^{2k-n-2}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{n+2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})

(5.5)

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}-1}^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{2}-1}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\big\{\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}^{1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-1}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-1}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{q}))^{2}}\big\}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{1})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{1}(\tilde{p}))^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\tilde{q}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+}-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}d\tilde{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we have used our assumption on the two indices
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
given in (
5.2
) and evaluated all integrals involving delta functions. Moreover, we have used the notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,

\displaystyle\tilde{\alpha})=\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{i_{1}-1},\tilde{p}+p_{i_{1}-1},\bm{\eta})\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{i_{2}-1},\tilde{q}+p_{i_{2}-1},\bm{\xi})}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-1}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}}(m)}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{q}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{q}),\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}}(\sigma_{i}^{1})}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{1}(\tilde{p}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\tilde{q}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\tilde{q}),\bm{\xi})}.

We note that this form is almost identical to the form of the function considered in the proof of Lemma
4.1
. The main difference is the dependence on the variables
\tilde{p}
and
\tilde{q}
.

We note that
\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}})\neq 0
for
\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{1}\leq m<\sigma_{i_{1}}^{1}
and
\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}})\neq 0
for
\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{2}\leq m<\sigma_{i_{1}}^{2}
. Hence we preform the following changes of variables
\tilde{p}\mapsto\tilde{p}+p_{i_{1}-1}
and
\tilde{q}\mapsto\tilde{q}_{1}+p_{i_{2}-1}
. This change of variables ensures that in all quadratic phases we only have one variable. Hence we can argue as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
to ensure integrability in the
t
’s and
\tilde{t}
’s and case by case use Lemma
2.2
directly for the all variables
p_{1},\dots,p_{n},\tilde{p},\tilde{q}
. Preforming these arguments we arrive at the bound

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]|\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-2-n}}{(k-1)!(k-1-n)!}.

(5.6)

We now turn to the case where such to indices does not exists. For this case we can without loss of generality assume that there exists
i_{1}
such that
m_{1}<\sigma^{1}_{i_{1}}<m_{2}
. We will here use the same expression as above for the function
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi
but with a rescaling of the time variables
s_{1},\dots,s_{k}
. Hence with the same notation (the functions
\pi^{1}_{m}
and
\pi^{2}_{m}
) as above we consider the expression

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]=\frac{(\rho(2\pi)^{d}\hbar)^{2k-n-2}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})

(5.7)

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}-1}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{2}-1}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\big\{\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}^{1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-1}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-1}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{q}))^{2}}\big\}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{1})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{1}(\tilde{p}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\tilde{q}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{i(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+}-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}d\tilde{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we in this case have that
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})=\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(q_{\sigma_{i(m_{2})}^{1}},\tilde{p}+q_{\sigma_{i(m_{2})}^{1}},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m_{2}}}(q_{\sigma_{\tilde{i}(m_{2})}^{2}},\tilde{q}+q_{\sigma_{\tilde{i}(m_{2})}^{2}},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma^{2}_{i})}}(q_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{q}),q_{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{q}),\bm{\xi})}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(\sigma^{1}_{i})}}(q_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}),q_{\sigma_{i-1}^{1}}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=0}^{n}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(q_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{q}),q_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{q}),\bm{\xi})}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(q_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}),q_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\bm{\eta}),

where
i(m_{2})
is the largest index such that
m_{2}\geq\sigma^{1}_{i(m_{2})}
and
\tilde{i}(m_{2})
is the largest index such that
m_{2}\geq\sigma^{2}_{\tilde{i}(m_{2})}
.
From this form the proof is similar to the case of
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
in the proof of Lemma
4.1
. We first insert a function
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
of some of the time variables as in the other proof. Again it will have the form that the sum over some of the
s
and
\tilde{s}
variables is bounded by
\hbar^{-1}t
. For
\bm{s}
it will not depend on
s_{m_{1}}
and
s_{m_{2}-1}
. For
\tilde{s}
it will not depend on
\tilde{s}_{m_{2}-1}
and
\tilde{s}_{m}
for
m\in\iota^{*}(\sigma^{2})
. For the cases, where
m_{2}-1\in\iota^{*}(\sigma^{2})
it will also not depend on
m_{2}
. Hence for
\tilde{s}
there are always
n+1
variables it does not depend on. The function can be written as

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i\in\mathcal{B}}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i\in\tilde{\mathcal{B}}}s_{i}\big),

where
\mathcal{B}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{B}}
are index set as described above. As in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we introduce the two variables
s_{0}
and
\tilde{s}_{0}
and thereby also two delta functions. We again use the function
\zeta
and insert the two functions
e^{(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\zeta(\hbar^{-1}t)}
and
e^{(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})\zeta(\hbar^{-1}t)}
, write the delta functions as Fourier transforms of
1
. Then we use Lemma
2.9
and the standard argument to obtain integrability in the
t
and
\tilde{t}
variables and we evaluate all integrals in
\tilde{s}
and
s
the the function
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
does not depend on. We first assume that we are in the case where
m_{2}-1\in\iota^{*}(\sigma^{2})
. Preforming these operations we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]|\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\rho\hbar(2\pi)^{d}\right)^{2k-2-n}e^{2\hbar^{-1}t\zeta}}{(2\pi)^{2}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{l=1}^{4^{a_{k}}}\sum_{|\epsilon_{1}|,\dots,|\epsilon_{j_{1}}|\leq d+1}\sum_{|\nu_{1}|,\dots,|\nu_{\tilde{j}_{1}}|\leq d+1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a_{k}}}_{+}}\bm{1}_{B_{l}}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})

(5.8)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{C(\epsilon_{i})}{(2\pi t_{i})^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{C(\nu_{i})}{(2\pi\tilde{t}_{i})^{\frac{d}{2}}}|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|d\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}d\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}},

where

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{I}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})\big|\leq{}

\displaystyle\int\prod_{i\in J_{1}}\frac{|x_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}|}{(1+|x_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{x}\int\prod_{i\in\tilde{J}_{1}}\frac{|y_{i}^{\nu_{i}}|}{(1+|y_{i}|^{2})^{d+1}}\,d\bm{y}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-3}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}\int\frac{|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}

(5.9)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}+q_{l_{1}})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}+q_{l_{2}})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\sigma^{2}_{m}}+\pi_{\sigma^{2}_{m}}(\tilde{q}))^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\Big|\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})\Big|\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{q}d\tilde{p}d\tilde{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}.

where the indices
l_{1}<l_{2}
and they depend on
\sigma^{1}
. That the indices
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
are different is due to the assumption that there exsist
i_{1}
such that
m_{1}<\sigma^{1}_{i_{1}}<m_{2}
. The index
m^{*}
is chosen such that
\sigma_{m^{*}}=m_{2}-1
. Again we need to estimate these integrals. Firstly we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-3}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}\leq\frac{(\hbar^{-1}t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-2)!(k-n-1)!}.

(5.10)

In order to estimate the integrals in
\tilde{p}
,
\tilde{q}
,
q
,
\eta
and
\xi
we insert the fraction

\frac{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}}{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}}\frac{\langle\tilde{q}\rangle^{5d+5}\langle\tilde{p}\rangle^{2d+2}}{\langle\tilde{q}\rangle^{5d+5}\langle\tilde{p}\rangle^{2d+2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\tilde{q})-q_{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}-\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{2}(\tilde{q})\rangle^{5d+5}}{\langle q_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\tilde{q})-q_{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}-\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{2}(\tilde{q})\rangle^{5d+5}}.

Firstly we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{q}}\int

\displaystyle\langle\tilde{q}\rangle^{5d+5}\langle\tilde{p}\rangle^{2d+2}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle q_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{1}(\tilde{q})-q_{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}-\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{2}(\tilde{q})\rangle^{5d+5}\Big|\partial_{\bm{\xi}_{\tilde{J}_{1}}}^{\bm{\nu}}\partial_{\bm{\eta}_{J_{1}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})\Big|\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}

(5.11)

\displaystyle\leq C^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}.

Using that

\frac{\langle\tilde{p}+q_{l_{2}}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}+q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{l_{2}}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}\rangle^{d+1}}{\langle\tilde{p}\rangle^{2d+2}\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}\langle\tilde{q}\rangle^{4d+4}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{\langle q_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\tilde{q})-q_{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}-\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{2}(\tilde{q})\rangle^{4d+4}}\leq C,

(5.12)

what remain to estimate is the integral

\displaystyle\int\frac{\langle\nu\rangle^{-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle^{-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}+q_{l_{2}}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}+q_{l_{1}})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle q_{l_{2}}\rangle^{-d-1}\langle\tilde{q}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}

(5.13)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}\rangle^{-d-1}\langle\tilde{p}+q_{l_{2}}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}+q_{l_{2}})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{1}(\tilde{q})-q_{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}-\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{2}(\tilde{q})\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\sigma^{2}_{m}}+\pi_{\sigma^{2}_{m}}(\tilde{q}))^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\,d\bm{q}d\tilde{p}d\tilde{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}.

To estimate this integral we first use Lemma
2.6
to do the integral in
\tilde{p}
. This gives us two powers of
|\log(\zeta)|
and the factor
|q_{l_{2}}-q_{l_{1}}|^{-1}
. We then use the bound
\leq 1
and do the
q
integrals starting from
q_{n}
and skipping the integral in
q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}
using Lemma
2.5
. We then to the integral in
\tilde{q}

q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}
,
\nu
and
\tilde{\nu}
in that order using Lemma
2.5
. This gives us the estimate

\displaystyle\int\frac{\langle\nu\rangle^{-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle^{-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}+q_{l_{2}}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}+q_{l_{1}})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle q_{l_{2}}\rangle^{-d-1}\langle\tilde{q}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}

(5.14)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle q_{\sigma^{2}_{m^{*}}}\rangle^{-d-1}\langle\tilde{p}+q_{l_{2}}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}+q_{l_{2}})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{1}(\tilde{q})-q_{\sigma_{i-1}^{2}}-\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{2}(\tilde{q})\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\sigma^{2}_{m}}+\pi_{\sigma^{2}_{m}}(\tilde{q}))^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\,d\bm{q}d\tilde{p}d\tilde{q}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}\leq C|\log(\zeta)|^{n+4}.

Combining the estimates in
eqs.

5.8
,
5.9
,
5.10
,
5.11
,
5.12
,
5.13
and
5.14
we arrive at

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]|\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+4}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-2)!(k-n-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(5.15)

The case where
m_{2}-1\notin\iota^{*}(\sigma^{2})
is estimated with an analogous argument and the same estimate as in (
5.15
) is obtained.

We now turn to the case where
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. As in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we will by
i^{*}
denote the first number such that
\kappa(i)\neq i
. For this case we also use the expression for
\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)
given in (
5.7
), where the difference is that in the delta function we now have
\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}}
instead of
\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}
. Again we start by introducing the a function
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
of some of the time variables as above. Again it will have the form that the sum over some of the
s
and
\tilde{s}
variables is bounded by
\hbar^{-1}t
. For
\bm{s}
it will not depend on
s_{\iota^{*}(i^{*})}
and
s_{m_{2}-1}
. If
\iota^{*}(i^{*})=m_{2}-1
it will not depend on
s_{m_{2}}
and
s_{m_{2}-1}
. For
\tilde{s}
it will not depend on
\tilde{s}_{m_{2}-1}
and
\tilde{s}_{m}
for
m\in\iota^{*}(\sigma^{2})
. For the cases, where
m_{2}-1\in\iota^{*}(\sigma^{2})
it will also not depend on
s_{m_{2}}
. From here the argument is analogous to the argument we have just done combined with the arguments in the proof of Lemma
4.1
for the case, where
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. Redoing the argument we get the bound

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)]|\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}+\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+4}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-2)!(k-n-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(5.16)

Combining
Equations

5.1
,
5.6
,
5.15
and
5.16
, using our assumptions on the coupling constant and the potential and argue as in the previous proofs we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k}}{(k-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar k|\log(\zeta)|^{k+4}C^{k}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the potential and the coupling constant. This is the desired estimate and this concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},1,1}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{9}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}(k_{0}-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar\frac{k_{0}^{12}}{\tau_{0}}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+8}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

We start by observing that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},1,1}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\leq Ck_{0}^{6}\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{2k_{0}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}}\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where we have used that the number of elements in
\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}
is bounded by a constant times
k^{2}
for all
k
. This follows from Remark
3.11
. As in the proof of Lemma
4.4
we fix a
k=k_{1}+k_{2}
and expand the
L^{2}
-norm and get that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{\alpha}(-i\lambda)^{\tilde{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}+n_{2}=n\\
n_{1}\leq k_{1},n_{2}\leq k_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},n_{1},k_{2},n_{2})}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{n}_{1}+\tilde{n}_{2}=n\\
\tilde{n}_{1}\leq k_{1},\tilde{n}_{2}\leq k_{2}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},\tilde{n}_{1},k_{2},\tilde{n}_{2})}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa),

the sets
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(k_{1},n_{1},k_{2},n_{2})
is defined in (
4.24
).
The numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{I}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}(k_{1},\bm{\tilde{x}}_{1},\tilde{\alpha},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}(k_{2},\bm{\tilde{x}}_{2},\tilde{\alpha},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx,

where again
\mathcal{I}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\alpha,\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)
are defined as
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)
for
\alpha
fixed. Again we distinguish the two cases
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
and
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. In the first case we will spilt into a further number of cases depending on the relations between
\sigma^{1}
,
\sigma^{2}
and the map
\iota
as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
. To estimate each of these cases we make an argument that is a combination of the argument made in the proofs of Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
5.1
. Making this argument we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

(5.18)

\displaystyle\leq C^{2k}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\Big[\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-2-n}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}}{(k_{1}-1)!(k_{2}-1)!(k-1-n)!}+\frac{\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar\tau_{0}}{t})|^{n+8}\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}n!\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{1}}(k_{1}-2)!(k_{2}-2)!(k-1-n)!}\Big].

From combining (
5.17
), (
5.18
) and arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.4
we obtain the desired bound.
This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},1,1}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{k_{0}^{9}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}(k_{0}-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar\frac{k_{0}^{12}}{\tau_{0}}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+8}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

The estimate is obtained by an argument analogous to that of Lemma
4.5
combined with the arguments used in the proof Lemma
5.3
.
∎

5.2
Estimates for truncated terms

Lemma 5.4
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.12
. Let
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+1,1,1}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}}}\mathcal{E}_{1,1}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{6}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-1}\hbar(k_{0}-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{7}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+5}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

The estimates follows from combining the arguments in the proofs of Lemma
4.6
and Lemma
5.1
. Moreover we also use that the number of maps in
\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}
grows quadratic in
k
.
∎

The proof of the following Lemma also follow from combining arguments made in earlier proofs. Hence we will not write it down explicitly.

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+k_{2}+1,1,1}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{1,1}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{8}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-1}\hbar(k_{0}-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{11}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+8}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+k_{2}+1,1,1}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}}}\mathcal{E}_{1,1}^{k_{0}+1}((x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{8}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-1}\hbar(k_{0}-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{11}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+8}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

6
Terms with two recollisions

We will in this section continue with the technical estimates which gives that the Duhamel expansion do converge in appropriate sense. We will here focus on the terms where we have observed two recollisions.

6.1
Estimates for fully expanded terms

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.12
. Let
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,1}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k_{0}}}{(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+k_{0}C^{k_{0}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

(6.1)

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

As in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we get two sums over the set
\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}
, when we take the
L^{2}
-norm. Again we divide into different cases depending on how many points
x_{m}
and
\tilde{x}_{m}
they have in common and where they are placed. This yields

\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{n=0}^{k-2}\sum_{\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(k-2,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|\tilde{\alpha}|}\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa),

(6.2)

where the numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{\bm{x},\tilde{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})\int\mathcal{I}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}(k,\bm{\tilde{x}},\tilde{\alpha},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx,

where the operators
\mathcal{I}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)
is defined as
\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)
just for a fixed
\alpha
. As in the previous proofs we will split the estimate into a number of cases. We will in all cases use the following form for the function we consider

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\iota(m)},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}.

Recall that since
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,1}
we have the numbers
m_{1}
,
m_{2}
and
m_{3}
associated to the map
\iota
and the “adjoint map”
\iota^{*}
. We start by making the change of variables
p_{m_{2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{2}-1}-p_{m_{2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-2\}
, where
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
are the numbers associated to the map
\iota
.
Moreover we also do the change of variables
p_{m_{3}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{3}-1}-p_{m_{3}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{3}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{3}-2\}\setminus\{m_{2}-1\}
. This yields after a relabelling

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-2}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

(6.3)

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m_{2}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{m_{2}-1},\tilde{p}_{1}+p_{m_{2}-1},\bm{\eta})e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{3}}\frac{1}{2}p_{m_{3}-2}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{3}}}(p_{m_{3}-1},\tilde{p}_{2}+p_{m_{3}-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k-2}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota}^{*}(m)}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{m-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})\Big\}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where we have used the following definition of the functions

\displaystyle\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{2}&\text{if $m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-1\}$}\\
\tilde{p}_{2}&\text{if $m\in\{m_{2},\dots,m_{3}-2\}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}\quad\quad\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{2}&\text{if $m\in\{m_{1}+1,\dots,m_{2}-1\}$}\\
\tilde{p}_{2}&\text{if $m\in\{m_{2},\dots,m_{3}-2\}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

How we will estimate the different terms will depend on
\kappa
, and the relation between the two
\sigma
’s and
\kappa
. Firstly we split into the case if
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
or
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
. We start with the case where
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
, here we again divide into two different cases. First case is if there exist
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
in
\{1,n+1\}
such that following condition is satisfied

\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{1}\leq m_{1}<m_{3}-2<\sigma_{i_{1}}^{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma_{i_{2}-1}^{2}\leq m_{1}<m_{3}-2<\sigma_{i_{2}}^{2}.

Second case is the converse. Assume such two indices exist. For this case we get after applying Lemma
2.9
and evaluating all integrals involving delta functions that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]=\frac{(\rho(2\pi)^{d})^{2k-n-4}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{n+4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i_{1}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2})^{2}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{3}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}-1}^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i_{2}-1}+\tilde{q}_{2})^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m_{3}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{2}-1}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}^{1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-1}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-1}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{1})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+}-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{G}(\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}d\tilde{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we have used the notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{p},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})=\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{i_{1}-1},\tilde{p}_{1}+p_{i_{1}-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{i_{2}-1},\tilde{q}_{1}+p_{i_{2}-1},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{3}}}(p_{i_{1}-1},\tilde{p}_{2}+p_{i_{1}-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\{\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-1}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}}(m)}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),\bm{\xi})}\Big\}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{3}}}(p_{i_{2}-1},\tilde{q}_{2}+p_{i_{2}-1},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}}(\sigma_{i}^{1})}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),\bm{\xi})}.

We note that
\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}})\neq 0
and
\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}})\neq 0
for
\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{1}\leq m<\sigma_{i_{1}}^{1}
and
\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}})\neq 0
and
\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{q}})\neq 0
for
\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{2}\leq m<\sigma_{i_{1}}^{2}
. Hence we preform the following changes of variables
\tilde{p}_{1}\mapsto\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{2}+p_{i_{1}-1}
,
\tilde{p}_{2}\mapsto\tilde{p}_{2}+p_{i_{1}-1}
,
\tilde{q}_{1}\mapsto\tilde{q}_{1}+\tilde{q}_{2}+p_{i_{2}-1}
and
\tilde{q}_{2}\mapsto\tilde{q}_{2}+p_{i_{2}-1}
. This change of variables ensures that in all quadratic phases we only have one variable. Hence we can argue as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
to ensure integrability in the
t
’s and
\tilde{t}
’s and case by case use Lemma
2.2
directly for the all variables
p_{1},\dots,p_{n},\tilde{p}_{1},\tilde{p}_{2},\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2}
. Preforming these arguments we arrive at the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n+4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n-4}}{(k-2)!(k-n-2)!}.

(6.4)

Now we turn to the case where such two indices does not exists. Here we have that there exists an
i^{*}
such that

m_{1}<\sigma_{i^{*}}^{1}\leq m_{3}-2\quad\text{or}\quad m_{1}<\sigma_{i^{*}}^{2}\leq m_{3}-2.

(6.5)

For this case we will use the “
\nu
”-representation of the operators. By again first introducing a suitable chosen
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
inserting this function and introduce the additional variable
s_{0}
. We introduce the function
\zeta
again and insert the functions
e^{(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\zeta(\hbar^{-1}t)}
and
e^{(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\zeta(\hbar^{-1}t)}
. Then we do an analogous argument to that of Lemma
5.1
to obtain the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}+\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+7}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-5}}{(k-3)!(k-n-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(6.6)

What remains is the case where
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. For this case we need just to keep track of the first
i
such that
\kappa(i)\neq i
and use an analogous argument to that used in the proof of Lemma
4.1
for the case, where
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. This yields the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)]\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}+\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+7}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-5}}{(k-3)!(k-n-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(6.7)

By combining (
6.2
), (
6.4
), (
6.6
), (
6.7
) and arguing as in the proofs for the previous estimates we get the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k_{0}}}{(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+k_{0}C^{k_{0}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(6.8)

This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.12
. Let
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{I}_{2,2}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k_{0}}}{(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+k_{0}C^{k_{0}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

(6.9)

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
6.1
. The same form for the function
\mathcal{I}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi
is used here. However, we here do the change of variables variables
p_{m_{1,2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{1,2}-1}-p_{m_{1,2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{1,2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1,2},\dots,m_{1,2}-2\}
, where
m_{1,1}
and
m_{1,2}
are numbers associated to the map
\iota
.
Moreover we also do the change of variables
p_{m_{2,2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{2,2}-1}-p_{m_{2,2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{2,2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{2,1},\dots,m_{2,2}-2\}\setminus\{m_{1,2}-1\}
, where again
m_{2,1}
and
m_{2,2}
are numbers associated to the map
\iota
. After this change of variable and a relabelling the proof proceeds as the proof of Lemma
6.1
by dividing into different cases depending on the relations between the pairing relations and the numbers associated to
\iota
.
∎

We will omit the proofs of the following Lemmas. Both proofs are done by combining arguments from previous proofs.

Lemma 6.3
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,1}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{12}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{13}C^{k_{0}}\hbar}{\tau_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,1}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{k_{0}^{12}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{13}C^{k_{0}}\hbar}{\tau_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on
\rho
,
t
, the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Lemma 6.4
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,2}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{I}_{2,2}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,2}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{15}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}\hbar}{\tau_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+10}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,2}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{I}_{2,2}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{k_{0}^{15}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}\hbar}{\tau_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+10}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

6.2
Estimates for truncated terms

Lemma 6.5
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.12
. Let
j\in\{1,2\}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+1,2,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}-1}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{13}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-4}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

The proof is done by combining the arguments of the previous proofs. We do remark that in the case
j=1
we can get
k_{0}^{9}
instead of
k_{0}^{12}
but we have choosen to give the general estimate.
∎

The proof of the following Lemma is also done by combining arguments from previous proofs. Here we have also chosen to give a general estimate for both types of configurations. For
j=1
we could have had
k_{0}^{11}
and not
k_{0}^{14}
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.14
. Let
j\in\{1,2\}
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any

\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+k_{2}+1,2,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}-1}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,j}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{15}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{17}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-4}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+k_{2}+1,2,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}-1}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{k_{0}+1}((x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,j}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k_{0}^{15}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{17}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-4}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

7
Recollision error terms for
\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,1}

We will in this section prove estimates for some of the terms in the “recollision”-error. The techniques used will mostly be the same as in the previous section. We will however in this case need to use some different techniques when we estimate the terms, where
\kappa
is the identity.

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.15
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1},2,1}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq k_{0}^{16}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

From the definition of the operator
\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,t;\hbar)
(Definition
3.13
) we get that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k},2,1}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k,0,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(7.1)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{Ck_{0}^{8}t}{\hbar^{2}}\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k},2,1}\sum_{l=1,\iota(l)\neq k}^{k-2}\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,ds_{k+1},

where we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)=\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\bm{1}_{[s_{k+1},t]}(s_{k})V^{s_{k+1}}_{\hbar,x_{l}}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m+k_{1})};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t).

As in the previous proofs we get two sums over the set
\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}
, when we take the
L^{2}
-norm. Again we divide into different cases depending on how many points
x_{m}
and
\tilde{x}_{m}
they have in common and where they are placed. This yields

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,ds_{k+1}

(7.2)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{n=0}^{k-2}\sum_{\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(k-2,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa),

where the numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)=\sum_{\bm{x},\tilde{\bm{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{t}\int\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{\tilde{x}},\tilde{\alpha},l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dxds_{k+1}.

As in the previous proofs we will split the estimate into a number of cases. However we will in all cases use the following form for the function we consider

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+1}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{l},p_{k+1}-p_{k}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{k+1}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{k+1}^{2}}\hat{V}(p_{k+1}-p_{k})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\iota(m)},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}.

Recall that since
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,1}
we have the numbers
m_{1}
,
m_{2}
and
m_{3}
associated to the map
\iota
and the “adjoint map”
\iota^{*}
.
We start by preforming the change of variables
p_{m_{2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{2}-1}-p_{m_{2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-2\}
and the change of variables
p_{m_{3}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{3}-1}-p_{m_{3}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{3}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{3}-2\}\setminus\{m_{2}-1\}
. Finally we preform the change of variables
p_{k}\mapsto p_{k}-p_{k+1}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{k}
for all
m\in\{l,\dots,k-1\}\setminus\{m_{2}-1,m_{3}-1\}
This yields after a relabelling

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi(x)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-2}\rangle}e^{is_{k+1}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{k-2}^{2}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{3}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m_{3}-2}+\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{l<m_{3}\}})^{2}}\hat{V}(-\tilde{p}_{3})

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m_{2}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2}+\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{l<m_{2}\}})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{m_{2}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2}+\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{l<m_{2}\}},\tilde{p}_{1}+p_{m_{2}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2}+\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{l<m_{2}\}},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k-2}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota}^{*}(m)}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{m-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{3}}}(p_{m_{3}-2}+\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{l<m_{3}\}},\tilde{p}_{2}+p_{m_{3}-2}+\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{l<m_{3}\}},\bm{\eta})e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where we have used the following definition of the functions

\displaystyle\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}})=\tilde{p}_{1}\bm{1}_{\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-1\}}(m)+\tilde{p}_{2}\bm{1}_{\{m_{1},\dots,m_{3}-2\}}(m)+\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{\iota^{*[-1]}(l),\dots,k-2\}}(m)

\displaystyle\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}})=\tilde{p}_{1}\bm{1}_{\{m_{1}+1,\dots,m_{2}-1\}}(m)+\tilde{p}_{2}\bm{1}_{\{m_{1}+1,\dots,m_{3}-2\}}(m)+\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{\iota^{*[-1]}(l+1),\dots,k-2\}}(m),

where
\iota^{*[-1]}
is the inverse of the map
\iota^{*}
with the convention that
\iota^{*[-1]}(m_{1})=m_{1}
. How we will estimate the different terms will depend on
\kappa
, and the relation between the two
\sigma
’s and
\kappa
. Firstly we split into the case if
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
or
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
. We start with the case where
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
here we will further split into the four cases
l>m_{3}
,
m_{3}>l>m_{1}
with

,

and

.
We start with the case
l>m_{3}
. Observe then we need
k\geq 8
for this to be possible. Here we split into different cases depending on
\sigma^{1}
and
\sigma^{2}
. Firstly we assume that
\sigma^{1}_{n},\sigma^{2}_{n}\leq l-2
1
1

1

It is indeed
l-2
here since we assume
l>m_{2}
and hence have that if
\sigma^{1}_{n},\sigma^{2}_{n}\leq l-2
then will
\iota^{*}(\sigma^{1}_{n}),\iota^{*}(\sigma^{2}_{n})\leq l
.
and that there exists two indices

and

in

such that

\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{1}\leq m_{1}<m_{3}-2<\sigma_{i_{1}}^{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma_{i_{2}-1}^{2}\leq m_{1}<m_{3}-2<\sigma_{i_{2}}^{2}.

Note that if such indices exists in the case
k=8
, then
n
can be at most
2
. In general we have that

otherwise such indices can not exists. For this case we get after applying Lemma
