and evaluating all integrals involving delta functions that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)]=\frac{(\rho(2\pi)^{d})^{2k-n-4}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{n+4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+1}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+s_{k+1}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})

(7.3)

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i_{1}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2})^{2}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m_{3}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}-1}^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i_{2}-1}+\tilde{q}_{2})^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m_{3}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{2}-1}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma_{n}^{1}+1}^{k-1}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}\prod_{m=\sigma_{n}^{2}+1}^{k-1}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{1})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-1}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-1}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{G}(\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})e^{i\hbar^{-1}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\hbar(\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+}))\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where the function
\mathcal{G}(\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})=\hat{V}(-\tilde{p}_{3})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{i_{1}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2},\tilde{p}_{1}+p_{i_{1}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2},\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{3}}}(p_{i_{1}-1},\tilde{p}_{2}+p_{i_{1}-1},\bm{\eta})

(7.4)

\displaystyle\times\overline{\hat{V}(-\tilde{q}_{3})}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{i_{2}-1}+\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{1}+p_{i_{2}-1}+\tilde{q}_{2},\bm{\xi})}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{3}}}(p_{i_{2}-1},\tilde{q}_{2}+p_{i_{2}-1},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma_{n}^{1}+1}^{k-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{n}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{n}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})\prod_{m=\sigma_{n}^{2}+1}^{k-1}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{n}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),p_{n}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{1})}}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),\bm{\xi})}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-1}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),\bm{\xi})}\Big\}.

We now preform the change of variables
\tilde{p}_{1}\mapsto p_{i_{1}-1}+\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{2}
,
\tilde{p}_{2}\mapsto p_{i_{1}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2}
,
\tilde{p}_{3}\mapsto p_{n}+\tilde{p}_{3}
,
\tilde{q}_{1}\mapsto p_{i_{2}-1}+\tilde{q}_{1}+\tilde{q}_{2}
,
\tilde{q}_{2}\mapsto p_{i_{2}-1}+\tilde{q}_{2}
,
\tilde{q}_{3}\mapsto p_{n}+\tilde{q}_{3}
. After this change of variables the argument is analogous to the case of
\kappa=\mathrm{id}
in the proof of Lemma
4.1
, where we use the integration by parts trick. Here we will do it in all
\bm{\eta}
,
\bm{\xi}
,
p_{1},\dots,p_{n}
,
\tilde{p}_{1},\tilde{p}_{2},\tilde{p}_{3},\tilde{q}_{1},\tilde{q}_{2}
and
\tilde{q}_{3}
if either
\sigma_{n}^{1}<l-2
or
\sigma_{n}^{2}<l-2
. If
\sigma_{n}^{1}=\sigma_{n}^{2}=l-2
, we cannot use the argument in the variable
p_{n}
. The difference in the two arguments will be in how we estimate the time integrals. The time integrals will in this case be

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k+1}}\tfrac{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{3}}+\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{1}}^{m_{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\sum_{m=m_{2}-2}^{\sigma_{i_{1}}^{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{2}}^{\sigma_{i_{1}}^{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\hbar l^{1}_{i_{1}-1}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{-\frac{d}{2}}}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{2}-1}^{1}}^{\sigma_{i_{2}}^{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\tilde{s}_{m_{3}}-\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{2}-1}^{2}}^{m_{1}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\sum_{m=m_{2}-2}^{\sigma_{i_{2}}^{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\hbar l^{1}_{i_{2}-1}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

(7.5)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
i\neq i_{1}-1,i_{2}-1\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\tfrac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+s_{k+1}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{\sigma_{i+1}^{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\sum_{m=\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\hbar l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=\sigma_{n}^{1}}^{l-3}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\sum_{m=\sigma_{n}^{2}}^{l-3}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+l^{1}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\tfrac{1}{[\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\tilde{l}^{1}_{1,p}(\bm{t})|)\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{2}}+\sum_{m=m_{2}}^{m_{3}-2}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\tilde{l}^{1}_{2,p}(\bm{t})|)\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=l-2}^{k-2}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\tilde{l}^{1}_{3,p}(\bm{t})|)]^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\tfrac{1}{[\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\tilde{l}^{1}_{1,q}(\bm{\tilde{t}})|)\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}+\sum_{m=m_{2}}^{m_{3}-2}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\tilde{l}^{1}_{2,q}(\bm{\tilde{t}})|)\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=l-2}^{k-2}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\tilde{l}^{1}_{3,q}(\bm{\tilde{t}})|)]^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where all functions of
t
are linear functions. They are determined by which
t_{i}
’s and
\tilde{t}_{i}
are larger than one or smaller. Moreover, we have included the integral over
s_{k+1}
. In particular we have that all functions with a tilde are a sum of either
t
’s or
\tilde{t}
’s hence they are positive and we get something larger by dropping them. Note the in the case, where
\sigma_{n}^{1}=\sigma_{n}^{2}=l-2
we have that the term with the sums ranging over

to

will not be there. Due to this we will also just drop this term in the following estimate. Doing this and using the definition of the map
\iota^{*}
we get that the integrals can be bounded by

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k+1}}\tfrac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+s_{k+1}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{2}-1}^{1}}^{\sigma_{i_{2}}^{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\tilde{s}_{m_{3}}-\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{2}-1}^{2}}^{m_{1}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\sum_{m=m_{2}-2}^{\sigma_{i_{2}}^{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\hbar l^{1}_{i_{2}-1}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{3}}+\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{1}}^{m_{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\sum_{m=m_{2}-2}^{\sigma_{i_{1}}^{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{2}}^{\sigma_{i_{1}}^{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\hbar l^{1}_{i_{1}-1}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
i\neq i_{1}-1,i_{2}-1\end{subarray}}^{n-1}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{\sigma_{i+1}^{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\sum_{m=\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{\sigma_{i+1}^{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}+\hbar l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}s_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=m_{2}}^{m_{3}-1}s_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=l}^{k}s_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=m_{2}}^{m_{3}-1}\tilde{s}_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\sum_{m=l}^{k}\tilde{s}_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

It is important to observe that each
s
and
\tilde{s}
only appears once. We note that for the characteristic functions we have the estimate

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+s_{k+1}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\leq\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,m_{1}-1}^{+}+\bm{s}_{m_{3},l-1}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{l,k}^{+})

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+}+s_{k})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{m_{2},m_{3}-1}^{+}+s_{k})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,m_{1}-1}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{s}}_{m_{3},l-1}^{+}-g(\tilde{s})+s_{k+1})

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{l,k}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+}+\tilde{s}_{k})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{m_{2},m_{3}-1}^{+}+\tilde{s}_{k}),

where
g(\tilde{s})=\sum_{i=1,i\neq i_{1}-1,i_{2}-1}^{n-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}+\tilde{s}_{m_{3}}+\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i_{1}-1}^{2})}\bm{1}_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}
.
We will further more make the following change of variables

\displaystyle s_{m}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{j=m}^{k}s_{j}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{s}_{m}\mapsto\sum_{j=m}^{k}\tilde{s}_{j}\qquad\text{for all $m\in\{l,\dots,k\}$},

\displaystyle s_{m}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{j=m}^{m_{3}-1}s_{j}+s_{k}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{s}_{m}\mapsto\sum_{j=m}^{m_{3}-1}\tilde{s}_{j}+\tilde{s}_{k}\qquad\text{for all $m\in\{m_{2},\dots,m_{3}-1\}$},

\displaystyle s_{m}

\displaystyle\mapsto\sum_{j=m}^{m_{2}-1}s_{j}+s_{k}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{s}_{m}\mapsto\sum_{j=m}^{m_{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{j}+\tilde{s}_{k}\qquad\text{for all $m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-1\}$}.

Using the estimate for the characteristic function and evaluating all integrals except the ones we have just made a change of variables we obtain that our initial integral is bounded by the expression

\displaystyle C^{n-1}\frac{\hbar^{n-1}t^{2(l-m_{3}+m_{1})-n}}{(l-m_{3}+m_{1}-1)!(l-m_{3}+m_{1}-n+1)!}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k-l}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k-l+1}}(\bm{s}_{k,l})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{l}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{l,k-1}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m_{2}-m_{1}}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{2}-m_{1}+1}}(s_{k},\bm{s}_{m_{2}-1,m_{1}})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{1}}-s_{k}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m_{3}-m_{2}}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{3}-m_{2}}}(s_{k},\bm{s}_{m_{3}-1,m_{2}})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{2}}-s_{k}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{m_{2},m_{3}-1}\,ds_{k}\Big]^{2}.

Evaluating the integral in
s_{l}
,
s_{m_{1}}
and
s_{m_{2}}
we get the bounds

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k-l}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k-l+1}}(\bm{s}_{k,l})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{l}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{l,k-1}\leq\frac{4\hbar}{d-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k-l-1}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k-l-1}}(\bm{s}_{k-1,l+1})}{(1+\hbar^{-1}s_{k})^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{l+1,k-1},

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m_{2}-m_{1}}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{2}-m_{1}+1}}(s_{k},\bm{s}_{m_{2}-1,m_{1}})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{1}}-s_{k}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\hbar}{d-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}}(\bm{s}_{m_{2}-1,m_{1}+1})}{(1+\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{1}+1}-s_{k}|)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{m_{1}+1,m_{2}-1},

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m_{3}-m_{2}}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{3}-m_{2}+1}}(s_{k},\bm{s}_{m_{3}-1,m_{2}})}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{2}}-s_{k}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{m_{2},m_{3}-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\hbar}{d-2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m_{3}-m_{2}-1}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{3}-m_{2}-1}}(\bm{s}_{m_{3}-1,m_{2}+1})}{(1+\hbar^{-1}|s_{m_{2}+1}-s_{k}|)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}\,d\bm{s}_{m_{2}+1,m_{3}-1}.

Applying these estimates and evaluating the integral in
s_{k}
, which gives an additional power of
\hbar
, and then the remaining integrals we obtain that the initial time integral in (
7.5
) is bounded by

\displaystyle C^{n}\frac{\hbar^{n+7}t^{2k-6-n}}{(l-m_{3}+m_{1}-1)!(l-m_{3}+m_{1}-n+1)!((m_{3}-m_{2}-1)!(m_{2}-m_{1}-1)!(k-l-1)!)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{n}\frac{\hbar^{n+7}t^{2k-6-n}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}.

Using this estimate and the methods used in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we can obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\frac{\hbar^{3}\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2(k-3)-n}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}.

(7.6)

We now turn to the case where
\sigma^{1}_{n}>l-2
or
\sigma^{2}_{n}>l-2
or there exists
i^{\prime}
such that
m_{1}<\sigma_{i^{\prime}}^{1}<m_{3}
or here exists
i^{\prime}
such that
m_{1}<\sigma_{i^{\prime}}^{2}<m_{3}
. This is the case where we have at least one genuine recollision happening between the internal recollisions. Again we have the expression from (
7.3
), but we have done a change of variables so we get the following expression

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]=\frac{(\rho\hbar(2\pi)^{d})^{2k-n-4}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+1}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\hbar\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+s_{k+1}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\hbar\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+s_{k+1}+\hbar\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+})e^{is_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i_{1}-1}+\tilde{p}_{2})^{2}}e^{is_{m_{3}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}-1}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i_{2}-1}+\tilde{q}_{2})^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m_{3}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{2}-1}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma_{n}^{1}+1}^{k-1}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}\prod_{m=\sigma_{n}^{2}+1}^{k-1}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{is_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{1})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i}^{2})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-1}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}^{2}+1}^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-1}e^{-is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i-1}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\times e^{i(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{k}}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha})|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where
\mathcal{G}
is defined in (
7.4
).
For this case we will use the “
\nu
-representation” of the kernel. Hence we again we insert a suitable
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
, where the dependence on
\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}}
is determined by the relation between the
\sigma
’s and
m_{1}
,
m_{2}
,
m_{3}
and
l
. It will depend on
k-4
of the
s
variables and
k-n-2
of the
\tilde{s}
variables, where we count
s_{k+1}
as a
\tilde{s}
variable. From here the argument is analogous that of Lemma
4.1
and one can obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]|\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\frac{\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}\hbar^{3}|\log(\zeta)|^{n+7}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(7.7)

In the special case where
n=1
and the numbers
\sigma_{1}^{1}
and
\sigma_{1}^{2}
satisfies that
\sigma_{1}^{2}=l-2
,
\sigma^{1}_{1}>l-2
or
m_{1}<\sigma_{1}^{1}<m_{3}
one has to combine the methods from above with the “
\nu
-representation”. For the notation used above one has to use method based on Fourier transform for the
\tilde{q}
variables and for the other “side” (the
\tilde{p}
variables) one uses the method of the “
\nu
-representation”.

We now turn to the case where
m_{3}>l>m_{1}
with
l\neq m_{2}
. For this case we can use the “
\nu
-representation” of the kernel and argue as above to obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]|\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\frac{\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}\hbar^{3}|\log(\zeta)|^{n+7}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(7.8)

for all
\sigma^{1}
and
\sigma^{2}
. For the cases where
m_{1}\geq l
we can argue as above and obtain that if
\sigma_{n}^{1},\sigma_{n}^{2}<l
the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]|\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\frac{\hbar^{3}\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2(k-3)-n}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}.

(7.9)

If this is not the case we can use the “
\nu
-representation” of the kernel and obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\mathrm{id})]|\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\frac{\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}\hbar^{3}|\log(\zeta)|^{n+7}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(7.10)

We now turn to the case where
\kappa\neq\mathrm{id}
. The estimates for all of these cases will be done by using the “
\nu
-representation” of the kernel. For this case we will also have to divide into different cases but for all cases we will obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\kappa)]|\leq C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\frac{\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}\hbar^{3}|\log(\zeta)|^{n+7}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(7.11)

This estimate is obtained analogous to the previous estimates. Combining our estimates in
eqs.

7.1
,
7.6
,
7.7
,
7.8
,
7.10
,
7.9
and
7.11
and arguing as in the previous proofs we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k},2,1}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k,0,\bm{x},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle k_{0}^{12}\hbar\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}C^{2k}\sum_{n=0}^{k-2}\Big[\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-5-n}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}+\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-5}|\log(\zeta)|^{n+7}n!}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle k_{0}^{16}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.15
and let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,1}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 5\}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq k_{0}^{18}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,1}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 5\}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1}+1,k_{2},(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq k_{0}^{18}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

We observe that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,1}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 5\}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle Ck_{0}^{10}\sum_{k=5}^{2k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,1}}\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\bm{1}_{\{1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq k_{0}\}}

\displaystyle\times\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

Moreover, we have for fixed
k,k_{1},k_{2}
and
\iota
that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t}{\hbar^{2}}\sum_{l=1,\iota(l)\neq k}^{k-2}\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{2}}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x}_{2},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,ds_{k+1},

To estimate the average of these terms we combine the arguments in the proof of Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
7.1
. From this we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\sum_{k_{1}+k_{2}=k}\bm{1}_{\{1\leq k_{1},k_{2}\leq k_{0}\}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\hbar\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}C^{2k}\sum_{n=0}^{k-2}\Big[\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-5-n}}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}+\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-5}|\log(\zeta)|^{n+9}n!}{(k-4)!(k-n-2)!}\Big].

Finally by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
7.1
we obtain the desired estimate. To obtain the second estimate an analogous argument is used combined with the arguments in the proof of Lemma
4.5
.
∎

8
Recollision error terms for
\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}

For this case we will have to distinguish between three types of configurations. Recall that for each
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}
we have the four numbers
m_{1,1},m_{1,2},m_{2,1}
and
m_{2,2}
, where the first two numbers keep track of the first internal recollision and the two last ones the second. The first type of configurations is the configurations such that

\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{3}=\{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}\,|\,m_{1,1}=k-3,m_{2,1}=k-2\}.

(8.1)

These are the configurations, where we will have to estimate two terms born series. Note that for all
k\geq 4
the set
\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{3}
will only contain one map/configuration. The second type of configurations is

\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{2}=\{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}\setminus\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{3}\,|\,m_{1,1}<m_{2,1}<m_{1,2}\}.

(8.2)

The third type of configurations are the remaing configurations

\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{1}=\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}\setminus(\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{2}\cup\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{3}).

(8.3)

We will treat each of these cases with slightly different methods. The estimate for configurations in
\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{1}
will be analogous to those used for configurations in
\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,1}
. The argument for configurations in
\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{2}
will be based only on the resolvent method. For certain configurations in
\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{3}
we will need to continue the expansion. This will depend on the “new” recollision we stopped at.

8.1
Estimates for
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{1}

Lemma 8.1
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.15
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{1}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq k_{0}^{19}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

We will only sketch the proof as it is analogous to that used for
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,1}
. We split into the two different cases
m_{2,1}>m_{1,2}
and
m_{2,1}<m_{1,1}
. We then write up the
L^{2}
-norm as in the previous proofs and get these long sums over different configurations. For the functions we obtain this we preform the change of variables
p_{m_{1,2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{1,2}-1}-p_{m_{1,2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{1,2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1,2},\dots,m_{1,2}-2\}
, and the change of variables
p_{m_{2,2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{2,2}-1}-p_{m_{2,2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{2,2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{2,1},\dots,m_{2,2}-2\}\setminus\{m_{1,2}-1\}
, where again
m_{1,1}
,
m_{1,2}
,
m_{2,1}
and
m_{2,2}
are the numbers associated to the map
\iota
. Finally we preform the change of variables
p_{k}\mapsto p_{k}-p_{k+1}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{k}
for all
m\in\{l,\dots,k-1\}\setminus\{m_{2,2}-1,m_{1,2}-1\}
. This we do in both cases.

After this we then write up the the averages of the inner products using Lemma
2.9
. For each of these terms we now need to estimate these using either the Fourier method or the resolvent method. In general for every case where we have a crossing coming from either
l
being between
m_{1,1}
and
m_{1,2}
or between
m_{2,1}
and
m_{2,2}
or from one of the index es
\sigma
being between
m_{1,1}
and
m_{1,2}
or between
m_{2,1}
and
m_{2,2}
or
\sigma_{n}>l
we will use the resolvent method. When such a crossing is not present we will use the Fourier method.
∎

The proof of the following Lemma follows as the other proofs of similar lemmas done in the previous sections.

Lemma 8.2
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.15
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,2}^{1}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 4\}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq k_{0}^{21}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,2}^{1}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 4\}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1}+1,k_{2},(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq k_{0}^{21}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

8.2
Estimates for
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{2}

Lemma 8.3
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.15
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{2}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq k_{0}^{19}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

To obtain these estimates we again write up the
L^{2}
-norm as in the previous proofs and get these long sums over different configurations. For the functions we obtain this we preform the change of variables
p_{m_{1,2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{1,2}-1}-p_{m_{1,2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{1,2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1,2},\dots,m_{1,2}-2\}
, and the change of variables
p_{m_{2,2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{2,2}-1}-p_{m_{2,2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{2,2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{2,1},\dots,m_{2,2}-2\}\setminus\{m_{1,2}-1\}
, where again
m_{1,1}
,
m_{1,2}
,
m_{2,1}
and
m_{2,2}
are the numbers associated to the map
\iota
. Finally we preform the change of variables
p_{k}\mapsto p_{k}-p_{k+1}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{k}
for all
m\in\{l,\dots,k-1\}\setminus\{m_{2,2}-1,m_{1,2}-1\}
. After this we then write up the the averages of the inner products using Lemma
2.9
. For each of these terms we now need to estimate these using the resolvent method. We always use the resolvent method to estimate these since we always have a crossing present.
∎

Lemma 8.4
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.15
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,2}^{2}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 4\}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq k_{0}^{21}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,2}^{2}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 4\}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1}+1,k_{2},(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq k_{0}^{21}C^{2k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar,

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

8.3
Estimates for
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{3}

For this case we will have to restart our expansion as the collision almost behave as a single collision happening within two potentials. It is only to estimate these terms that we are restricting the dimension to be three. Hence in this section we always assume
d=3
.

Lemma 8.5
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.15
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and for all
k
let
\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,2,2}^{3}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k,0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\hbar k_{0}^{10}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C\tau_{0}^{-3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{10}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+20},

where the constants
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

From the definition of the operator
\mathcal{E}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k,0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)
we have that

\displaystyle\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k,0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

(8.4)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\hbar^{2}}\big\lVert\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}\int_{0}^{t}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k+1})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k+1})\sum_{l=1}^{k-4}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\,ds_{k+1}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle+2\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\frac{(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}}{\hbar}\int_{0}^{t}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k+1})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k+1})\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,k-3,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\,ds_{k+1}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)=\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\bm{1}_{[s_{k+1},t]}(s_{k})V^{s_{k+1}}_{\hbar,x_{l}}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m+k_{1})};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t).

We start by estimating the average of the first
L^{2}
-norm. From applying Lemma
2.11
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}\int_{0}^{t}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k+1})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k+1})\sum_{l=1}^{k-4}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\,ds_{k+1}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

(8.5)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle 2k_{0}^{4}\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{l=1}^{k-4}\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\lambda^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\sum_{n=0}^{k-2}\binom{k-2}{n}n!\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-2,n)}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},l;\hbar)]\big|,

where we have used the notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},l;\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{(\bm{x},\tilde{\bm{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k-4}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{i}})}{\rho}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{t}\int\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{\tilde{x}},\tilde{\alpha},l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dxds_{k+1}.

Before we evaluate the averages
\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},l;\hbar)]
we will consider the expressions for the functions. By definition we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{l},p_{k+1}-p_{k}\rangle}e^{is_{k+1}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{k+1}^{2}}\hat{V}(p_{k+1}-p_{k})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\iota(m)},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}.

Firstly we preform the following change of variables in the mentioned order
p_{k-2}\mapsto p_{k-2}-p_{k-1}
,
p_{k-3}\mapsto p_{k-3}-p_{k-2}
,
p_{k}\mapsto p_{k}-p_{k+1}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{k}
for all
m\in\{l,\dots,k-3\}
and a relabelling this gives us the expression

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(|\alpha|+2)d}}\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}+\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-2}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{k+1}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{k-2}^{2}}\hat{V}(-\tilde{p}_{3})e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{3})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k}}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{3},\tilde{p}_{2},\bm{\eta})e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k-1}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{2}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-1}}(\tilde{p}_{2},\tilde{p}_{2}+\tilde{p}_{1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{2})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{2},\tilde{p}_{1}+p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{3},\bm{\eta})e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{3}+p_{k-3})^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{3}+p_{k-3},p_{k-4}+\tilde{p}_{3},\bm{\eta})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k-2}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}\Big\{e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{p}_{3}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{p}_{3}),p_{m-1}+\pi_{m-1}(\tilde{p}_{3}),\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}(t-\bm{s}_{1,k+1}^{+}-\hbar\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where we have used the notation

\pi_{m}(\tilde{p}_{3})=\tilde{p}_{3}\bm{1}_{\{l,\dots,k-4\}}(m).

This is the expression for the function we will be working with for this case.
To estimate these averages we can argue as in the proofs of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{T}(k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},l;\hbar)\big]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\overline{\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}-\tilde{s}_{k+1}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{3})^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{k}\frac{1}{2}(q_{k-2}+\tilde{q}_{3})^{2}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k-1}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{2}^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{k-1}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{2}^{2}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{2})^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{1}+\tilde{q}_{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{3}+p_{k-3})^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{1}+\tilde{q}_{3}+q_{k-3})^{2}}\prod_{m=0}^{k-4}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{m}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{p}_{3}))^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}(q_{m}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{q}_{3}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where the function
f
is given by

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\begin{cases}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-4}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+1}\big)&\text{if $\sigma_{n}\leq l$}\\
\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\neq l}^{k-4}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-3}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+1}\big)&\text{if $\sigma_{n}>l$}.\end{cases}

The function
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)=\hat{V}(-\tilde{p}_{3})\overline{\hat{V}(-\tilde{q}_{3})}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k}}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{3},\tilde{p}_{2},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k}}(q_{k-2}+\tilde{q}_{3},\tilde{q}_{2},\bm{\eta})}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-1}}(\tilde{p}_{2},\tilde{p}_{2}+\tilde{p}_{1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-1}}(\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{2}+\tilde{q}_{1},\bm{\eta})}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{2},\tilde{p}_{1}+p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{3},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{q}_{1}+\tilde{q}_{2},\tilde{q}_{1}+q_{k-3}+\tilde{q}_{3},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(\tilde{p}_{1}+\tilde{p}_{3}+p_{k-3},p_{k-4}+\tilde{p}_{3},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-3}}(\tilde{q}_{1}+\tilde{q}_{3}+q_{k-3},q_{k-4}+\tilde{q}_{3},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{p}_{3}),p_{m-1}+\pi_{m-1}(\tilde{p}_{3}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{q}_{3}),q_{m-1}+\pi_{m-1}(\tilde{q}_{3}),\bm{\xi})}.

The function
\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)
is given by

\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)=\delta(p_{k-2}-q_{k-2})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{\sigma_{i-1}}-q_{\sigma{i-1}}-p_{\sigma_{n}}+q_{\sigma_{n}}))\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}-1}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma_{i-1}})\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}}).

After having obtained this form the proof is analogous to the proofs previously done. That is we introduce the function
\zeta
, write our delta functions as the Fourier transform of
1
. Next we integrate in all
s
and
\tilde{s}
that the function
f
does not depend on. Moreover, we also do the usual argument by dividing into different cases depending on the size of
t
and
\tilde{t}
and do integration by parts for all for all cases, where the variables is “large”.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}(k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},l;\hbar)\big]\leq C_{d}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,6d+6}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\frac{\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}\hbar^{3}|\log(\zeta)|^{n+9}}{(k-5)!(k-n-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(8.6)

By combining (
8.5
) and (
8.6
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}\int_{0}^{t}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k+1})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k+1})\sum_{l=1}^{k-4}\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,l,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\,ds_{k+1}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

(8.7)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\hbar^{3}k_{0}^{10}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

We now turn to the second term in (
8.4
). We will for this case have to continue the Duhamel expansion. We will stop if we obtain a new genuine collision center or if we see a new recollision that is not in either
x_{k-2}
or
x_{k-3}
. If we continue to see the pattern where we jump between
x_{k-2}
and
x_{k-3}
we continue the expansion. This yields the following formal expression

\displaystyle\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\frac{(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}}{\hbar}\int_{0}^{t}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k+1})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k+1})\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,k-3,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\,ds_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\beta=1}^{\infty}\Big[\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)+\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k;\hbar)+\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k;\hbar)\Big]

where the operators
\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{j}(\beta,k;\hbar)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)=\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha^{1}\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{1}}}\int_{[0,t]^{\beta+k}}\tilde{\Theta}_{\beta}^{\mathrm{dob}}(\bm{s}_{\beta+k,k};V,\hbar)\prod_{m=1}^{k_{1}}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m)};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t),

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k;\hbar)=\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{k-4}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha^{1}\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{1}}}\int_{[0,t]^{\beta+k+1}}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k_{\beta+k+1}})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k_{\beta+k+1}})V^{s_{k+\beta+1}}_{\hbar,x_{l}}

\displaystyle\times\tilde{\Theta}_{\beta}^{\mathrm{dob}}(\bm{s}_{\beta+k,k};V,\hbar)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}^{1}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m)};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t),

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k;\hbar)=\frac{i\lambda}{\hbar}

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\sum_{\alpha^{1}\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{1}}}\int_{[0,t]^{\beta+k+1}}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k_{\beta+k+1}})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k_{\beta+k+1}})V^{s_{k+\beta+1}}_{\hbar,x_{k-1}}

\displaystyle\times\tilde{\Theta}_{\beta}^{\mathrm{dob}}(\bm{s}_{\beta+k,k};V,\hbar)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}^{1}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m)};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t).

Moreover, we have her introduced the operators
\tilde{\Theta}_{\beta}^{\mathrm{dob}}(\bm{s}_{\beta+k,k};V,\hbar)
, which is defined by

\displaystyle\tilde{\Theta}_{\beta}^{\mathrm{dob}}(\bm{s}_{\beta+m,k};V,\hbar)=\sum_{\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{\beta}}\prod_{m=1}^{\beta}\Theta_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(s_{m-1+k},{s}_{m+k},x_{\tilde{\iota}(m)};V,\hbar),

where

\tilde{\iota}(m)=\begin{cases}k-3&\text{for $m$ odd}\\
k-2&\text{for $m$ even}.\end{cases}

With this further expansion we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\frac{(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}}{\hbar}\int_{0}^{t}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k+1})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k+1})\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,k-3,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\,ds_{k+1}\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\beta=1}^{\infty}\beta^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=k_{j}}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{j}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big],

where
k_{1}=k_{3}=4
and
k_{2}=5
.
By applying Lemma
8.6
, Lemma
8.7
, and Lemma
8.8
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\frac{(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|+1}}{\hbar}\int_{0}^{t}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-s_{k+1})U_{\hbar,0}(s_{k+1})\tilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2,2}^{\mathrm{rec}}(s_{k+1},k,\bm{x},\alpha,k-3,t,\iota;\hbar)\varphi\,ds_{k+1}\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

(8.8)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta=1}^{\infty}\beta^{2}\Big\{\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{5}C^{k_{0}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+19}+\hbar\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{10}C^{k_{0}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+19}

\displaystyle+\tau_{0}^{-3}\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{5}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+20}\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\tau_{0}^{-3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{10}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+20}.

From combining the estimates in
eqs.

8.7
,
8.8
and
8.4
we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

Lemma 8.6
.

Assume we are in the setting of Lemma
8.5
. Then for any
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{5}C^{k_{0}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+19},

where
\tilde{\lambda}<1
.

Proof.

Firstly we note that

\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leq k_{0}^{2}\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\big\lVert\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(8.9)

Using the definition of the operator
\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)
and applying Lemma
2.11
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq 2\sum_{n=0}^{k-2}\binom{k-2}{n}n!\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}\lambda^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-2,n)}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|,

(8.10)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k-4}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{i}})}{\rho}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\alpha,\bm{x},\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\tilde{\alpha},\bm{\tilde{x}},\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx.

Before we proceed we will divide into the two cases
\beta\leq 6
and
\beta>6
. We will start with the first case. From the definition of the operator we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k,\bm{x};\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(|\alpha|+1)d}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+\beta}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=-3}^{\beta}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\tilde{\iota}(k)},p_{k+m}-p_{k+m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k+\beta}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

Before taking the average we will preform a series of change of variables we will start with the change of variables
p_{k-2}\mapsto p_{k-2}-p_{k-1}
and
p_{k-3}\mapsto p_{k-3}-p_{k-2}
. To motivate the next change of variables we remark that under this change of variables we get that

\displaystyle\prod_{m=-3}^{1}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\tilde{\iota}(k)},p_{k+m}-p_{k+m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\mapsto e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-2},p_{k}-p_{k-1}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-2},p_{k-1}-p_{k-3}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-3},p_{k-3}-p_{k-4}\rangle}

\displaystyle=e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-2},p_{k}-p_{k-3}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-3},p_{k-3}-p_{k-4}\rangle}.

With this observation in mind we proceed with the change of variables

p_{k+m}\mapsto p_{k+m}-p_{k+m+1}\quad\text{and}\quad p_{k-3}\mapsto p_{k-3}-p_{k+m}\quad\text{for all }\begin{cases}m\in\{0\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{1,2\}$}\\
m\in\{0,2\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{3,4\}$}\\
m\in\{0,2,4\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{5,6\}$}.\end{cases}

After this change of variables and a relabelling we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k,\bm{x};\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-2}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})e^{is_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1},p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{k-4},\bm{\eta})\prod_{m=1}^{k-2}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where

\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}&\text{if $\beta\in\{1,2\}$}\\
\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}+\tilde{p}_{2}&\text{if $\beta\in\{3,4\}$}\\
\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}+\tilde{p}_{2}+\tilde{p}_{4}&\text{if $\beta\in\{5,6\}$}.\end{cases}

For
\beta=1
we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})={}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-1}}(\tilde{p}_{-1},\tilde{p}_{-1}+\tilde{p}_{-2},\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k}}(\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{-1},\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+1}}(p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{1}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})={}

\displaystyle e^{is_{k-1}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-1}^{2}}e^{is_{k}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2})^{2}}e^{is_{k+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}

and for
\beta\geq 2
we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+\beta}}(p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})&\text{$\beta$ even}\\
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+\beta}}(p_{k-2},p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta-1}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})&\text{$\beta$ odd}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\beta-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})&\text{$\beta$ even}\\
e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\beta-1}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})&\text{$\beta$ odd}.\end{cases}

This is the expression for the function we will be working with for this case.
To estimate these averages we can argue as in the proofs of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)\big]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\overline{\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(q_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\tilde{s}})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{is_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1})^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{-2}+\tilde{q}_{-1})^{2}}e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(q_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=0}^{k_{1}-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where the function
f
is given by

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\begin{cases}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}+s_{k+\beta}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-3}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}\big)&\text{if $k-2\neq\sigma_{n}$}\\
\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}+s_{k+\beta}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-3}\tilde{s}_{i}\big)&\text{if $k-2=\sigma_{n}$}.\end{cases}

(8.11)

The function
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)=\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(q_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\xi})}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1},p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-2}}(\tilde{q}_{-2}+\tilde{q}_{-1},q_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),\bm{\xi})}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{k-4},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-3}}(q_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),q_{k-4},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}.

The function
\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)
is given by

\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)=\delta(p_{k-2}-q_{k-2})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{\sigma_{i-1}}-q_{\sigma{i-1}}-p_{\sigma_{n}}+q_{\sigma_{n}}))\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}-1}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma_{i-1}})\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}}).

We again use the function
\zeta
as in the previous proofs and write the delta functions as Fourier transforms of the constant function
1
.
Next we integrate in all
s
and
\tilde{s}
that the function
f
does not depend on. Moreover, we also do the usual argument by dividing into different cases depending on the size of
t
and
\tilde{t}
and do integration by parts for all for all cases, where the variables is “large”. To estimate the bound we now have obtained we argue as in the previous proofs. This yields the bound

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\big[\mathcal{T}(k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},l;\hbar)\big]|\leq C_{d}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,4d+4}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-5}\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+19}}{(k-3)!(k-n-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(8.12)

Combining (
8.10
) and (
8.12
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq 4\hbar\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,4d+4})^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}

(8.13)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{k-2}\binom{k-2}{n}n!\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-2,n)}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-5}}{(k-3)!(k-2-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+19}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{4d+4}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}kC^{k}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k+19},

where

\tilde{\lambda}=\frac{C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,4d+4}}{1-C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,4d+4}}.

(8.14)

That
\tilde{\lambda}
is strictly less than one follows form our assumptions. Combining (
8.9
) and (
8.13
) we obtain the desired estimate. We now turn to the case where
\beta>6
. Here we will for notational convenience consider
\beta
even and at the end say how to modify the argument for
\beta
odd. Here we will use the following expression for the function
\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x};\hbar)\varphi(x)
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k,\bm{x};\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+\beta}\rangle}

(8.15)

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}(x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}),\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2+1}p_{2m+k-2}-p_{2m+k-3}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-3},p_{\beta+k}-p_{k-4}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k+\beta}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}_{1,a_{k}}d\bm{p}_{0,k}d\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}d\bm{s}_{1,k},

where we have also chosen a specific way of writing our phase function. To estimate the averages we will distinguish between three different cases. These are
k-2\notin\sigma
,
k-3,k-2\in\sigma
and finally the case
k-2\in\sigma
and
k-3\notin\sigma
. We start with the first case
k-2\notin\sigma
. We will assume
k-3\in\sigma
precisely we will assume
\sigma_{n}=k-3
. This is only done to simplify notation and the other case is estimated completely analogous. After taking the average we star by preforming the change of variables
x_{k-2}\mapsto x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}
and
\tilde{x}_{k-2}\mapsto\tilde{x}_{k-2}-\tilde{x}_{k-3}
. Then by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
and introducing the function
\zeta
as in the previous proofs and write the delta functions as Fourier transforms of the constant function
1
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}e^{2\hbar^{-1}t\zeta}}{(2\pi)^{2}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t\tilde{\nu}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t\nu}

(8.16)

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle x,p_{n}-\tilde{p}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{p}_{2m-2}-\tilde{p}_{2m-3}\rangle}e^{i\langle\tilde{x},p_{n}-\tilde{q}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{q}_{2m-2}-\tilde{q}_{2m-3}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{k_{1}+\beta}(\frac{1}{2}p_{n}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k_{1}+\beta}(\frac{1}{2}p_{n}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\Big\{\prod_{m=-3}^{\beta-1}e^{is_{m+k}(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{m}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m+k}(\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\Big\}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}e^{is_{m}(\frac{1}{2}p_{i}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}(\frac{1}{2}p_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}dxd\tilde{x}d\bm{p}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

where the function
f
is given by

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}+s_{k+\beta}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-4}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}\big)

(8.17)

and the function
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)={}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\beta+k_{1}}}(p_{n},\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\beta+k_{1}}}(p_{n},\tilde{q}_{\beta-1},\bm{\xi})}\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m+k_{1}}}(\tilde{p}_{m},\tilde{p}_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m+k_{1}}}(\tilde{q}_{m},\tilde{q}_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}

(8.18)

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k_{1}-3}}(\tilde{p}_{-3},p_{n-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k_{1}-3}}(\tilde{q}_{-3},p_{n-1},\bm{\eta})}\prod_{m=1}^{\sigma_{1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{0},p_{0},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(p_{0},p_{0},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n_{1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\sigma_{i}}}(p_{i},p_{i-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\sigma_{i}}}(p_{i},p_{i-1},\bm{\xi})}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}+1}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{i},p_{i},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(p_{i},p_{i},\bm{\xi})}.

We now evaluate the integrals in
s_{0}
,
\tilde{s}_{0}
,
\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{i}}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
and
s_{m}
,
\tilde{s}_{m}
for
m\in\{k-3,\dots,k+\beta-1\}
. From here we do the usual argument by dividing into different cases depending on the size of
t
and
\tilde{t}
and do integration by parts for all for all cases, where the varables is “large”. For the variables
\tilde{p}_{m}
,
\tilde{q}_{m}
with
m\in\{-2,\dots,\beta-1\}
we use Lemma
2.1
and integration by parts. How we use Lemma
2.1
and integration by parts is captured in the following calculation

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2\beta+4}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)\frac{e^{i\langle x,\tilde{p}_{-3}\rangle}}{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-3}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta}\frac{e^{-i\langle\tilde{x},\tilde{q}_{-3}\rangle}}{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{-3}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta}\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\frac{e^{-i\langle x,(-1)^{m}\tilde{p}_{m}\rangle}}{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{m}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta}\frac{e^{i\langle\tilde{x},(-1)^{m}\tilde{q}_{m}\rangle}}{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{m}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta}\,d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}\Big|

(8.19)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2\beta+4}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4\beta+8}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\frac{e^{-i\langle z_{m},\tilde{p}_{m}\rangle}e^{i|z_{m}|\sqrt{2(-\nu-i\zeta)}e^{-i\langle\tilde{z}_{m},\tilde{q}_{m}\rangle}e^{i|\tilde{z}_{m}|\sqrt{2(\tilde{\nu}+i\zeta)}}}}{|\tilde{z}_{m}-(-1)^{m}\tilde{x}||z_{m}-(-1)^{m}x|}

\displaystyle\times\frac{e^{i\langle x,\tilde{p}_{-3}\rangle}}{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-3}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta}\frac{e^{-i\langle\tilde{x},\tilde{q}_{-3}\rangle}}{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{-3}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta}\,d\bm{z}d\bm{\tilde{z}}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\sum_{|\beta_{-2}|,\dots,|\beta_{k+\beta-1}|\leq d+1}\sum_{|\epsilon_{-2}|,\dots,|\epsilon_{k+\beta-1}|\leq d+1}\frac{\prod_{i=-2}^{k+\beta-1}C(\beta_{i})C(\epsilon_{i})}{(2\pi)^{2\beta+4}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{4\beta+8}}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-3}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{-3}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{z}_{m}\rangle^{d+1}|\tilde{z}_{m}-(-1)^{m}\tilde{x}|\langle z_{m}\rangle^{d+1}|z_{m}-(-1)^{m}x|}\big|\partial_{\bm{\tilde{p}}}^{\bm{\beta}}\partial_{\bm{\tilde{q}}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)\big|\,d\bm{z}d\bm{\tilde{z}}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}.

Combining these arguments we get that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-6}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}_{1,k-3}d\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k-n-3}\sup_{\bm{\epsilon},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\delta},\bm{\varepsilon}}\int|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}

(8.20)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle p_{0}\rangle^{2(d+1)}}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\langle\nu\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-3}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{-3}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\frac{\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}

\displaystyle\times\Big|\partial_{\bm{\eta}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bm{\xi}}^{\bm{\gamma}}\partial_{\bm{\tilde{p}}}^{\bm{\delta}}\partial_{\bm{\tilde{q}}}^{\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)\Big|\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{z}_{m}\rangle^{d+1}|\tilde{z}_{m}-(-1)^{m}\tilde{x}|\langle z_{m}\rangle^{d+1}|z_{m}-(-1)^{m}x|}

\displaystyle\times\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}m=1\\
m\neq n_{1}\end{subarray}}^{n}\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{3(d+1)}\,dxd\tilde{x}d\bm{z}d\bm{\tilde{z}}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{p}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi},

where
C
is a constant only depending on the dimension and we have used the notation

\sup_{\bm{\epsilon},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\delta},\bm{\varepsilon}}\coloneqq\sup_{|\epsilon_{1}|,\dots,|\epsilon_{a_{k+\beta}}|\leq d+1}\sup_{|\gamma_{1}|,\dots,|\gamma_{a_{k+\beta}}|\leq d+1}\sup_{|\delta_{1}|,\dots,|\delta_{\beta+2}|\leq d+1}\sup_{|\varepsilon_{1}|,\dots,|\varepsilon_{\beta+2}|\leq d+1}.

(8.21)

Moreover, we have used the inequality

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}_{0}\rangle^{2d+2}\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{3(d+1)}}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}_{0}\rangle^{2d+2}\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{3(d+1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}_{0}\rangle^{2d+2}\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{3(d+1)}}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{d+1}}.

To estimate this we note that

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{p}}\int\frac{\Big|\partial_{\bm{\eta}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bm{\xi}}^{\bm{\gamma}}\partial_{\bm{\tilde{p}}}^{\bm{\delta}}\partial_{\bm{\tilde{q}}}^{\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)\Big|}{(\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle)^{-d-1}}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{3(d+1)}\,d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}dp_{n}\leq(C\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3})^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}.

(8.22)

Moreover, we have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-6}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}_{1,k-3}d\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k-n-3}=\frac{t^{2k-n-6}}{\hbar^{2k-n-6}(k-3)!(k-3-n)!}.

(8.23)

By applying Lemma
2.4
and Lemma
2.5
we get that

\displaystyle\int|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\frac{\langle p_{0}\rangle^{2(d+1)}}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\langle\nu\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-3}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{-3}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\frac{\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}

(8.24)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{z}_{m}\rangle^{d+1}|\tilde{z}_{m}-(-1)^{m}\tilde{x}|\langle z_{m}\rangle^{d+1}|z_{m}-(-1)^{m}x|}\,dxd\tilde{x}d\bm{z}d\bm{\tilde{z}}d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}_{-3}d\tilde{q}_{-3}

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi\hbar)^{d}C|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\int\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{\frac{\beta+2}{2}}\langle\tilde{x}\rangle^{\frac{\beta+2}{2}}}\,dxd\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\leq(2\pi\hbar)^{d}C|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where we have used that
\beta>6
. Combining the estimates in
eqs.

8.20
,
8.22
,
8.23
and
8.24
we obtain that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{2}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}}{(k-3)!(k-3-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}.

(8.25)

Note that for this case we have assumed that
k-2\notin\sigma
, hence
n\leq k-3
implying that we are not taking the factorial of a negative number.

We now turn to the case where
k-2\in\sigma
and
k-3\notin\sigma
. For this case we preform the change of variables
x_{k-3}\mapsto x_{k-3}-x_{k-2}
and
\tilde{x}_{k-3}\mapsto\tilde{x}_{k-3}-\tilde{x}_{k-2}
after taking the average. Other than that one obtains with an analogous argument to the one above the estimate

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{2}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}}{(k-3)!(k-3-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}.

(8.26)

Moreover, we again in this case have that
n\leq k-3
.
We now turn to the case where
k-2,k-3\in\sigma
. For this case we also preform the change of variables
x_{k-2}\mapsto x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}
and
\tilde{x}_{k-2}\mapsto\tilde{x}_{k-2}-\tilde{x}_{k-3}
after taking the average. However this time when we argue as in the proofs of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})e^{-i\langle x,\tilde{q}_{\beta-1}-\tilde{p}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{p}_{2m-2}-\tilde{p}_{2m-3}-\tilde{q}_{2m-2}+\tilde{q}_{2m-3}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-3}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{-3}^{2}}e^{i(s_{k+\beta}-\tilde{s}_{k+\beta})\frac{1}{2}p_{n-1}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}e^{is_{m+k}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{m}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m+k}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{m}^{2}}\prod_{i=0}^{n-2}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}e^{i(s_{m}-\tilde{s}_{m})\frac{1}{2}p_{i}^{2}}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}dxd\tilde{x}d\bm{p}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where the function
f
still is given by (
8.11
)
and the function
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}

\displaystyle(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)=\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\beta+k}}(p_{n-1},\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\beta+k}}(p_{n-1},\tilde{q}_{\beta-1},\bm{\xi})}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k_{1}-3}}(\tilde{p}_{-3},p_{n_{1}-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k_{1}-3}}(\tilde{q}_{-3},p_{n_{1}-1},\bm{\eta})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m+k_{1}}}(\tilde{p}_{m},\tilde{p}_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m+k_{1}}}(\tilde{q}_{m},\tilde{q}_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\prod_{m=1}^{\sigma_{1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{0},p_{0},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(p_{0},p_{0},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n-2}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\sigma_{i}}}(p_{i},p_{i-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\sigma_{i}}}(p_{i},p_{i-1},\bm{\xi})}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}+1}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{i},p_{i},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(p_{i},p_{i},\bm{\xi})}.

We observe here that effectively we now only have
n-1
collisions and not
n
. This illustrates the intuition that in this case we treat the collisions in the position
x_{k-2}
and
x_{k-3}
as a single collision. By arguing as above we obtain that if
2\leq n\leq k-3
the estimate

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{2}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}}{(k-3)!(k-3-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}.

(8.27)

Note that this case is empty if
k=4
. Here we can only have that
n=2
if
k-2,k-3\in\sigma
. This is covered in the next case. In the case
n=k-2
we get the estimate

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\rho(\rho t)^{k-3}}{(k-3)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}.

(8.28)
