That this case is different is the reason we have to do the full double Born series expansion. Combining the estimates in
eqs.

8.10
,
8.25
,
8.26
,
8.27
and
8.28
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq 2\hbar\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3})^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}

(8.29)

\displaystyle\times\Big[k\rho(\rho t)^{k-3}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k+1}+2\sum_{n=0}^{k-3}\binom{k-2}{n}n!\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-2,n)}\frac{\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}}{(k-3)!(k-3-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}t^{k-3}kC^{k}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k+1},

where
\tilde{\lambda}
is given by (
8.14
). Combining (
8.10
) and (
8.29
) we obtain a bound better than stated in the Lemma. We have chosen to state the Lemma with a bound that covers both small and large values of
\beta
. For
\beta
odd, the difference is that instead of (
8.15
) we now have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{1}(\beta,k,\bm{x};\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+\beta}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}(x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}),\sum_{m=0}^{(\beta+1)/2}p_{2m+k-2}-p_{2m+k-3}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-3},p_{\beta+k}-p_{k-4}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k+\beta}\Big\{e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

Form here the proofs are analogous and we obtain the same bound as in (
8.29
).
∎

Lemma 8.7
.

Assume we are in the setting of Lemma
8.5
. Then for any
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\hbar\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{10}C^{k_{0}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+19},

where
\tilde{\lambda}<1
.

Proof.

The proof is a combination of the proof of Lemma
8.6
and the first part of the proof of Lemma
8.5
. Firstly we note that

\big\lVert\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leq\frac{k_{0}^{4}t\lambda^{2}}{\hbar^{2}}\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{l=1}^{k-4}\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,l,s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,ds_{k+\beta+1},

(8.30)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,l,s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-2}}\sum_{\alpha^{1}\in\mathbb{N}^{k_{1}}}\int_{[0,t]^{\beta+k}}\bm{1}_{[s_{k+\beta+1},t]}(s_{k+\beta})V^{s_{k+\beta+1}}_{\hbar,x_{l}}\tilde{\Theta}_{\beta}^{\mathrm{dob}}(\bm{s}_{\beta+k,k};V,\hbar)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}^{1}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m)};V,\hbar)U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}.

Applying Lemma
2.11
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}\big\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,l,s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,ds_{k+\beta+1}\Big]

(8.31)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{n=0}^{k-2}\binom{k-2}{n}n!\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}\lambda^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-2,n)}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},l;\hbar)]\big|,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},l;\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k-4}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{i}})}{\rho}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\alpha,\bm{x},\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\tilde{\alpha},\bm{\tilde{x}},\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dxds_{k+\beta+1}.

Before we proceed we will divide into the two cases
\beta\leq 6
and
\beta>6
. We will start with the first case. From the definition of the operator we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\alpha,\bm{x},\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+\beta}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{l},p_{k+\beta+1}-p_{k+\beta}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k+\beta+1}^{2}}\hat{V}(p_{k+\beta+1}-p_{k+\beta})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=-3}^{\beta}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\tilde{\iota}(k)},p_{k+m}-p_{k+m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k+\beta}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.6
we preform the change of variables

p_{k+m}\mapsto p_{k+m}-p_{k+m+1}\quad\text{and}\quad p_{k-3}\mapsto p_{k-3}-p_{k+m}\quad\text{for all }\begin{cases}m\in\{-2,0\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{1,2\}$}\\
m\in\{-2,0,2\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{3,4\}$}\\
m\in\{-2,0,2,4\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{5,6\}$}.\end{cases}

After this change of variables and a relabelling we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\alpha,\bm{x},\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-1}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{l},p_{k-1}-p_{k-2}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-1}^{2}}\hat{V}(p_{k-1}-p_{k-2})\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})e^{is_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1},p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{k-4},\bm{\eta})\prod_{m=1}^{k-2}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where

\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}&\text{if $\beta\in\{1,2\}$}\\
\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}+\tilde{p}_{2}&\text{if $\beta\in\{3,4\}$}\\
\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}+\tilde{p}_{2}+\tilde{p}_{4}&\text{if $\beta\in\{5,6\}$}.\end{cases}

For
\beta=1
we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})={}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-1}}(\tilde{p}_{-1},\tilde{p}_{-1}+\tilde{p}_{-2},\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k}}(\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{-1},\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+1}}(p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{1}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})={}

\displaystyle e^{is_{k-1}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-1}^{2}}e^{is_{k}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2})^{2}}e^{is_{k+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}

and for
\beta\geq 2
we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+\beta}}(p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})&\text{$\beta$ even}\\
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+\beta}}(p_{k-2},p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta-1}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})&\text{$\beta$ odd}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\beta-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})&\text{$\beta$ even}\\
e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\beta-1}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})&\text{$\beta$ odd}.\end{cases}

Next we preform the change of variables
p_{k-2}\mapsto p_{k-2}-p_{k-1}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{k-2}
for all
m\in\{l,\dots,k-3\}
with this change of variable and a relabelling we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\alpha,\bm{x},\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-2}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}\hat{V}(-\tilde{p}_{\beta})\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}+p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}+p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1},p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{k-4}+\tilde{p}_{\beta},\bm{\eta})e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta+1}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k-2}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}))^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}),p_{m-1}+\tilde{\pi}_{m-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}),\bm{\eta})\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where we have used the notation

\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{p}_{\beta})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}_{\beta}&\text{if $m\in\{l,\dots,k-4\}$}\\
0&\text{if $m\notin\{l,\dots,k-4\}$}.\end{cases}

Using this form we can argue as in the proofs of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)\big]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}\hbar(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\overline{\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k+\beta}^{+}-\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k+\beta+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(q_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\tilde{s}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{is_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1})^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{-2}+\tilde{q}_{-1})^{2}}e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(q_{k-3}+\tilde{q}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=0}^{k-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}(q_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{q}_{\beta}))^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we have preformed the change of variables
\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}
. The function
f
is given by

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\begin{cases}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-3}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\big)&\text{if $\sigma_{n}\leq l$}\\
\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\neq l}^{k-4}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-3}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\big)&\text{if $\sigma_{n}>l$}.\end{cases}

The function
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}+p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1},p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{k-4},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\tilde{q}_{\beta}+q_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\xi})}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-2}}(\tilde{q}_{-2}+\tilde{q}_{-1},q_{k-3}+\tilde{q}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),\bm{\xi})}\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-3}}(q_{k-3}+\tilde{q}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),q_{k-4},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}),p_{m-1}+\tilde{\pi}_{m-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{q}_{\beta}),q_{m-1}+\tilde{\pi}_{m-1}(\tilde{q}_{\beta}),\bm{\xi})}.

The function
\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)
is given by

\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)=\delta(p_{k-2}-q_{k-2})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{\sigma_{i-1}}-q_{\sigma{i-1}}-p_{\sigma_{n}}+q_{\sigma_{n}}))\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}-1}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma_{i-1}})\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}}).

From applying the “usually” arguments we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{3}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-6}}{(k-5)!(k-2-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+19}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,6d+6}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2},

(8.32)

where we have used that
\beta\leq 6
. We remark that this is a “combined” bound, that will be true for all cases. Some of the different configurations will have better bounds, but we have choosen to use a single bound as it will suffices. Combining (
8.30
), (
8.31
), (
8.32
) and arguing as above we obtain the estimate

\mathbb{E}\left[\big\lVert\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\right]\leq\hbar\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{10}C^{k_{0}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+19},

(8.33)

where
\tilde{\lambda}
is given by (
8.14
) and satisfies that
\tilde{\lambda}<1
. We now turn to the case
\beta>6
again for notational convenience we will assume
\beta
to be even and in the end of the proof mention what we will need to change for
\beta
odd. From the definition of the operator we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,\bm{x},s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar,l)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+\beta+1}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{l},p_{k+\beta+1}-p_{k+\beta}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k+\beta+1}^{2}}\hat{V}(p_{k+\beta+1}-p_{k+\beta})e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-3},p_{\beta+k_{1}}-p_{k-4}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}(x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}),\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2+1}p_{2m+k-2}-p_{2m+k-3}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k+\beta}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}.

We start by preforming the change of variables
p_{k+\beta}\mapsto p_{k+\beta}-p_{k+\beta+1}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{k+\beta}
for all
m\in\{l,\dots,k+\beta-1\}
. After a relabelling, where we relabel
p_{k+\beta}
as
\tilde{\eta}
and
p_{k+\beta+1}
as
p_{k+\beta}
we get that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{2}(\beta,k,\bm{x},s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar,l)\varphi(x)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}\frac{(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+\beta}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k+\beta}^{2}}\hat{V}(-\tilde{\eta})e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}(x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}),\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2+1}p_{2m+k-2}-p_{2m+k-3}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k+\beta}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\eta}))^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\eta}),p_{m-1}+\pi_{m-1}(\tilde{\eta}),\bm{\eta})e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-3},p_{\beta+k}-p_{k-4}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}\Big\{e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\Big\}e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where the function
\pi_{m}(\tilde{\eta})
is given in (
8.32
).
As in the proof of Lemma
8.6
we again have to distinguish the three cases
k-2\notin\sigma
,
k-3,k-2\in\sigma
and finally the case
k-2\in\sigma
and
k-3\notin\sigma
. We start with the first case
k-2\notin\sigma
and again we will assume
\sigma_{n}=k-3
, this is only done to simplify notation and the other case is estimated completely analogous. After taking the average we start by preforming the change of variables
x_{k-2}\mapsto x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}
and
\tilde{x}_{k-2}\mapsto\tilde{x}_{k-2}-\tilde{x}_{k-3}
. Then by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}\hbar(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\xi},\sigma)

(8.34)

\displaystyle\times\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k+\beta+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k+\beta}^{+}-\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle x,p_{n}-\tilde{p}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{p}_{2m-2}-\tilde{p}_{2m-3}\rangle}e^{i\langle\tilde{x},p_{n}-\tilde{q}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{q}_{2m-2}-\tilde{q}_{2m-3}\rangle}e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-3}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{-3}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+\tilde{\eta})^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+\tilde{\xi})^{2}}\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}e^{is_{m+k}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{m}+\tilde{\eta})^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m+k}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{m}+\tilde{\xi})^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\eta}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\xi}))^{2}}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}dxd\tilde{x}d\tilde{\eta}d\tilde{\xi}d\bm{p}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where the function
f
is given by

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-4}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\big)

and the function
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\xi},\sigma)=\overline{\hat{V}(\tilde{\eta})}\hat{V}(\tilde{\xi})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\beta+k}}(p_{n}+\tilde{\eta},\tilde{p}_{\beta-1}+\tilde{\eta},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\beta+k}}(p_{n}+\tilde{\xi},\tilde{q}_{\beta-1}+\tilde{\xi},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m+k}}(\tilde{p}_{m}+\tilde{\eta},\tilde{p}_{m-1}+\tilde{\eta},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m+k}}(\tilde{q}_{m}+\tilde{\xi},\tilde{q}_{m-1}+\tilde{\xi},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(\tilde{p}_{-3}+\tilde{\eta},p_{n-1}+\tilde{\eta},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-3}}(\tilde{q}_{-3}+\tilde{\xi},p_{n-1}+\tilde{\xi},\bm{\eta})}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\sigma_{i}}}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}(\tilde{\eta}),p_{i-1}\pi_{\sigma_{i}-1}(\tilde{\eta}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\sigma_{i}}}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}(\tilde{\xi}),p_{i-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}-1}(\tilde{\xi}),\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}+1}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\eta}),p_{i}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\eta}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\xi}),p_{i}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\xi}),\bm{\xi})}\prod_{m=1}^{\sigma_{1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{0},p_{0},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(p_{0},p_{0},\bm{\xi})}.

We again use the function
\zeta
as in the previous proofs and write the delta functions as Fourier transforms of the constant function
1
. We the obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}\hbar(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}e^{2\hbar^{-1}t\zeta}}{(2\pi)^{2}(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\langle x,p_{n}-\tilde{p}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{p}_{2m-2}-\tilde{p}_{2m-3}\rangle}e^{i\langle\tilde{x},p_{n}-\tilde{q}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{q}_{2m-2}-\tilde{q}_{2m-3}\rangle}e^{is_{k-3}(\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-3}+\tilde{\eta})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-3}(\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{-3}+\tilde{\xi})^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}(\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\Big\{e^{is_{m+k}(\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{m}+\tilde{\eta})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m+k}(\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{m}+\tilde{\xi})^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\Big\}e^{is_{k+\beta}(\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+\tilde{\eta})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k_{1}+\beta}(\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+\tilde{\xi})^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\xi},\sigma)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}e^{is_{m}(\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\eta}))^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}(\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}(\tilde{\xi}))^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}dxd\tilde{x}d\tilde{\eta}d\tilde{\xi}d\bm{p}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

We now evaluate the integrals in
s_{0}
,
s_{k+\beta}
,
\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}
,
\tilde{s}_{0}
,
\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{i}}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
and
s_{m}
,
\tilde{s}_{m}
for

. From here we do the usual argument by dividing into different cases depending on the size of

and
\tilde{t}
and do integration by parts for all for all cases, where the varables is “large”. For the variables

,
\tilde{q}_{m}
with

we use Lemma
2.1
and integration by parts as in the proof of Lemma

.
Combining these arguments we get that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar,l)]\big|\leq\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar(\rho\hbar)^{2k-4-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2k-n-7}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}\sup_{\bm{\epsilon},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\delta},\bm{\varepsilon}}\int|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}

(8.35)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle\tilde{\eta}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(p_{n_{1}}+\tilde{\xi})^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\xi}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(p_{n_{1}}+\tilde{\eta})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle p_{0}\rangle^{2(d+1)}}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\langle\nu\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-3}+\tilde{\eta})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{-3}+\tilde{\xi})^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}m=1\\
m\neq n_{1}\end{subarray}}^{n}\frac{\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{\sigma_{m}}(\tilde{\xi}))^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}

\displaystyle\times\Big|\partial_{\bm{\eta}}^{\bm{\epsilon}}\partial_{\bm{\xi}}^{\bm{\gamma}}\partial_{\bm{\tilde{p}}}^{\bm{\delta}}\partial_{\bm{\tilde{q}}}^{\bm{\varepsilon}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\xi},\sigma)\Big|\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{z}_{m}\rangle^{d+1}|\tilde{z}_{m}-(-1)^{m}\tilde{x}|\langle z_{m}\rangle^{d+1}|z_{m}-(-1)^{m}x|}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{\eta}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{\xi}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}_{0}\rangle^{2d+2}\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}-p_{n-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{3(d+1)}}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{\eta}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{\xi}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}\rangle^{d+1}\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{d+1}}.

\displaystyle\leq C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}.

(8.38)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{p}_{-3}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-3}+\tilde{\eta})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle\tilde{q}_{-3}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{-3}+\tilde{\xi})^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\prod_{m=1}^{n-1}\frac{\langle p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{\sigma_{m}}(\tilde{\xi}))^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle(2\pi\hbar)^{d}C|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+5}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\int\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{\frac{\beta+2}{2}}\langle\tilde{x}\rangle^{\frac{\beta+2}{2}}}\,dxd\tilde{x}

\displaystyle\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar C^{k_{0}}k_{0}^{10}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3},

) similar to the one stated in the proof of Lemma
8.6
but otherwise the proofs are analogous.
This concludes the proof as the the bound obtained for
\beta>6
is “better” than the bound stated in the Lemma.
∎

Lemma 8.8
.

Assume we are in the setting of Lemma
8.5
. Then for any
\beta\in\mathbb{N}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\tau_{0}^{-3}\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{5}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+20}.

where
\tilde{\lambda}<1
.

Proof.

The proof is very similar to the proof of Lemma
8.6
. Firstly we note that

\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leq\frac{k_{0}^{2}t\lambda^{2}}{\tau_{0}\hbar^{2}}\sum_{k=5}^{k_{0}}\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\big\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,ds_{k+\beta+1},

(8.47)

where
t_{0}=\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\int_{[0,t_{0}]^{\beta+k}}\bm{1}_{[s_{k+\beta+1},t]}(s_{k+\beta})V^{s_{k+\beta+1}}_{\hbar,x_{k-1}}\tilde{\Theta}_{\beta}^{\mathrm{dob}}(\bm{s}_{\beta+k,k};V,\hbar)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m)};V,\hbar)U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}.

Applying Lemma
2.11
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\big\lVert\tilde{\mathcal{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\,ds_{k+\beta+1}\Big]

(8.48)

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{n}n!\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}\lambda^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-2,n)}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|,

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k-4}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{i}})}{\rho}

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{t_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\alpha,\bm{x},\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\tilde{\alpha},\bm{\tilde{x}},\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dxds_{k+\beta+1}.

Before we proceed we will divide into the two cases
\beta\leq 6
and
\beta>6
. We will start with the first case. From the definition of the operator we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\alpha,\bm{x},\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t_{0}]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(|\alpha|+1)d}}e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+\beta}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{l},p_{k+\beta+1}-p_{k+\beta}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k+\beta+1}^{2}}\hat{V}(p_{k+\beta+1}-p_{k+\beta})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=-3}^{\beta}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\tilde{\iota}(k)},p_{k+m}-p_{k+m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k+\beta}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t_{0}-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.6
we preform the change of variables

p_{k+m}\mapsto p_{k+m}-p_{k+m+1}\quad\text{and}\quad p_{k-3}\mapsto p_{k-3}-p_{k+m}\quad\text{for all }\begin{cases}m\in\{-2,0\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{1,2\}$}\\
m\in\{-2,0,2\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{3,4\}$}\\
m\in\{-2,0,2,4\}&\text{if $\beta\in\{5,6\}$}.\end{cases}

After this change of variables and a relabelling we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,s_{k+\beta+1};\alpha,\bm{x},\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t_{0}]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-1}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-1},p_{k-1}-p_{k-2}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-1}^{2}}\hat{V}(p_{k-1}-p_{k-2})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})e^{is_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1})^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1},p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(p_{k-3}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{k-4},\bm{\eta})\prod_{m=1}^{k-2}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\times e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t_{0}-\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where

\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}&\text{if $\beta\in\{1,2\}$}\\
\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}+\tilde{p}_{2}&\text{if $\beta\in\{3,4\}$}\\
\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{0}+\tilde{p}_{2}+\tilde{p}_{4}&\text{if $\beta\in\{5,6\}$}.\end{cases}

For
\beta=1
we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{1}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})={}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-1}}(\tilde{p}_{-1},\tilde{p}_{-1}+\tilde{p}_{-2},\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k}}(\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{-1},\bm{\eta})\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+1}}(p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{1}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})={}

\displaystyle e^{is_{k-1}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{-1}^{2}}e^{is_{k}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{0}+p_{k-2})^{2}}e^{is_{k+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}

and for
\beta\geq 2
we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+\beta}}(p_{k-2},\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})&\text{$\beta$ even}\\
\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k+\beta}}(p_{k-2},p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta-1}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})&\text{$\beta$ odd}\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\beta-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})&\text{$\beta$ even}\\
e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-2}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{\beta-1}(p_{k-2}+\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})&\text{$\beta$ odd}.\end{cases}

As in the previous proofs we can now argue as in the proofs of of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\big[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)\big]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}\hbar(\rho\hbar)^{2k-2-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\overline{\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\delta(\tfrac{t_{0}}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k+\beta}^{+}-\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t_{0}}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k+\beta+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{s})\mathcal{P}_{\beta}(q_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\tilde{s}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{is_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1})^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-2}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{q}_{-2}+\tilde{q}_{-1})^{2}}e^{is_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k-3}\frac{1}{2}(q_{k-3}+\tilde{q}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}))^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=0}^{k_{1}-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}(q_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{q}_{\beta}))^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we have preformed the change of variables
\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}
. The function
f
is given by

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\begin{cases}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}+s_{k+\beta}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-3}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\big)&\text{if $k-2\notin\sigma$}\\
\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}+s_{k+\beta}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-3}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\big)&\text{if $k-2\in\sigma$}.\end{cases}

The function
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\sigma)=\hat{V}(p_{k-1}-p_{k-2})\overline{\hat{V}(q_{k-1}-q_{k-2})}\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}+p_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\eta})\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\beta}(\tilde{q}_{\beta}+q_{k-2},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-2}}(\tilde{p}_{-2}+\tilde{p}_{-1},p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-2}}(\tilde{q}_{-2}+\tilde{q}_{-1},q_{k-3}+\tilde{q}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(p_{k-3}+\tilde{p}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{p}}),p_{k-4},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-3}}(q_{k-3}+\tilde{q}_{\beta}+\pi_{\beta}(\bm{\tilde{q}}),q_{k-4},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}),p_{m-1}+\tilde{\pi}_{m-1}(\tilde{p}_{\beta}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m}+\tilde{\pi}_{m}(\tilde{q}_{\beta}),q_{m-1}+\tilde{\pi}_{m-1}(\tilde{q}_{\beta}),\bm{\xi})}.

The function
\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)
is given by

\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)=\delta(p_{k-1}-q_{k-1})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{\sigma_{i-1}}-q_{\sigma_{i-1}}-p_{\sigma_{n}}+q_{\sigma_{n}}))\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}-1}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma_{i-1}})\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma^{1}_{i-1}}).

To estimate these averages we can use the same techniques as in the previous proofs. However we will split into the two different cases
\sigma_{n}<k-1
and
\sigma_{n}=k-1
. We start with the case where
\sigma_{n}=k-1
. Here we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{2}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho(\rho t_{0})^{2k-n-3}}{(k-3)!(k-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+20}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,6d+6}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2},

where we again have used that
\beta\leq 6
. Recalling that
t_{0}=\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}
and observing that for all possible values of
k
and
n
we have that
2k-n-3\geq 2
we get that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\frac{\hbar^{2}}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-3)!(k-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+20}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,6d+6}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}.

(8.49)

Note that for any possible value of
k
and
n
we always have the integrals in
s_{k+\beta}
and
\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}
.
2
2

2

This obersevation is crucial when we want to generalise this to the case where we consider time division. Since we can here have cases where we only have that it is the variables
s_{k+\beta}
,
\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}
and
\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}
that lives in a small time interval.

For the case where
\sigma_{n}<k-1
we argue as in the previous proofs and obtain the bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{3}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho^{2}(\rho t_{0})^{2k-n-4}}{(k-3)!(k-1-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+20}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,6d+6}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}.

(8.50)

From combining (
8.49
) and (
8.50
) we obtain for any configuration
\sigma
in this case the bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\frac{\hbar^{2}}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-4}}{(k-3)!(k-1-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+20}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,6d+6}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}.

(8.51)

Then from combining (
8.47
), (
8.48
) and (
8.51
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\tau_{0}^{-3}\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{5}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+20}.

This concludes the proof for the case of
\beta\leq 6
. We now turn to the case
\beta>6
again for notational convenience we will assume
\beta
to be even and in the end of the proof mention what we will need to change for
\beta
odd. From the definition of the operator we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{\tilde{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,\bm{x},s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k+\beta}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t_{0}]}(\bm{s}_{1,k+\beta}^{+}+\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})

(8.52)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(|\alpha|+1)d}}e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k+\beta+1}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-1},p_{k+\beta+1}-p_{k+\beta}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}s_{k+\beta+1}\frac{1}{2}p_{k+\beta+1}^{2}}\hat{V}(p_{k+\beta+1}-p_{k+\beta})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}(x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}),\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2+1}p_{2m+k-2}-p_{2m+k-3}\rangle}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{k-3},p_{\beta+k}-p_{k-4}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-4}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k+\beta}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}.

As in the proof of Lemma
8.6
we again have to distinguish the three cases
k-2\notin\sigma
,
k-3,k-2\in\sigma
and finally the case
k-2\in\sigma
and
k-3\notin\sigma
. We start with the first case
k-2\notin\sigma
. After taking the average we star by preforming the change of variables
x_{k-2}\mapsto x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}
and
\tilde{x}_{k-2}\mapsto\tilde{x}_{k-2}-\tilde{x}_{k-3}
. Then by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}\hbar(\rho\hbar)^{2k-2-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\xi},\sigma)

(8.53)

\displaystyle\times\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k+\beta+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k+\beta}^{+}-\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle x,p_{k-3}-\tilde{p}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{p}_{2m-2}-\tilde{p}_{2m-3}\rangle}e^{i\langle\tilde{x},q_{k-3}-\tilde{q}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{q}_{2m-2}-\tilde{q}_{2m-3}\rangle}e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-3}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}q_{k-3}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=-3}^{\beta-1}e^{is_{m+k}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{m}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m+k}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{m}^{2}}\prod_{m=0}^{k-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}dxd\tilde{x}d\tilde{\eta}d\tilde{\xi}d\bm{p}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where the function
f
is given by

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\begin{cases}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}+s_{k+\beta}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-4}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\big)&\text{if $k-3\notin\sigma$}\\
\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-4}s_{i}+s_{k+\beta}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{k-4}\tilde{s}_{i}+\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}\big)&\text{if $k-3\in\sigma$},\end{cases}

(8.54)

and the function
\mathcal{G}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\xi},\sigma)=\hat{V}(p_{k-2}-p_{k-3})\overline{\hat{V}(q_{k-2}-q_{k-3})}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\beta+k}}(p_{k-3},\tilde{p}_{\beta-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\beta+k}}(q_{k-3},\tilde{q}_{\beta-1},\bm{\xi})}

(8.55)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=-2}^{\beta-1}\Big\{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m+k}}(\tilde{p}_{m},\tilde{p}_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m+k}}(\tilde{q}_{m},\tilde{q}_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}\Big\}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{k-3}}(\tilde{p}_{-3},p_{k-4},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{k-3}}(\tilde{q}_{-3},q_{k-4},\bm{\eta})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=1}^{k-3}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m}}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\bm{\xi})}.

and the function
\Lambda_{n}
will, due to our relabelling, depend on
\sigma
in the following way, where we stress that we have assumed
k-2\notin\sigma
. If we have that
\sigma_{n}\leq k-3
we get that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)={}

\displaystyle\delta(p_{k-2}-q_{k-2})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{\sigma_{i-1}}-q_{\sigma_{i-1}}-p_{\sigma_{n}}+q_{\sigma_{n}})\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}-1}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma_{i-1}})\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma_{i-1}})

(8.56)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma_{n}+1}^{k-2}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma_{n}})\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma_{n}}).

In the case where
\sigma_{n}=k-1
we get that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)={}

\displaystyle\delta(p_{k-2}-q_{k-2})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{\sigma_{i-1}}-q_{\sigma_{i-1}})\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma_{i}-1}\delta(p_{m}-p_{\sigma_{i-1}})\delta(q_{m}-q_{\sigma_{i-1}}).

(8.57)

For any of the configuration
\sigma
we can argue as in the proof of Lemma
8.6
and obtain the bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{3}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho^{3}(\rho t)^{2k-n-5}}{(k-3)!(k-2-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2},

(8.58)

where we have used that
t_{0}<t
. For the case where we have
k-2\in\sigma
and
k-3\notin\sigma
we switch the roles of
x_{k-2}
and
x_{k-3}
in the above arguments as done in the proofs of Lemma
8.6
and obtain for any
\sigma
the bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{3}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho^{3}(\rho t)^{2k-n-5}}{(k-3)!(k-2-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}.

(8.59)

We now turn to the case where we have
k-2,k-3\in\sigma
. After taking the average we star by preforming the change of variables
x_{k-2}\mapsto x_{k-2}-x_{k-3}
and
\tilde{x}_{k-2}\mapsto\tilde{x}_{k-2}-\tilde{x}_{k-3}
. Then by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
4.1
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]=\frac{(2\pi)^{(2k-2-n)d}\hbar(\rho\hbar)^{2k-2-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\int\Lambda_{n}(\bm{p},\bm{q},\sigma)\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\bm{\tilde{p}},\bm{\tilde{q}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi},\tilde{\eta},\tilde{\xi},\sigma)

\displaystyle\times\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k+\beta+1}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k+\beta}^{+}-\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k+\beta}}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k+\beta}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k+\beta}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\langle x,p_{k-3}-q_{k-3}+\tilde{q}_{\beta-1}-\tilde{p}_{\beta-1}+\sum_{m=0}^{\beta/2}\tilde{p}_{2m-2}-\tilde{p}_{2m-3}-\tilde{q}_{2m-2}+\tilde{q}_{2m-3}\rangle}e^{is_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}p_{k-3}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{k+\beta}\frac{1}{2}q_{k-3}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=-3}^{\beta-1}e^{is_{m+k}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}_{m}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m+k}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{m}^{2}}\prod_{m=0}^{k-4}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}q_{m}^{2}}|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}dxd\tilde{x}d\tilde{\eta}d\tilde{\xi}d\bm{p}d\bm{\tilde{p}}d\bm{\tilde{q}}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where the function
f
is given by (
8.54
), the function
\mathcal{G}
is given by (
8.55
), if
\sigma_{n}=k-2
the function
\Lambda_{n}
is given by (
8.56
) and if
\sigma_{n}=k-1
the function
\Lambda_{n}
is given by (
8.57
). The estimates will be different for the two cases
\sigma_{n}=k-2
and
\sigma_{n}=k-1
. For both cases we can argue as in the proof of Lemma
8.6
to obtain the bounds. For the case where
\sigma_{n}=k-2
we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|\leq\hbar^{3}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho^{2}(\rho t)^{2k-n-4}}{(k-3)!(k-1-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2},

(8.60)

where we again have used that
t<t_{0}
. For the case where
\sigma_{n}=k-1
we get that estimate

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(\beta,k,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|

\displaystyle\leq\hbar^{2}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho(\rho t_{0})^{2k-n-3}}{(k-3)!(k-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t_{0}})|^{n+4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}

(8.61)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\hbar^{2}}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\frac{C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2}\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-3)!(k-n)!}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+4}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|+2},

where we have used that for any possible
k
and
n
we have that
2k-n-3\geq 2
and
t_{0}=\frac{t}{\tau_{0}}
. Again it is the integrals in the variables
s_{k+\beta}
and
\tilde{s}_{k+\beta+1}
that ensures we get the factor
\tau_{0}^{-2}
.
Now from combining the estimates in (
8.47
), (
8.48
), (
8.58
), (
8.59
), (
8.60
) (
8.61
) and by arguing as in the previous proofs we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k=4}^{k_{0}}\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\tau_{0}^{-3}\tilde{\lambda}^{\beta}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{d+1}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{5}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+4}

for all
\beta>6
and even. For
\beta>6
and odd we obtain the same estimate by an analogous argument. The main difference is in the starting expression of the function
\mathcal{\tilde{I}}^{\mathrm{dob}}_{3}(\beta,k,\bm{x},s_{k+\beta+1};\hbar)\varphi(x)
as described in the proof of Lemma
8.6
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.9
.

Assume we are in the setting of Definition
3.15
. Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 4\}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\hbar k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+11}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C\tau_{0}^{-3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+22},

and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 4\}}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1}+1,k_{2},(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\hbar k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+11}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+C\tau_{0}^{-3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+22},

where the constant
C
depends on the single site potential
V
and the coupling constant
\lambda
. In particular we have that the function is in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proof.

The proof is done by combining the arguments from the proof of Lemma
4.4
, Lemma
4.5
and Lemma
8.5
. Again we will need to continue expand certain terms. For the terms where we have to continue the expansion we also have to prove “new” versions of
Lemma
8.6
, Lemma
8.7
, and Lemma
8.8
. This is done by combining the arguments in the original proof with the arguments in Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
4.5
.
∎

9
Regularisation of main terms

In this section we will prove some results that enable us to “regularise” the main terms in our expansion. The regularisation can be thought of as going to complex valued energies, which of cause does not make physical sense. But this will ensure that the resolvents become bounded operators. The main thing is that these bounds will be uniform in
\hbar
.
We will from here and in the following need to choose
k_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
depending on
\hbar
in the statement of some of our Lemmas. To be precise we will choose them as

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tau_{0}=\hbar^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

With this choice of
k_{0}
we have that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\hbar^{\frac{1}{3}}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\hbar)|^{k_{0}+50}=0,

(9.1)

where
C
is some constant. The choice of
\tau_{0}
ensure the factor
\hbar^{\frac{1}{3}}
, when needed. We will use the convention that if we sum up to either
k_{0}
or
\tau_{0}
we mean the sum over all natural numbers less than or equal to
k_{0}
or
\tau_{0}
respectively.

Lemma 9.1
.

Assume we are in the same setting as in Definition
3.6
, let
\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\varepsilon>0
be given. Moreover let

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}.

Then there exists
\hbar_{0},\gamma_{0}>0
such that

\sup_{\hbar\in(0,\hbar_{0}]}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\varepsilon,

for all
\gamma\in[0,\gamma_{0}]
, where
\gamma_{0}
and
\hbar_{0}
are independent of each other.

Proof.

By definition,

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)-\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)d}}e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{m}^{2}-p_{m-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1})-\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1})\,\Big\}e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{p}d\bm{s},

where we have omitted the dependece on
\hbar^{-1}(s_{m-1}-s_{m})
and
V
from all
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}
and
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}
. For the difference of the products we will use the identity

\prod_{m=1}^{k}A_{m}-\prod_{m=1}^{k}B_{m}=\sum_{m=1}^{k}A_{1}\cdots A_{m-1}(A_{m}-B_{m})B_{m+1}\cdots B_{k},

(9.2)

with the convention
A_{0}=B_{k+1}=1
. We then have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)-\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\sum_{\nu=1}^{k}\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi(x),

(9.3)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k}\rangle}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{m}^{2}-p_{m-1}^{2})}\tilde{\Psi}_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma,\nu}(p_{m},p_{m-1})\,\Big\}e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{p}d\bm{s},

with the notation

\tilde{\Psi}_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma,\nu}(p_{m},p_{m-1})=\begin{cases}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1})&\text{if $m<\nu$}\\
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1})-\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1})&\text{if $m=\nu$}\\
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1})&\text{if $m>\nu$}.\end{cases}

Note that for the cases where
\alpha_{\nu}=1
we have that
\tilde{\Psi}_{\alpha_{\nu}}^{\gamma,\nu}(p_{\nu},p_{\nu-1})=0
for all
(p_{\nu},p_{\nu-1})
. Hence in the following we will only consider the cases where
\alpha_{\nu}>1
. Using (
9.3
) and the triangle equality for
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega,dx\otimes\mathbb{P})
-norm we see that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sum_{\nu=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(9.4)

Since the form of the function
\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi(x)
is essentially the same as
\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)
we will use an argument similar to that used in Lemma
4.1
. We again expand the
L^{2}
-norm and get

\big\lVert\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa),

(9.5)

where the numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)
are given by

\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{\tilde{x}},\tilde{\alpha},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx,

where again
\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)
is defined as
\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)
for a fixed
\bm{x}
and
\alpha
. Again we consider the direct and crossing terms separately. For the direct terms we get from arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]|\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}

(9.6)

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}}}\frac{1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}}\frac{1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1}^{+}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}.

The difference to the estimate obtained in Lemma
4.1
is the two times integrals we have to estimate differently in this case. The two factors
1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}}
and
1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1}^{+}}
is due to the definition of the functions
\tilde{\Psi}_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma,\nu}
. We have estimated all other exponentials with a
\gamma
by
1
.
Using the identity in (
9.2
) and the fact that the exponentials are bounded by one, we get

\displaystyle(1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}})(1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1}^{+}})\leq\sum_{l_{1}=1}^{\alpha_{\nu}-1}\sum_{l_{2}=1}^{\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1}(1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}t_{l_{1}}})(1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}t_{l_{2}+\alpha_{\nu}}}).

Using the inequality we have just obtained we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}}}\frac{1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}}\frac{1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1}^{+}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}

(9.7)

\displaystyle\leq(\alpha_{\nu}-1)(\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1)\left(\frac{d}{d-2}\right)^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}-2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\frac{(1-e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}t})}{\max(1,|t|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}dt\right)^{2}\leq(\alpha_{\nu}-1)(\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1)C^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}-2}\gamma^{\frac{1}{3}},

where we in the last inequality have used Lemma
2.3
. With these estimates we get from combining (
9.6
), (
9.7
) that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]|\leq(\alpha_{\nu}-1)(\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1)\left(\lambda C_{d}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\gamma^{\frac{1}{3}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}

(9.8)

for all
\gamma\in[0,\gamma_{0}]
. For the crossing terms we get the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)]|\leq\left(C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

(9.9)

This estimate is obtained complete analogous to the estimate obtained in the proof of Lemma
4.1
.
Combining the estimates in (
9.5
) (
9.8
) and (
9.9
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}

(9.10)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{k}\Big[(\alpha_{\nu}-1)(\tilde{\alpha}_{\nu}-1)\gamma^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}+\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}n!}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}\Big],

where we have used that the number of elements in
\mathcal{A}(k,n)
is bounded by
2^{k}
and that the number of elements in
\mathcal{S}_{n}
is
n!
. Evaluating the sums in
\alpha
and
\tilde{\alpha}
and combing the estimates in (
9.4
) and (
9.10
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\nu=1}^{k}\Big[\sum_{n=0}^{k}\gamma^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{(\rho t)^{2k-n}}{k!(k-n)!}+\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}n!}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\nu=1}^{k}\Big[\gamma^{\frac{1}{3}}\frac{(\rho t)^{k}e^{\rho t}}{k!}+C\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the constant
C
depends on the potential and the coupling constant but is independent of
\gamma
and
\hbar
. Using the subadditivity of the square root and that
\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{k(\sqrt{\rho t})^{k}}{\sqrt{k!}}
is finite we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big(\gamma^{\frac{1}{6}}+\sqrt{\hbar k_{0}^{2}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3}}\Big).

(9.11)

Since we have that
\sup_{\hbar\in I}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
is uniformly bounded and
\hbar k_{0}^{2}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3}\rightarrow 0
as
\hbar\rightarrow 0
due to our assumptions on
k_{0}
we get the existence of
\gamma_{0}
and
\hbar_{0}
depending on
\varepsilon
from (
9.11
) such that

\sup_{\hbar\in(0,\hbar_{0}]}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\varepsilon,

for all
\gamma\in[0,\gamma_{0}]
, where
\gamma_{0}
and
\hbar_{0}
are independent of each other. This concludes the proof.
∎

In the following we will use the operator
\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
, which is defined analogous to
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
but with
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\hbar^{-1}(s_{m-1}-s_{m});V)
replaced by
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\infty;V)
.

Lemma 9.2
.

Assume we are in the same setting as in Definition
3.6
, let
\varphi\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
\gamma>0
. Moreover let

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}.

Then

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}^{k}_{\neq}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

Proof.

The proof will be similar to the proof of Lemma
9.1
. Firstly by using the identity in (
9.2
) we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}^{k}_{\neq}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)-\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\sum_{\nu=1}^{k}\mathcal{I}_{\infty,\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi(x),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k}\rangle}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{m}^{2}-p_{m-1}^{2})}\tilde{\Psi}_{\alpha_{m},\infty}^{\gamma,\nu}(p_{m},p_{m-1})\,\Big\}e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{p}d\bm{s},

with the notation

\displaystyle\tilde{\Psi}_{\alpha_{m},\infty}^{\gamma,\nu}(p_{m},p_{m-1})=\begin{cases}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\hbar^{-1}(s_{m-1}-s_{m});V)&\text{if $m<\nu$}\\
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\hbar^{-1}(s_{m-1}-s_{m});V)-\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\infty;V)&\text{if $m=\nu$}\\
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\infty;V)&\text{if $m>\nu$}.\end{cases}

Note again that for the cases where
\alpha_{\nu}=1
we have that
\tilde{\Psi}_{\alpha_{\nu},\infty}^{\gamma,\nu}(p_{\nu},p_{\nu-1})=0
for all
(p_{\nu},p_{\nu-1})
. Hence in the following we will only consider the cases where
\alpha_{\nu}>1
.
The triangle inequality for
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega,dx\otimes\mathbb{P})
-norm gives us that

\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}^{k}_{\neq}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sum_{\nu=1}^{k}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\mathcal{I}_{\infty,\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}},

(9.12)

As in the previous proof we will use an argument similar to that used in Lemma
4.1
. We again expand the
L^{2}
-norm and get

\big\lVert\mathcal{I}_{\infty,\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}=\sum_{n=0}^{k}\sum_{\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa),

(9.13)

where the numbers
\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-1}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)^{2}}})\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{I}_{\infty,\nu}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}_{\infty,\nu}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{\tilde{x}},\tilde{\alpha},t;\hbar)\varphi(x)}\,dx,

where again
\mathcal{I}_{\infty,\nu}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)
is defined as
\mathcal{I}_{\infty,\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)
for a fixed
\bm{x}
and
\alpha
. Again we consider the direct and crossing terms separately.
For the direct terms we get from arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]\leq\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(C\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\rho^{2k-n}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\tfrac{1}{\hbar\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}}\tfrac{\bm{1}_{[\hbar^{-1}(s_{\nu-1}-s_{\nu}),\infty)}(\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+})e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\tfrac{\bm{1}_{[\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\nu-1}-\tilde{s}_{\nu}),\infty)}(\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+})e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}\max(1,|\tilde{t}_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\tilde{\bm{s}},

where we her have not estimate any time integrals yet.
We now bound
\bm{1}_{[\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\nu-1}-\tilde{s}_{\nu}),\infty)}(\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+})e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}}
by
1
and evaluate all integrals in
\tilde{s}
and
\tilde{t}
. We then get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{\rho^{2k-n}t^{k-n}}{(k-n)!}

(9.14)

\displaystyle\times\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[\hbar^{-1}(s_{\nu-1}-s_{\nu}),\infty)}(\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+})e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where we have absorbed all constants from the integration only depending on dimension in to the constant
C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}
.
For the remaining integrals in time we will divide them into two according to if
\gamma\hbar^{-1}(s_{\nu-1}-s_{\nu})
is smaller than one or greater than or equal to one. In the latter case we have that

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{[\hbar^{-1}(s_{\nu-1}-s_{\nu}),\infty)}(\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}}^{+})e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}}^{+}}\leq\frac{C}{|\gamma\hbar^{-1}(s_{\nu-1}-s_{\nu})|^{d}}

for all
\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}}^{+}
and in the first case we can use the trivial bound one. This yields

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[\hbar^{-1}(s_{\nu-1}-s_{\nu}),\infty)}(\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+})e^{-\gamma\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,\alpha_{\nu}-1}^{+}}}{\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{t}d\bm{s}

(9.15)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{d}{d-2}\right)^{a_{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\frac{C}{\max(1,|\gamma\hbar^{-1}(s_{\nu-1}-s_{\nu})|^{d})}\,d\bm{s}_{1,k}\leq\frac{\hbar t^{k-1}d}{\gamma(d-1)(k-1)!}\left(\frac{d}{d-2}\right)^{a_{k}}.

Combining (
9.14
) and (
9.15
) we obtain the bound

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]|\leq{}

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{\hbar\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}}{\gamma(k-n)!(k-1)!},

(9.16)

where we again have absorbed all constants from the integration only depending on dimension in to the constant
C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}
. For the crossing terms we get the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa)]|\leq\left(C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(9.17)

This estimate is obtained complete analogous to the estimate obtained in the proof of Lemma
4.1
. Combining the estimates in (
9.13
) (
9.16
) and (
9.17
) we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\mathcal{I}_{\nu}^{\gamma}(k,t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(C_{d}\lambda\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}

(9.18)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n=0}^{k}\Big[\frac{\hbar\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}}{\gamma(k-1)!(k-n)!}+\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-1}n!}{(k-1)!(k-n)!}\Big],

where we have used that the number of elements in
\mathcal{A}(k,n)
is bounded by
2^{k}
and that the number of elements in
\mathcal{S}_{n}
is
n!
. By arguing as in the proof of Lemma
9.1
we get from (
9.12
) and (
9.18
) that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}^{k}_{\neq}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big(\sqrt{\tfrac{\hbar}{\gamma}}+\sqrt{\hbar k_{0}^{2}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3}}\Big).

(9.19)

Since we have that
\sup_{\hbar\in I}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
and our assumption on
k_{0}
we get from the estimate in (
9.19
) that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}^{k}_{\neq}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

As desired. This concludes the proof.
∎

10
Convergence of error terms

Lemma 10.1
.

Assume we are in the setting of Lemma
3.16
and that
d=3
. Let
\{\varphi_{\hbar}\}_{\hbar\in I}
be a uniformly bounded family in
\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{5d+5}
. Moreover let

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tau_{0}=\hbar^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

Then we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]=0.

Proof.

Firstly recal that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)=\sum_{\tau=1}^{\tau_{0}}U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-\tfrac{\tau_{0}-\tau}{\tau_{0}}t)\big[\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)+\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\mathcal{R}_{\tau,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)\big].

We get from Jensens inequality, the unitarity of
U_{\hbar,\lambda}(t)
and linearity of the average that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]\leq\sum_{\tau=1}^{\tau_{0}}\tau_{0}\Big\{\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]+4\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\tau,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]\Big\}.

(10.1)

We will consider each average separately. We will start with the averages of
\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}
. By definition we have for
\tau=1
that

\mathcal{R}_{1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}=6-j}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1},2,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar).

Recalling that we in Section
8
divided
\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,2}
into three disjoint sets we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]\leq{}
