\displaystyle 4\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=5}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1},2,1}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]

(10.2)

\displaystyle+4\sum_{j=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{k_{1}=4}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1},2,2}^{j}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},0,\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar).\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big].

From applying
Lemmas

7.1
,
8.1
,
8.3
and
8.5
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{1}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]\leq{}

\displaystyle Ck_{0}^{19}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar+C\tau_{0}^{-3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{10}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+20},

(10.3)

where we have combined some of the estimates obtained in the different lemmas by multiplying one of the constants with 10.
For
\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},2,j}}\bm{1}_{\{k_{1}+k_{2}\geq 6-j\}}\Big\{\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1},k_{2},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)

\displaystyle+\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{1}+1,k_{2},(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{2,j}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)\Big\}.

Hence if we apply the “same” inequality as in (
10.2
) and apply
Lemmas

7.2
,
8.2
,
8.4
and
8.9
we obtain for all
\tau\geq 2
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]\leq{}

\displaystyle Ck_{0}^{21}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar+C\tau_{0}^{-3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+22}.

(10.4)

Combining the two estimates in (
10.3
) and (
10.4
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau=1}^{\tau_{0}}\tau_{0}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq C\tau_{0}^{2}k_{0}^{21}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{\tau_{0}})|^{k_{0}+9}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\hbar+C\tau_{0}^{-1}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+22}.

With this estimate and our assumptions on
k_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
it follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{\tau=1}^{\tau_{0}}\tau_{0}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{rec}}(k_{0};\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]=0.

(10.5)

We now turn to the error terms where we have seen
k_{0}+1
different collision centers. Here we have by definition for all
(i,j)\in\{(0,0),(1,1),(2,1),(2,2)\}
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{1,i,j}^{k_{0}}(\hbar)=\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+1,i,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+1-i}}\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar).

Applying
Lemmas

4.6
,
5.4
and
6.5
We obtain the estimate

\displaystyle 4\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{1,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]

(10.6)

\displaystyle\leq 16\Big[\frac{k_{0}^{13}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{16}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-4}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big],

where we have estimate all terms with the “worst” estimate. For
\tau\in\{2,\dots,\tau_{0}\}
and all
(i,j)\in\{(0,0),(1,1),(2,1),(2,2)\}
we have by definition that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{\tau,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)=\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+1,i,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+1-i}}\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)U_{\hbar,0}(-\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+k_{2}+1,i,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}+1-i}}\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{k_{0}}(\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k_{2}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{0}+k_{2}+1,i,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{0}+k_{2}+1-i}}\mathcal{E}_{i,j}^{k_{0}+1}((x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},i,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-i}}\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k_{1},\bm{x}_{1},\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k_{1}=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k_{2}=k_{0}-k_{1}+1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k_{1}+k_{2},i,j}}\sum_{(\bm{x}_{1},\bm{x}_{2})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k_{1}+k_{2}-i}}\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k_{1}+1,(x_{2,k_{2}},\bm{x}_{1}),\iota,\tfrac{t}{\tau_{0}};\hbar)\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k_{2},\bm{x}_{2},\iota,\tfrac{\tau-1}{\tau_{0}}t;\hbar).

Here we will apply the following results:
Lemmas

4.4
,
4.5
,
4.6
,
4.7
,
5.2
,
5.3
,
5.4
,
5.5
,
6.3
,
6.4
,
6.5
and
6.6
. This gives us the estimate

\displaystyle 4\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{1,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]

(10.7)

\displaystyle\leq 50\Big[\frac{k_{0}^{15}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-2}\hbar(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{17}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-4}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big].

Combining the estimates in (
10.6
) and (
10.7
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\sum_{\tau=1}^{\tau_{0}}4\tau_{0}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\tau,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq 50\Big[\frac{k_{0}^{15}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-4}\hbar(k_{0}-2)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\frac{k_{0}^{17}C^{k_{0}}}{\tau_{0}^{k_{0}-6}}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+7}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{3d+3}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big].

With this estimate and our assumptions on
k_{0}
and
\tau_{0}
it follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{\tau=1}^{\tau_{0}}4\tau_{0}\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}_{\tau,i,j}^{k_{0}}(N;\hbar)\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]=0.

(10.8)

Finally by combing (
10.1
), (
10.5
) and (
10.8
) we get that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}\big[\lVert\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\big]=0.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 10.2
.

Assume we are in the setting of Lemma
3.16
, let
\{\varphi_{\hbar}\}_{\hbar\in I}
be a uniformly bounded family in
\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{5d+5}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Moreover let

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}.

Then we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\hbar\in I}\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C,

where
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(0,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)=U_{\hbar,0}(-t)
.

Proof.

Firstly note that by assumption and unitarity of
U_{\hbar,0}(-t)
we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\hbar\in I}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(0,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sup_{\hbar\in I}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C.

(10.9)

For
k\geq 1
we have from Lemma
4.1
the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq\frac{C^{k}}{k!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}+\hbar kC^{k}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k+3}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

where
C
is independent of
\hbar
.
Applying this and the subadditivity of the square-root we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{C^{k}}{k!}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\sqrt{\hbar kC^{k}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k+3}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Since we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{C^{k}}{k!}}<\infty\quad\text{and}\quad\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\hbar k_{0}^{3}C^{k_{0}}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3}=0,

due to our assumption on
k_{0}
, we get that

\sup_{\hbar\in I}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C.

(10.10)

From (
10.9
) and (
10.10
) we get the desired result.
∎

Lemma 10.3
.

Assume we are in the setting of Lemma
3.16
, let
\{\varphi_{\hbar}\}_{\hbar\in I}
be a uniformly bounded family in
\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{5d+5}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Moreover let

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}.

Then for
(i,j)\in\{(1,1),(2,1),(2,2)\}
we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}=0,

where
k_{11}=3
,
k_{21}=5
,
k_{22}=4
.

Proof.

We will only prove the case
(i,j)=(1,1)
as the other cases are proved analogously.
As previous we will start by “writting” up the
L^{2}
-norm and divide into cases depending on the number of collisions. However, before this we will insert “
1
” in the following way
1-e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}}+e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}}
, where
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
are the numbers associated to
\iota
. The numbers
s_{m_{1}}
and
s_{m_{2}}
are the time variables we preform integration in from the definition of the operator
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
. To be precise we will make the following splitting

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)=\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)+\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{2}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\big(1-e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}\big)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m)};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{2}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Theta_{\alpha_{m}}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{\iota(m)};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t).

Applying the triangle inequality for the
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\Omega,dx\otimes\mathbb{P})
-norm we get

\displaystyle\sum_{k=3}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(10.11)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=3}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{2}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

As in the proof of Lemma
7.1
we will then apply Lemma
2.11
such that we only have to consider ladder terms for both cases. Hence we obtain for
j
equal
1
or
2
and for each fixed
\kappa
and
\iota
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{j}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq 2\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\lambda^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{n}n!\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-1,n)}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{j}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\big|,

(10.12)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{j}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\tilde{\bm{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{2k-2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{i}})}{\rho}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{j}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}(x)\overline{\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{j}(k,\bm{\tilde{x}},\tilde{\alpha},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}(x)}\,dx,

where as usual
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{j}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)
is defined as
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{j}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
for fixed
\alpha
. We start with the case
j=1
for a fixed
\kappa
and
\iota
. By definition of the operators we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{j+1}}\int\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\big)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,p_{k}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}p_{m}^{2}}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{\iota(m)},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{is_{0}\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}dyd\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where we have done the change of variables in the time variables as described in Observation
4.2
. Under this change of variables we have that the difference
s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}}
is mapped to
\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}s_{m}
.
We now preform the change of variables
p_{m_{2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{2}-1}-p_{m_{2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-2\}
, where
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
are the numbers associated to the map
\iota
. This change of variables and a relabelling yields the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{j+1}}\int\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\big)

(10.13)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{m_{2}-1}^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{m_{2}-1},\tilde{p}+p_{m_{2}-1},\bm{\eta})\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=1}^{k-1}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Big\{\prod_{m=1}^{k-1}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}))^{2}}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}),p_{m-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\bm{\eta})\Big\}e^{is_{0}\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s},

where
\iota^{*}
is the function associated to
\iota
and

\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}&\text{if $m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-1\}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p})=\begin{cases}\tilde{p}&\text{if $m\in\{m_{1}+1,\dots,m_{2}-1\}$}\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

When taking the averages of
\mathcal{T}^{1}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)
we will consider the following two different cases separately. The cases are given by if there is at least one
\sigma_{i}
such that
m_{1}<\sigma_{i}<m_{2}
or not. For the case where such an
\sigma_{i}
we can argue as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
end obtain the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{1}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+4}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-2)!(k-n-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(10.14)

where in addition to the arguments previous used in the proof of Lemma
5.1
also have used the trivial estimate

\displaystyle 1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\leq 1.

For the other case we have there exists an
i_{1}
such that
\sigma_{i_{1}}<m_{1}<m_{2}\leq\sigma_{i_{1}+1}
. When we take the average of
\mathcal{T}^{1}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)
we apply Lemma
2.9
and integrate all variables in the delta functions to obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{1}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]=\frac{(\rho\hbar(2\pi)^{d})^{2k-2-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2j+2}}\int\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\big)\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{s}_{m}}\prod_{j=\tilde{a}_{m-1}+1}^{\tilde{a}_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{j}}\big)e^{is_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{1}(\tilde{p}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{1}(\tilde{q}))^{2}}\prod_{i=0}^{n}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}+1}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}))^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{q}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{is_{0}\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{0}\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi})|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where the function
\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi})
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\tilde{p},\tilde{q},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi})={}

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{i_{1}},\tilde{p}+p_{i_{1}},\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{m_{2}}}(p_{i_{1}},\tilde{q}+p_{i_{1}},\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{1}(\tilde{p}),p_{\sigma_{i}-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}}(p_{i}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{1}(\tilde{q}),p_{\sigma_{i}-1}+\pi_{\sigma_{i}}^{2}(\tilde{q}),\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=0}^{n}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}+1}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}),p_{i}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\bm{\eta})\overline{\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{\tilde{\alpha}_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{i}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{q}),p_{i}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{q}),\bm{\xi})}.

Due to the definition of
\pi_{m}
and our assumptions on
\sigma
we have that
\pi_{m}
is different from zero only in the places where it is added to
p_{i_{1}}
. Hence we preform the following change of variables
\tilde{p}\mapsto\tilde{p}-p_{i_{1}}
and
\tilde{q}\mapsto\tilde{q}-p_{i_{1}}
. With this change of variables we obtain the following expression

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{1}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]=\frac{(\rho\hbar(2\pi)^{d})^{2k-2-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2j}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})

(10.15)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=\tilde{a}_{m-1}+1}^{\tilde{a}_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\big)\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{s}_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{j}}\big)e^{is_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{i(s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})})\frac{1}{2}p_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=0,i\neq i_{1}}^{n}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i}+1}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}e^{i(s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)})\frac{1}{2}p_{i}^{2}}\prod_{m=\sigma_{i_{1}}+1}^{m_{1}-1}e^{i(s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)})\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{p}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}^{2}}\prod_{m=m_{2}}^{\sigma_{i_{1}+1}-1}e^{i(s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)})\frac{1}{2}p_{i_{1}}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}\eta_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}e^{-i\tilde{t}_{i}\frac{1}{2}\xi_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}-\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\mathcal{G}(\bm{p},\tilde{p}-p_{i_{1}},\tilde{q}-p_{i_{1}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi})|\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,d\bm{\eta}d\bm{\xi}d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}},

where we also have evaluated the integral in
s_{0}
and
\tilde{s}_{0}
. As in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we will now apply the “Fourier method” to estimate the integrals. So we start by divide into
2^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}
cases depending on the size of the
t
’s and
\tilde{t}
’s. Then for each of this cases we divide into a further
2^{n+2}
cases depending on the size of linear combinations of the
s
and
\tilde{s}
variables. Preforming this argument we obtain the estimate

|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{1}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]|\leq C^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n+2}(\rho\hbar)^{2k-2-n}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\mathcal{I}(t,\hbar),

(10.16)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(t,\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2j}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})}{\max(1,|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\frac{\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})}{\max(1,|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|\tilde{t}_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\big)\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{s}_{m}}\prod_{j=\tilde{a}_{m-1}+1}^{\tilde{a}_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{j}}\big)}{\max(1,|\sum_{m=\sigma_{i_{1}}}^{m_{1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}+\sum_{m=m_{2}}^{\sigma_{i_{1}+1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}+s_{m_{3}}-\tilde{s}_{m_{2}}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1,i\neq i_{1}}^{n}\frac{1}{\max(1,|\sum_{m=\sigma_{i}}^{\sigma_{i+1}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(m)}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}.

To estimate the time integrals we first observe that

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,k}^{+}+\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,a_{k}}^{+})

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,m_{1}-1}^{+}+\bm{s}_{m_{2},k}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\sum_{m=1,m\notin\iota^{*}(\sigma)}^{k}\tilde{s}_{m}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+}).

After applying this inequality we evaluate all integrals in
s
and
\tilde{s}
except
s_{m_{1}},\dots,s_{m_{2}-1}
and
\tilde{s}_{m_{1}},\dots,\tilde{s}_{m_{2}-1}
. Moreover we also use that
\iota^{*}(m)=m
for all
m\leq m_{2}-1
. This gives the bound

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(t,\hbar)\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{C(\hbar^{-1}t)^{2k-2(m2-m_{1})-n}}{(k-m_{2}+m_{1})!(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-n)!}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}+m_{2}-m_{1}}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+})\tfrac{\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\big)}{\max(1,|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}s_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}

\displaystyle\times\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{\tilde{a}_{k}+m_{2}-m_{1}}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+})\tfrac{\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{s}_{m}}\prod_{j=\tilde{a}_{m-1}+1}^{\tilde{a}_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}\tilde{t}_{j}}\big)}{\max(1,|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}\tilde{s}_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{k}}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,|\tilde{t}_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}.

Using the identity for difference given in (
9.2
) of products and that
e^{-t}\leq 1
for all
t\geq 0
we get that

\displaystyle\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\big)\leq\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}1-e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}+\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}\sum_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}1-e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}.

Applying this inequality and Lemma
2.3
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}+m_{2}-m_{1}}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+})\frac{\big(1-\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}\prod_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}\big)}{\max(1,|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}+m_{2}-m_{1}}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{m_{1},m_{2}-1}^{+}-s_{m})\frac{1-e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m}}}{\max(1,|s_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}

\displaystyle+\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}\sum_{j=a_{m-1}+1}^{a_{m}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{a_{k}+m_{2}-m_{1}}}\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{m_{1}+1,m_{2}-1}^{+})\frac{1-e^{-\hbar^{\frac{3}{7}}\frac{1}{2}t_{j}}}{\max(1,|s_{m_{1}}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|t_{i}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{t}d\bm{s}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{|\alpha|}\hbar^{\frac{1}{7}}\frac{(\hbar^{-1}t)^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}}{(m_{2}-m_{1}-1)!}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\alpha_{m},

where we have used that we at most can have
|\alpha|
terms and that for any
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-1\}
we have that

\frac{1}{\max(1,|\sum_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}-1}s_{\iota^{*}(m)}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{\max(1,|s_{m}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}.

We get an analogous estimate for the integral over the remaining
\tilde{s}
and
\tilde{t}
. Using these estimates we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(t,\hbar)\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar^{\frac{2}{7}}\frac{(\hbar^{-1}t)^{2k-2-n}}{(k-m_{2}+m_{1})!(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-n)!(m_{2}-m_{1}-1)!(m_{2}-m_{1}-1)!}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\alpha_{m}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\tilde{\alpha}_{m}

(10.17)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar^{\frac{2}{7}}\frac{2^{2k-2-n}(\hbar^{-1}t)^{2k-2-n}}{(k-1)!(k-1-n)!}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\alpha_{m}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\tilde{\alpha}_{m}.

From combining (
10.16
) and (
10.17
) we obtain that

|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{1}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]|\leq(C_{d}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3})^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar^{\frac{2}{7}}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\frac{(2\rho t)^{2k-2-n}}{(k-1)!(k-1-n)!}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\alpha_{m}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\tilde{\alpha}_{m}.

(10.18)

From (
10.12
), (
10.14
) and (
10.18
) we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq 2\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\lambda^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{n}n!

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-1,n)}\Big\{C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+4}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-2)!(k-n-1)!}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+(C_{d}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3})^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar^{\frac{2}{7}}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\frac{(2\rho t)^{2k-2-n}}{(k-1)!(k-1-n)!}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\alpha_{m}\sum_{m=1}^{k}\tilde{\alpha}_{m}\Big\}.

We can estimate all these sums as in the previous proofs and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq(C^{k}k\hbar|\log(\hbar)|^{k+4}+C^{k}k^{2}\hbar^{\frac{2}{7}})\sup_{\hbar\in\mathcal{I}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(10.19)

We now turn to the case where
j=2
. Here we will again consider the two different cases are given by if there is at least one
\sigma_{i}
such that
m_{1}<\sigma_{i}<m_{2}
or not. For the case where such an
\sigma_{i}
we can again argue as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
end obtain the bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}^{2}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)]\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+4}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-2)!(k-n-1)!}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}.

(10.20)

For the other case we again have there exists an
i_{1}
such that
\sigma_{i_{1}}<m_{1}<m_{2}\leq\sigma_{i_{1}+1}
. Observe that for this to be true then
n\leq k-1-m_{2}+m_{1}
. For the previous estimates this observation was not crucial but it will be now. We write up the expression for
\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)
and preform the change of variables
p_{m_{2}-1}\mapsto p_{m_{2}-1}-p_{m_{2}}
and
p_{m}\mapsto p_{m}-p_{m_{2}-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}-2\}
, where
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
are the numbers associated to the map
\iota
. Moreover, we also do a relabelling by changing
p_{m_{2}-1}
into
\tilde{p}
and
p_{m}
into
p_{m-1}
for all
m\in\{m_{2},\dots,k\}
. This yields the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{2}(n,\bm{x},\alpha,\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}x,p_{k-1}\rangle}e^{i(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}\tilde{p}^{2}}e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle\tilde{p},s_{m_{1}}p_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}}p_{m_{2}-1}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\sum_{i=m_{1}+1}^{m_{2}-1}s_{i}\langle\tilde{p},p_{i}-p_{i-1}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k-1}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{is_{\iota^{*}(m)}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{m}^{2}-p_{m-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=1}^{k-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{\iota^{*}(m)}}(p_{m}+\pi_{m}^{1}(\tilde{p}),p_{m-1}+\pi_{m}^{2}(\tilde{p}),\hbar^{-1}(s_{\iota^{*}(m)-1}-s_{\iota^{*}(m)});V)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Psi_{\alpha_{m_{2}}}(p_{m_{2}-1},\tilde{p}+p_{m_{2}-1},\hbar^{-1}(s_{m_{2}-1}-s_{m_{2}});V)e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\,d\bm{p}d\tilde{p}d\bm{s}.

Note the we here have used the form obtained in the proof for the Duhamel expansion. We can again use this to write an expression for
\mathcal{T}^{2}(n,\sigma,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha};\hbar)
. This expression will be similar to the one considered in the proof of Lemma
4.1
. Hence we can preform an argument similar to the one used there. Where the main difference will be in how we handle the integrals in the time variables
s
and
\tilde{s}
. The main ideas is to first divide into cases depending on the size of the
t
’s and
\tilde{t}
’s. Then for each of these terms we use Lemma
2.2
in the variables
p_{1},\dots,p_{n}
. The main difference to the argument used in Lemma
4.1
is in the estimate done in (
4.7
). For the current case we need to estimate the following expression

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\tfrac{e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{s}_{m_{1}}-\tilde{s}_{m_{2}})}}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{n})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{n})}+l^{1}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i+1})}+\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i+1})}+l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

(10.21)

where we due to our assumptions have that
{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}\notin\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}\}
for all
i
and
l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})
are a linear function of the the variables
\bm{t}
and
\bm{\tilde{t}}
. Moreover the the linear function
l^{1}_{i_{1}}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})
will also depend on
s_{m}
and
\tilde{s}_{m}
for
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}\}
. In order to estimate (
10.21
) we firstly observe that

\displaystyle\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}(s_{k},\dots,s_{1})\leq\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1}}(s_{k},\dots,s_{m_{2}+1},s_{m_{1}-1},\dots,s_{1})\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{2}-m_{1}+1}}(s_{m_{2}},\dots,s_{m_{1}}).

We use the same estimate for
\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}(\tilde{s}_{k},\dots,\tilde{s}_{1})
but here we also exclude
\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. Applying these observations/estimates and integrating all variables except
s_{m}
and
\tilde{s}_{m}
for
m\in\{m_{1},\dots,m_{2}\}
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\tfrac{e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{s}_{m_{1}}-\tilde{s}_{m_{2}})}}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{n})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{n})}+l^{1}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i+1})}+\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i+1})}+l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}

(10.22)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{n}\tfrac{\hbar^{n}t^{2k-2m_{2}+2m_{1}-2-n}}{(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1)!(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1-n)!}\Big(\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{2}-m_{1}+1}}e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}\,d\bm{s}\Big)^{2}.

In order to estimate the last integral we start by integrating all variables
s_{i}
with
m_{1}<i<m_{2}
, observe that there is at least one such index. This gives us

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{2}-m_{1}+1}}e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}\,d\bm{s}=\tfrac{1}{(m_{2}-m_{1}-1)!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{2}}(s_{m_{2}},s_{m_{1}})(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}e^{-\hbar^{\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}s_{m_{1}}}\,d\bm{s}.

We then preform the change of variables
s_{m_{1}}\mapsto\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})
. With this change of variables we get

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{m_{2}-m_{1}+1}}e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}\,d\bm{s}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\hbar^{\frac{4}{7}(m_{2}-m_{1})}}{(m_{2}-m_{1}-1)!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{2}}(s_{m_{2}},\hbar^{\frac{4}{7}}s_{m_{1}}+s_{m_{2}})s_{m_{1}}^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s_{m_{1}}}\,d\bm{s}

(10.23)

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{\hbar^{\frac{4}{7}(m_{2}-m_{1})}t}{(m_{2}-m_{1}-1)!}\int_{0}^{\infty}s_{m_{1}}^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}s_{m_{1}}}\,ds_{m_{1}}=\tfrac{\hbar^{\frac{4}{7}(m_{2}-m_{1})}t}{2^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}}.

Combining the estimates in (
10.22
) and (
10.23
) we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\tfrac{e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(s_{m_{1}}-s_{m_{2}})}e^{-\hbar^{-\frac{4}{7}}\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{s}_{m_{1}}-\tilde{s}_{m_{2}})}}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{n})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{n})}+l^{1}_{n}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\tfrac{1}{\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i})}-s_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i+1})}+\tilde{s}_{\iota^{*}(\sigma_{i+1})}+l^{1}_{i}(\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}

(10.24)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{n}\tfrac{\hbar^{n+\frac{4}{7}(m_{2}-m_{1})}t^{2k-2m_{2}+2m_{1}-n}}{(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1)!(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1-n)!4^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}}.

Using the estimate obtained in (
10.24
) and by arguing as in the proof of Lemma
4.1
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{T}(n,\alpha,\tilde{\alpha},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id})]\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}+n}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}

(10.25)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\rho^{2k-n}\hbar^{\frac{8}{7}(m_{2}-m_{1})}t^{2k-2m_{2}+2m_{1}-n}}{(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1)!(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1-n)!4^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}}.

Combining the estimates in (
10.12
), (
10.20
), (
10.24
) and recalling that
n\leq k-1-m_{2}+m_{1}
for the cases where there exists
i_{1}
such that
\sigma_{i_{1}}<m_{1}<m_{2}\leq\sigma_{i_{1}+1}
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq 2\sum_{\alpha,\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\lambda^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\binom{k-1}{n}n!

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{A}(k-1,n)}\Big\{C_{d}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{k}}\left(\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}\right)^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\hbar|\log(\zeta)|^{n+4}\frac{\rho(\rho t)^{2k-n-3}}{(k-2)!(k-n-1)!}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}

\displaystyle+(C_{d}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,3d+3})^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\frac{\bm{1}_{\{n\leq k-1-m_{2}+m_{1}\}}\rho^{2k-n}\hbar^{\frac{8}{7}(m_{2}-m_{1})}t^{2k-2m_{2}+2m_{1}-n}}{(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1)!(k-m_{2}+m_{1}-1-n)!4^{m_{2}-m_{1}-1}}\Big\}.

Using that for the second term we have that
n\leq k-1-m_{2}+m_{1}
we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}^{1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]\leq(C^{k}k\hbar|\log(\hbar)|^{k+4}+C^{k}k^{2}\hbar^{\frac{16}{7}})\sup_{\hbar\in\mathcal{I}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2},

(10.26)

where this is found using the same arguments as previously. From (
10.11
), (
10.19
) and (
10.26
) we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{k=3}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

(10.27)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{k=3}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}}\sqrt{(C^{k}k\hbar|\log(\hbar)|^{k+4}+C^{k}k^{2}\hbar^{\frac{2}{7}})\sup_{\hbar\in\mathcal{I}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=3}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}}\sqrt{(C^{k}k\hbar|\log(\hbar)|^{k+4}+C^{k}k^{2}\hbar^{\frac{16}{7}})\sup_{\hbar\in\mathcal{I}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sup_{\hbar\in\mathcal{I}}\lVert\varphi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}^{2d+2}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}}\Big\{\sqrt{(C^{k_{0}}k_{0}^{7}\hbar|\log(\hbar)|^{k_{0}+4}+C^{k_{0}}k_{0}^{9}\hbar^{\frac{2}{7}})}+\sqrt{(C^{k_{0}}k_{0}^{7}\hbar|\log(\hbar)|^{k_{0}+4}+C^{k_{0}}k_{0}^{9}\hbar^{\frac{16}{7}})}\Big\}.

Our assumptions on
k_{0}
and the estimate obtained in (
10.27
) give us that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k=3}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,1,1}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-1}}\mathcal{I}_{1,1}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

As desired. This concludes the proof.
∎

11
Semiclassical limits for terms without recollisions

Before we start considering the term wise limits and the full limits we will need the following bounds for the function
\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{0},\infty;V)
. In this Lemma it is important that
\gamma>0
.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
m
in
\mathbb{N}
,
\gamma\in(0,1]
and
V\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be given and consider the function
\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{0},\infty;V)
defined in Definition
3.1
. The function is smooth in the variables
p_{m}
and
p_{0}
, and for all all
\alpha
,
\beta
,
\delta
and
\epsilon
in
\mathbb{N}^{d}_{0}
there exists a constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{q,p_{0},p_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{p_{m}}^{\alpha}\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\partial_{q}^{\delta}\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m}+q,p_{0}+q,\infty;V)|

\displaystyle\leq C,

\displaystyle\sup_{q\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|p_{0}^{\epsilon}\partial_{p_{m}}^{\alpha}\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\partial_{q}^{\delta}\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m}+q,p_{0}+q,\infty;V)|\,dp_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\epsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon}|p_{m}^{\epsilon_{1}}|,

\displaystyle\sup_{p_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|p_{0}^{\alpha}\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{0},\infty;V)|

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\alpha_{1}\leq\alpha}|p_{m}^{\alpha_{1}}|

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|p_{0}^{\alpha}\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{0},\infty;V)|\,dp_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{\alpha_{1}\leq\alpha}|p_{m}^{\alpha_{1}}|.

In all estimates the role of
p_{0}
and
p_{m}
can be switched.

Proof.

Note that in the case
m=1
we have by definition that

\Psi_{1}^{\gamma}(p_{1}+q,p_{0}+q,\infty;V)=\hat{V}(p_{1}-p_{0}).

Thus the results follow directly from our assumptions on
V
in this case. We now assume
m\geq 2
.
First we observe that via the change of variables
p_{i}\mapsto p_{i}-p_{0}-q
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,m-1\}
we can write the function
\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{0},\infty;V)
as

\displaystyle\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m}+q,p_{0}+q,\infty;V)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{dm}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m-1}}\int\hat{V}(p_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{m-1}\hat{V}(p_{i}-p_{i-1})\hat{V}(p_{m}-p_{m-1}-p_{0})\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}^{2}+2\langle p_{i},p_{0}+q\rangle+i\gamma)}\,d\bm{p}_{1,m-1}d\bm{t}.

The derivatives are then given by

\displaystyle\partial_{p_{m}}^{\alpha}\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\partial_{q}^{\delta}\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m}+q,p_{0}+q,\infty;V)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{dm}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m-1}}\int\hat{V}(p_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{m-1}\hat{V}(p_{i}-p_{i-1})\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}^{2}+i\gamma)}

\displaystyle\times\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\big[\partial_{p_{m}}^{\alpha}\hat{V}(p_{m}-p_{m-1}-p_{0})\partial_{q}^{\delta}e^{i\langle\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}t_{i}p_{i},p_{0}+q\rangle}\big]\,d\bm{p}_{1,m-1}d\bm{t}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{dm}}\sum_{\beta_{1}\leq\beta}\binom{\beta}{\beta_{1}}i^{|\beta_{1}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m-1}}\int\hat{V}(p_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{m-1}\hat{V}(p_{i}-p_{i-1})\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}e^{it_{i}\frac{1}{2}(p_{i}^{2}+i\gamma)}

\displaystyle\times\big[\partial_{1}^{\alpha+\beta-\beta_{1}}\hat{V}](p_{m}-p_{m-1}-p_{0})\Big(i\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}t_{i}p_{i}\Big)^{\beta_{1}+\delta}e^{i\langle\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}t_{i}p_{i},p_{0}+q\rangle}\,d\bm{p}_{1,m-1}d\bm{t}.

If we just consider the sum
(\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}t_{i}p_{i})^{\beta_{1}}
, then we have that

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}t_{i}p_{i}\Big)^{\beta_{1}+\delta}=\sum_{\epsilon_{1}+\cdot+\epsilon_{m-1}=\beta_{1}+\delta}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\binom{(\beta_{1}+\delta)_{i}}{\epsilon_{1,i}\cdots\epsilon_{m-1,i}}t_{i}^{|\epsilon_{i}|}p_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}},

where the notation
(\beta_{1}+\delta)_{i}
is the
i
’th coordinate of
\beta_{1}+\delta
and
\epsilon_{j,i}
is the
i
’th coordinate of
\epsilon_{j}
. With this notation we get the following bound

\displaystyle|\partial_{p_{m}}^{\alpha}\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\partial_{q}^{\delta}\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m}+q,p_{0}+q,s;V)|

(11.1)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{dm}}\sum_{\beta_{1}\leq\beta}\binom{\beta}{\beta_{1}}\sum_{\epsilon_{1}+\cdot+\epsilon_{m-1}=\beta_{1}+\delta}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m-1}}\int\Big|\hat{V}(p_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{m-1}\hat{V}(p_{i}-p_{i-1})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}\binom{(\beta_{1}+\delta)_{i}}{\epsilon_{1,i}\cdots\epsilon_{m-1,i}}t_{i}^{|\epsilon_{i}|}e^{-t_{i}\frac{1}{2}\gamma}p_{i}^{\epsilon_{i}}\big[\partial_{1}^{\alpha+\beta-\beta_{1}}\hat{V}](p_{m}-p_{m-1}-p_{0})\Big|\,d\bm{p}_{1,m-1}d\bm{t}.

Since we have that
\hat{V}(p_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{m-1}\hat{V}(p_{i}-p_{i-1})
is Schwartz in
(p_{1},\dots,p_{m}-1)
and
t_{i}^{|\epsilon_{i}|}e^{-t_{i}\frac{1}{2}\gamma}
is integrable for all values of
|\epsilon_{i}|
over the positive half-axis we get that

\sup_{q,p_{0},p_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{p_{m}}^{\alpha}\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\partial_{q}^{\delta}\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m}+q,p_{0}+q,\infty;V)|\leq C.

where
C
depends on
\gamma
. From (
11.1
) we do also immediately get that

\sup_{q\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|p_{0}^{\epsilon}\partial_{p_{m}}^{\alpha}\partial_{p_{0}}^{\beta}\partial_{q}^{\delta}\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m}+q,p_{0}+q,\infty;V)|\,dp_{0}\leq C\sum_{\epsilon_{1}\leq\epsilon}|p_{m}^{\epsilon_{1}}|.

The last two bounds follow from analogous arguments. This concludes the proof.
∎

For the next lemmas we will use the following notation.

Notation 11.2
.

Assume we are in the same setting as in Definition
3.6
and with the notation defined in Section
9
. That is we have
\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
, which is defined analogous to
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
but with
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\hbar^{-1}(s_{m-1}-s_{m});V)
replaced by
\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\infty;V)
.
Then we will use the following notation/identification

\displaystyle\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\mathcal{I}^{\gamma}_{\infty}(k,\alpha;\hbar).

That is for each
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)=\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\tilde{\Theta}_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\,d\bm{s}_{k,1}U_{\hbar,0}(-t),

where the kernels of
\tilde{\Theta}_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(s_{m-1},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)
is given by

\displaystyle(p_{m},p_{0})\mapsto{}\frac{1}{\hbar}e^{-i\hbar^{-1/d}\langle z,p_{m}-p_{0}\rangle}e^{is_{m}\hbar^{-1}\frac{1}{2}(p_{m}^{2}-p_{0}^{2})}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{0},\infty;V),

Lemma 11.3
.

Assume we are in the same setting as in Definition
3.6
with the notation introduced in Notation
11.2
and let
\{\varphi_{\hbar}\}_{\hbar\in I}
be a uniform semiclassical family in
\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with Wigner measure
\mu_{0}
. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
. Then for any
a\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho t)^{k}}{k!}\int a(x-tq,q)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q,q,\infty;V)\,d\mu_{0}(x,q).

Proof.

From the definition of the operators we have that the inner product is given by

\displaystyle\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{3d}\hbar^{k}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,q_{1}\rangle}a(\tfrac{x+y}{2},q_{1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y_{0},p_{0}\rangle}e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1}y,p_{k}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k}e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\infty;V)e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{m}^{2}-p_{m-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-x_{0},q_{0}\rangle}e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})e^{-it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dy_{0}dxdyd\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{s}.

When taking the average we get from Campbell’s theorem that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]=\frac{{((2\pi)^{d}\rho t)^{k}}}{k!(2\pi\hbar)^{3d}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,q_{1}\rangle}a(\tfrac{x+y}{2},q_{1})e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y_{0}-y-\frac{1}{2}tp_{0},p_{0}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-x-\frac{1}{2}tq_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{0},p_{0},\infty;V)\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dy_{0}dxdyd{p}_{0}d\bm{q},

where we have integrated all delta functions arising from the integrals in the positions of the Poisson point process and evaluated all integrals in
\bm{s}
. We now preform the change of variables
(x,y)\mapsto(x-y,\frac{1}{2}(x+y))
. This gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]=\frac{{((2\pi)^{d}\rho t)^{k}}}{k!(2\pi\hbar)^{3d}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,q_{1}-\frac{1}{2}(p_{0}+q_{0})\rangle}a(y,q_{1})e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y_{0}-y-\frac{1}{2}tp_{0},p_{0}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y-\frac{1}{2}tq_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{0},p_{0},\infty;V)\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dy_{0}dxdyd{p}_{0}d\bm{q}.

The integral in
q_{1}
and then the integral in
x
is an inverse Fourier transform and a Fourier transform respectively. Combing this with the change of variables
p_{0}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q)
and then
y\mapsto y-y_{0}+\frac{1}{2}tp_{0}+tq
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]=\frac{{((2\pi)^{d}\rho t)^{k}}}{k!(2\pi\hbar)^{d}(2\pi)^{d}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q\rangle}a(y+y_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}t\hbar p_{0}-tq,\tfrac{1}{2}\hbar p_{0}+q)

(11.2)

\displaystyle\times e^{i\langle y,p_{0}\rangle}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(\hbar p_{0}+q,\hbar p_{0}+q,\infty;V)\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dy_{0}dyd{p}_{0}dq.

To calculate the integral in
p_{0}
and
y
we perform a first order Taylor expansion of the function

p_{0}\mapsto a(y+y_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}t\hbar p_{0}-tq,\tfrac{1}{2}\hbar p_{0}+q)\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(\hbar p_{0}+q,\hbar p_{0}+q,\infty;V).

This produces an error of order
\hbar
. To see this let

f(y,y_{0},\hbar p_{0},q)=a(y+y_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}t\hbar p_{0}-tq,\tfrac{1}{2}\hbar p_{0}+q)\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(\hbar p_{0}+q,\hbar p_{0}+q,\infty;V).

(11.3)

Observe that for this function we have integrability in the variables
y
and
q
also after taking a number of derivatives. This is due to our assumption that
a\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
and Lemma
11.1
. Then by a first order Taylor expansion in
\hbar p_{0}
around zero we get that

f(y,y_{0},\hbar p_{0},q)=f(y,y_{0},0,q)+\sum_{\lvert\alpha\rvert=1}(\hbar p_{0})^{\alpha}\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\eta}^{\alpha}f(y,y_{0},\eta+s\hbar p_{0},q))\big|_{\eta=0}\,ds.

Using this we have

\displaystyle\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q\rangle}e^{i\langle y,p_{0}\rangle}f(y,y_{0},\hbar p_{0},q)\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dy_{0}dyd{p}_{0}dq

(11.4)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q\rangle}e^{i\langle y,p_{0}\rangle}f(y,y_{0},0,q)\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dy_{0}dyd{p}_{0}dq

\displaystyle+\sum_{\lvert\alpha\rvert=1}\int\int_{0}^{1}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q\rangle}e^{i\langle y,p_{0}\rangle}(\hbar p_{0})^{\alpha}\partial_{\eta}^{\alpha}f(y,y_{0},\eta+s\hbar p_{0},q))\big|_{\eta=0}\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,\,dsdx_{0}dy_{0}dyd{p}_{0}dq,

where we have omitted the prefactor
(2\pi\hbar)^{-d}
. In order to estimate the error terms we define the two linear operators

L_{q}=\frac{1-i\langle\nabla_{y},\hbar^{-1}(x_{0}-y_{0})\rangle}{1+|\hbar^{-1}(x_{0}-y_{0})|^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad L_{y}=\frac{1-i\langle\nabla_{y},p_{0}\rangle}{1+p_{0}^{2}}.

These operators acts as the identity on
e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q\rangle}
and
e^{i\langle y,p_{0}\rangle}
respectively. Inserting each of these
d+1
times, and integrating by parts, we obtain for each
\alpha
the estimate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\Big|\int\int_{0}^{1}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q\rangle}e^{i\langle y,p_{0}\rangle}(\hbar p_{0})^{\alpha}\partial_{\eta}^{\alpha}f(y,y_{0},\eta+s\hbar p_{0},q))\big|_{\eta=0}\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,\,dsdx_{0}dy_{0}dyd{p}_{0}dq\Big|

(11.5)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{\hbar}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\Big|\sum_{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|\leq d+1}\binom{d+1}{({\tilde{\alpha}},d+1-|{\tilde{\alpha}}|)}\sum_{|\epsilon|\leq d+1}\binom{d+1}{(\epsilon,d+1-|\epsilon|)}\int\frac{|(\hbar^{-1}x_{0}-\hbar^{-1}y_{0})^{\epsilon}|}{(1+|\hbar^{-1}(x_{0}-y_{0})|^{2})^{d+1}}\frac{|p_{0}^{\tilde{\alpha}}|}{(1+p_{0}^{2})^{d+1}}

\displaystyle}{\displaystyle\times(|\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})|^{2}+|{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}|^{2})\sup_{\eta\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\partial_{y}^{{\tilde{\alpha}}+\alpha}\partial_{q}^{\epsilon}\partial_{\eta}^{\alpha}f(y,y_{0},\eta,q))|\,dx_{0}dy_{0}dyd{p}_{0}dq\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\hbar C,

where we have used the inequality
2|\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})|\leq|\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})|^{2}+|\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})|^{2}
. Combining the estimates in
eqs.

11.2
,
11.3
,
11.4
and
11.5
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]

(11.6)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{{((2\pi)^{d}\rho t)^{k}}}{k!(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q\rangle}a(y_{0}-tq,q)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q,q,\infty;V)\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dy_{0}dqdx_{0}+\mathcal{O}(\hbar).

From our assumptions on our states and (
11.6
) we get that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho t)^{k}}{k!}\int a(x-tq,q)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q,q,\infty;V)\,d\mu_{0}(x,q).

This concludes the proof.
∎

Next we want to establish the term wise convergence for the terms, where we are not just acting with the free propagator. This will be done in a number of steps. In order to limit notation we will define the following setting.

Setting 11.4
.

Assume we are in setting of Definition
3.6
with the notation introduced in Notation
11.2
. Let
\{\varphi_{\hbar}\}_{\hbar\in I}
be a uniform semiclassical family in
\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with Wigner measure
\mu_{0}
. Let
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
,
r\in\mathbb{N}
and
{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
. Then for any
a\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
consider the expression

\displaystyle\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle=\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar),

where
\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\tau,\hbar)
is defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k+r}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\rho^{-n}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}

\displaystyle\times\langle\OpW(a)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\tilde{\Theta}_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(s_{{m-1}},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\prod_{m=1}^{r}\tilde{\Theta}_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(s_{{m-1}},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where
\tilde{\Theta}
is the operators from Notation
11.2
.
Finally for
\varepsilon\geq 0
we define
\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k+r}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{\kappa(i)}^{2}})\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{\kappa(i)}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\times\langle\OpW(a)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\tilde{\Theta}_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(s_{{m-1}},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\prod_{m=1}^{r}\tilde{\Theta}_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(s_{{m-1}},{s}_{m},x_{m};V,\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

Lemma 11.5
.

Assume we are in Setting
11.4
and assume that
\kappa
is different from the identity. Then we have that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]|\leq C_{a}\frac{\hbar\rho(\rho t)^{k+r-n-1}}{(k-1)!(r-n)!}(C\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5})^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{2d+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where
C_{a}
depends on the symbol
a
and
C
only depend on the dimension.

Proof.

From the definition of the number
\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)
and by applying the version of Lemma
2.9
mentioned in Remark
2.10
we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{3d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,q_{n+1}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{n}\rangle}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,q_{n}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y_{0},p_{0}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{i}-q_{\kappa(i)}-p_{0}+q_{0}-\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}))\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(p_{i},p_{i-1})e^{is_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{i}^{2}-p_{i-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{i-1},p_{i-1})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle a(\tfrac{x+y}{2},q_{n+1})e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})e^{-it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,d\bm{x}d\bm{y}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where we have used the notation
\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q})=\sum_{j=1}^{i}(q_{{\kappa(j-1)}}-q_{{\kappa(j)-1}})
, and
the convention
\kappa(0)=0
. We are here not explicit writing the dependence on
\infty
and
V
for the
\Psi
-functions. We have also evaluated some of the integrals in the position variables and momentum variables and done a relabelling of some variables. We now do the change of variables

\displaystyle s_{k}\mapsto s_{k}\quad\text{and}\quad s_{m}\mapsto s_{m}-s_{m+1}\quad\text{for all $m\in\{1,,\dots,k-1\}$}

\displaystyle\tilde{s}_{r}\mapsto\tilde{s}_{r}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{s}_{m}\mapsto\tilde{s}_{m}-\tilde{s}_{m+1}\quad\text{for all $m\in\{1,,\dots,r-1\}$}.

Furthermore, we also make the change of variables
(x,y)\mapsto(x-y,\frac{1}{2}(x+y))
. This gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{r}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,r}^{+})e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{n}-q_{n}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y_{0},p_{0}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(p_{i}-q_{\kappa(i)}-p_{0}+q_{0}-\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}))\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(p_{i},p_{i-1})e^{i\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}e^{-i\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i},\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}^{+}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}q_{i}^{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{i-1},p_{i-1})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle a(y,\tfrac{p_{n}+q_{n}}{2})e^{i(t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})e^{-i(t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,r}^{+})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,d\bm{x}d\bm{y}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where we after the change of variables have evaluated the integrals in
x
and
q_{n+1}
.
We now evaluate the integrals in
x_{0}
,
y_{0}
and all integrals in all
p
’s except
p_{0}
. This gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\hbar\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{r}}\int\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,r}^{+})e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{s}_{1,k}^{+})\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i(\hbar^{-1}t-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,r}^{+})\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}\mathcal{G}(p_{0},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2})\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{i\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i)}+p_{0}-q_{0}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}))^{2}}e^{-i\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i},\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}^{+}\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle a(y,\tfrac{p_{0}-q_{0}}{2}+q_{n})\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\overline{\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}\,dydp_{0}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where we have done the change of variables
s_{m}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}s_{m}
and
\tilde{s}_{m}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}\tilde{s}_{m}
for all
m
, we have introduced the notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}(p_{0},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2})=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{\kappa(i)}+p_{0}-q_{0}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}),q_{\kappa(i)-1}+p_{0}-q_{0}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}))\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{\kappa(i)-1}+p_{0}-q_{0}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}),q_{\kappa(i)-1}+p_{0}-q_{0}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}))\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})},

and used that

q_{\kappa(n)}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{n}(\bm{q})=q_{n}\quad\text{and}\quad q_{\kappa(i-1)}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i-1}(\bm{q})=q_{\kappa(i)-1}+l^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}).

As in previous proofs we will let
i^{*}
denote the smallest index such that
\kappa(i^{*})\neq i^{*}
and we define the function
f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})
to be

f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})=\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\neq i^{*}}^{k}s_{i}\big)\bm{1}_{[0,\hbar^{-1}t]}\big(\sum_{i=1,i\notin\sigma}^{r}\tilde{s}_{i}\big).

Using this function and introducing the two variables
s_{0}
and
\tilde{s}_{0}
we get that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]|=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\hbar\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{r}}\int\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{s}_{0,k}^{+})\delta(\tfrac{t}{\hbar}-\bm{\tilde{s}}_{0,r}^{+})f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}e^{is_{0}\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{0}\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}\mathcal{G}(p_{0},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2})\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{i\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i)}+p_{0}-q_{0}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}))^{2}}e^{-i\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i},\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}^{+}\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{6d+6}a(y,\tfrac{p_{0}-q_{0}}{2}+q_{n})\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{-6d-6}\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\overline{\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}\,dydp_{0}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}\Big|,

where we have used integration by parts in
y
to obtain the factor
\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{-6d-6}
. We again use the function
\zeta
as in the previous proofs and write the delta functions as Fourier transforms of the constant function
1
. We then obtain that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]|\leq\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\hbar\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi)^{2}(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{r}}\int f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}\mathcal{G}(p_{0},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{is_{0}(\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{0}(\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{i\bm{s}_{\sigma^{1}_{i},\sigma^{1}_{i+1}-1}^{+}(\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i)}+p_{0}-q_{0}+\tilde{l}^{\kappa}_{i}(\bm{q}))^{2}+\nu+i\zeta)}e^{-i\bm{\tilde{s}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i},\sigma^{2}_{i+1}-1}^{+}(\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{6d+6}a(y,\tfrac{p_{0}-q_{0}}{2}+q_{n})\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{-6d-6}\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\overline{\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}\,dydp_{0}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}\Big|,

Next we evaluate all integrals in
s
and
\tilde{s}
that
f
does not depend on. We then get the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]|\leq\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\hbar\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi)^{2}(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k+r-n-1}}\int f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})\frac{|\mathcal{G}(p_{0},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2})|}{\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{6d+6}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+p_{0}-q_{0}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{|(\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)|}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle|\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{6d+6}a(y,\tfrac{p_{0}-q_{0}}{2}+q_{n})||\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})||\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|\,dydp_{0}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

From here we will mostly use the standard tricks used in previous proofs. In this case we use the estimates

\displaystyle\frac{\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}+p_{0}-q_{0}+q_{i^{*}-1}\rangle^{d+1}}{\prod_{m=1}^{\kappa(i^{*})-2}\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}\prod_{m=\kappa(i^{*})+2}^{n}\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}\langle p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle^{d+1}\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{5d+5}

and

\displaystyle\sup_{\bm{q},p_{0}}\big|\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}\rangle^{d+1}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\langle q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle^{5d+5}\mathcal{G}(p_{0},\bm{q},\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2})\big|\leq C^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}.

Moreover we have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{k+r-n-1}}f(\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}})d\bm{s}_{1,k-1}d\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,r-n}\leq\frac{t^{k+r-n-1}}{\hbar^{k+r-n-1}(k-1)!(r-n)!}.

With these estimates we obtain that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]|\leq\frac{\hbar\rho(\rho t)^{k+r-n-1}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}(k-1)!(r-n)!}C^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\int\frac{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}\langle p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}{\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{6d+6}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\langle\nu\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\frac{1}{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle|\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta|}\prod_{i=\kappa(i^{*})-1}^{\kappa(i^{*})+1}\frac{1}{\langle q_{i}\rangle^{d+1}|(\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)|}\prod_{i=1}^{\kappa(i^{*})-2}\frac{\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|(\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)|}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle\langle q_{\kappa(i^{*})}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1}+p_{0}-q_{0}+q_{i^{*}-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}(q_{\kappa(i^{*})}+p_{0}-q_{0}+q_{i^{*}-1}-q_{\kappa(i^{*})-1})^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}|\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{6d+6}a(y,\tfrac{p_{0}-q_{0}}{2}+q_{n})|\prod_{i=\kappa(i^{*})+2}^{n}\frac{\langle q_{i}-q_{i-1}\rangle^{-d-1}}{|(\frac{1}{2}q_{i}^{2}-\tilde{\nu}-i\zeta)|}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle(|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}+|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2})\,dydp_{0}d\nu d\tilde{\nu}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where we also have used that
|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|\leq|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}+|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}
.
By applying Lemma
2.4
and Lemma
2.5
we obtain that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]|\leq\frac{\hbar\rho(\rho t)^{k+r-n-1}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}(k-1)!(r-n)!}C^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5}^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sup_{q_{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int|\langle\nabla_{y}\rangle^{6d+6}a(y,q_{n})|\,dy\int\frac{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}\langle p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}{\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{6d+6}}(|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}+|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2})\,dp_{0}dq_{0},

where we have absorbed all constants only depending on dimension into
C^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}
. For the remaining integrals we have that the integral over the symbol
a
is a finite constant by assumption. For the other two integrals we have that

\displaystyle\int\frac{\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}\langle p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}{\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{6d+6}}(|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}+|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2})\,dp_{0}dq_{0}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\int\frac{\langle p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle^{5d+5}}{\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{6d+6}}\langle p_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,dp_{0}dq_{0}+\int\frac{\langle p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}{\langle\hbar^{-1}(p_{0}-q_{0})\rangle^{6d+6}}\langle q_{0}\rangle^{4d+4}|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}\,dp_{0}dq_{0}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\hbar^{2d}C\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{2d+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Combining these estimates we get that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]|\leq C_{a}\frac{\hbar\rho(\rho t)^{k+r-n-1}}{(k-1)!(r-n)!}(C\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5})^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{2d+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where
C_{a}
only depend on the symbol
a
and
C
only on the dimension. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 11.6
.

Assume we are in Setting
11.4
and let
\delta>0
. Then there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0
independent of
\hbar
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{\hbar\in I}|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]|\leq C\frac{nt^{r+k-n}}{k!(r-n)!}(C\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,2d+2})^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\delta,

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, where the constant depend on
\gamma
.

Proof.

The proof will be very similar to that of Lemma
4.1
. By definition,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)=\sum_{(\bm{x},\bm{\tilde{x}})\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k+r}}\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(x_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-\tilde{x}_{\sigma_{i}^{2}})}{\rho^{n}(2\pi\hbar)^{3d}\hbar^{k+r}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int\Big(1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}\Big)e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,q_{r+1}\rangle}a(\tfrac{x+y}{2},q_{r+1})e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{k}\rangle}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,q_{r}\rangle}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y_{0}-t\frac{1}{2}p_{0},p_{0}\rangle}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-t\frac{1}{2}q_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{m-1},\infty;V)e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{m},p_{m}-p_{m-1}\rangle}e^{is_{m}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{m}^{2}-p_{m-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=1}^{r}\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{m},q_{m-1},\infty;V)}e^{i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}\tilde{x}_{m},q_{m}-q_{m-1}\rangle}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{m}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{m}^{2}-q_{m-1}^{2})}\,dxdydx_{0}dy_{0}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

We then make the change of variables
(x,y)\mapsto(x-y,\frac{1}{2}(x+y))
and applying the version of Lemma
2.9
mentioned in Remark
2.10
. With this we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{3d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int\Big(1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}\Big)e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,q_{n+1}\rangle}a(y,q_{n+1})e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y+\frac{1}{2}x,q_{n}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y-\frac{1}{2}x,{p}_{n}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}t\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(p_{0})e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(q_{0})}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{\delta(p_{i}-q_{i}-p_{0}+q_{0})\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(p_{i},p_{i-1})e^{is_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{i}^{2}-p_{i-1}^{2})}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\Big\}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{i-1},p_{i-1})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}\,dxdydx_{0}dy_{0}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where we have also evaluated the integrals in
x_{0}
and
y_{0}
. We now evaluate the integrals in
p_{1},\dots,p_{n},q_{n+1}
and
x
. This gives us that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int a(y,\tfrac{p_{0}-q_{0}}{2}+q_{n})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Big(1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}\Big)\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{is_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}((p_{0}-q_{0}+q_{i})^{2}-(p_{0}-q_{0}+q_{i-1})^{2})}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(p_{0}-q_{0}+q_{i},p_{0}-q_{0}+q_{i-1})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}\Big\}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\Big\{\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1}+p_{0}-q_{0},q_{i-1}+p_{0}-q_{0})

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}\Big\}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}t\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(p_{0})e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(q_{0})}\,dxdydx_{0}dy_{0}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

Recalling the expression for the
\Psi
functions from (
3.1
) we can rewrite these functions as follows

\displaystyle\Psi_{m}^{\gamma}(p_{m},p_{0},\infty;V)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m-1}}\int\hat{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(p_{m},p_{0},\bm{\eta}_{1,m-1})e^{-\frac{1}{2}\gamma\bm{t}_{1,m-1}^{+}}\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}(\eta_{i}^{2}-p_{0}^{2})}\,d\bm{\eta}_{1,m-1}d\bm{t}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{m-1}}\int\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(p_{m}-p_{0},\bm{\eta}_{1,m-1})\prod_{i=1}^{m-1}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}(\eta_{i}^{2}+i\gamma)}e^{it_{i}\langle\eta_{i},p_{0}\rangle}\,d\bm{\eta}_{1,m-1}d\bm{t},

where the functions
\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(p_{m}-p_{0},\bm{\eta}_{1,m-1})
is defined by
