\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{m}(p_{m}-p_{0},\bm{\eta}_{1,m-1})=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{dm}}\begin{cases}\hat{V}(p_{m}-p_{0})&\text{if $m=1$,}\\
\hat{V}(p_{m}-p_{0}-\eta_{m-1})\hat{V}(\eta_{1})\prod_{i=2}^{m-1}\hat{V}(\eta_{i}-\eta_{i-1})&\text{if $m>1$.}\end{cases}

Using this expression we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{a_{k}+\tilde{a}_{r}}_{+}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int\Big(1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}\Big)\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-i(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}e^{i\langle q_{i},l_{i}(\hbar,\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}},q_{0},p_{0},\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi})\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{a_{k}}e^{i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}(\eta_{i}^{2}+i\gamma)}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{\tilde{a}_{r}}e^{-i\frac{1}{2}t_{i}(\xi_{i}^{2}-i\gamma)}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}(q_{i}-q_{i-1},\bm{\eta})\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}(q_{i}-q_{i-1},\bm{\xi})}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\alpha_{m}}(0,\bm{\eta})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\tilde{\mathcal{V}}_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}(0,\bm{\xi})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{0}-q_{0}\rangle}a(y,\tfrac{p_{0}-q_{0}}{2}+q_{n})e^{i\hbar^{-1}t\frac{1}{2}p_{0}^{2}}\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(p_{0})e^{-i\hbar^{-1}t\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(q_{0})}\,dxdydx_{0}dy_{0}dp_{0}d\bm{q}d\bm{t}d\bm{\tilde{t}}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where the functions
l_{i}(\hbar,\bm{s},\bm{\tilde{s}},q_{0},p_{0},\bm{t},\bm{\tilde{t}},\bm{\eta},\bm{\xi})
are linear functions. As in previous proofs we will now use Lemma
2.2
for the variables
q_{1},\dots,q_{n}
. Moreover, we use integration by parts in the variable
y
to get the factor
\sqrt{1+|\hbar^{-1}(q_{0}-p_{0})|^{2}}^{-d-1}
. We then obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]|\leq\frac{C\rho^{k+r-n}}{\hbar^{2d}}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,2d+2}^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{a_{k}}_{+}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+}\gamma}\,d\bm{t}

(11.7)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\sup_{|\beta|\leq d+1}\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{\beta}a(y,q)\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar}){\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}|}{(1+|\hbar^{-1}(q_{0}-p_{0})|^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}dydp_{0}dq_{0}dq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{a}_{r}}_{+}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{r}}^{+}\gamma}\,d\bm{\tilde{t}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\tfrac{1}{\hbar\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{n}^{2}}-s_{\sigma_{n}^{1}}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\Big(1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{\kappa(i)}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}\Big)\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\tfrac{1}{\hbar\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}-s_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-(\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}-s_{\sigma_{i+1}^{1}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{s}d\tilde{\bm{s}},

Firstly we observe that using the inequality
2|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar}){\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}|\leq|\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar})|^{2}+|{\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}|^{2}
we get the estimate

\displaystyle\int\frac{|\partial_{y}^{\beta}a(y,q)\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{p_{0}}{\hbar}){\hat{\varphi}_{\hbar}(\tfrac{q_{0}}{\hbar})}|}{(1+|\hbar^{-1}(q_{0}-p_{0})|^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}dydp_{0}dq_{0}dq\leq C\hbar^{2d}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\lVert\partial_{x}^{\beta}a(x,p)\rVert_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(11.8)

Since
\gamma>0
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{a_{k}}_{+}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\bm{t}_{1,a_{k}}^{+}\gamma}\,d\bm{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{\tilde{a}_{r}}_{+}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\bm{\tilde{t}}_{1,\tilde{a}_{r}}^{+}\gamma}\,d\bm{\tilde{t}}\leq C_{\gamma}.

(11.9)

Preforming the change of variables
\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}})
for all
i
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\tfrac{1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{\kappa(i)}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}}{\hbar\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{n}^{2}}-s_{\sigma_{n}^{1}}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\tfrac{1}{\hbar\max(1,\hbar^{-1}|\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{i}^{2}}-s_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}-(\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{i+1}^{2}}-s_{\sigma_{i+1}^{1}})|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{s}d\tilde{\bm{s}}

(11.10)

\displaystyle=\frac{t^{r+k-n}}{k!(r-n)!}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon s_{i})^{2}}}{\max(1,|s_{n}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\frac{1}{\max(1,|s_{i}-s_{i+1}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{s}.

Since we have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{\max(1,|s_{n}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\frac{1}{\max(1,|s_{i}-s_{i+1}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{s}<\infty.

(11.11)

It follows by dominated convergence that we can find
\varepsilon_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1-\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon s_{i})^{2}}}{\max(1,|s_{n}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}\frac{1}{\max(1,|s_{i}-s_{i+1}|)^{\frac{d}{2}}}d\bm{s}\leq C\delta

(11.12)

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
. Combining the estimates in (
11.7
), (
11.8
), (
11.9
) , (
11.10
) and (
11.12
) we arrive at the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]|\leq C\frac{nt^{r+k-n}}{k!(r-n)!}(C\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,2d+2})^{|\alpha|+|\tilde{\alpha}|}\delta

for all
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, where the constant
C
is independent of
\hbar
but depend on
\gamma
.
∎

In the introduction we presented the Weyl quantisation of a symbol. We will in the following lemma also need the “
0
-quantisation” of a symbol. For
a\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
this quantisation is given by

\mathrm{Op}_{0,\hbar}(a)\psi(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,p\rangle}a(y,p)\psi(y)\,dydp

with
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The two types of quantisation are related, in particular we have that

\lVert\OpW(a)-\mathrm{Op}_{0,\hbar}(a)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}=\mathcal{O}(\hbar),

(11.13)

for all
a\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
; for more details see
[
15
,
18
]
. This in particular implies that if we have convergence of our semiclassical measures for the Weyl-quantisation we also have it for the
0
-quantisation, and vice versa. This we have already used in the proof of Lemma
11.3
.

Lemma 11.7
.

Assume we are in Setting
11.4
and assume that
\kappa
is the identity. Then we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\langle\mathrm{Op}_{0,\hbar}(a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t})\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle+\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}(\sqrt{\hbar}),

(11.14)

where the symbol
a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t}
is Schwartz class and given by

\displaystyle a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t}(x,q_{0})={}

\displaystyle\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}\pi^{\frac{n}{2}}}{\varepsilon^{n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nd}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{-2}\frac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}

\displaystyle\times a(x+y_{0}-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})\,d\bm{q}_{1,n}d\bm{s}_{n,1}.

We observe that from this lemma and (
11.13
) we get that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2d}}a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(y,q_{0})\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0}).

(11.15)

Proof.

From the definition of the number
\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)
and by applying Campbell’s theorem we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{3d}\hbar^{2n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,q_{n+1}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{n}\rangle}

(11.16)

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,q_{n}\rangle}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y_{0},p_{0}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{-i\langle\hbar^{-1/d}x_{i},p_{i}-p_{i-1}-q_{i}+q_{i-1}\rangle}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(p_{i},p_{i-1})e^{is_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{i}^{2}-p_{i-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\Big\}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{i-1},p_{i-1})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times a(\tfrac{x+y}{2},q_{n+1})e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})e^{-it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dyd\bm{x}dy_{0}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

where we are using the convention
\sigma^{1}_{n+1}=k+1
and
\sigma^{2}_{\kappa(n+1)}=r+1
. We are here not explicit writing the dependence on
\infty
and
V
for the
\Psi
-functions. We have also evaluated some of the integrals in the position variables and momentum variables and furthermore a relabelling of some variables. To see how the matching of the remaining momentum variables is, it will be convenient to do the change of variables
x_{1}\mapsto\hbar^{-\frac{1}{d}}x_{1}
and
x_{i}\mapsto\hbar^{-\frac{1}{d}}(x_{i}-x_{i-1})
for
i\in\{2,\dots,n\}
. Furthermore we also make the change of variables
(x,y)\mapsto(x-y,\frac{1}{2}(x+y))
. This gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{3d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,q_{n+1}-\frac{1}{2}(p_{n}+q_{n})\rangle}

(11.17)

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y,p_{n}-q_{n}\rangle}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}\langle y_{0},p_{0}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{-i\langle x_{i},p_{i-1}-q_{i-1}-p_{n}+q_{n}\rangle}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(p_{i},p_{i-1})e^{is_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{i}^{2}-p_{i-1}^{2})}

\displaystyle\times\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\Big\}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(p_{i-1},p_{i-1})\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times a(y,q_{n+1})e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}p_{0}^{2}}\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})e^{-it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dyd\bm{x}dy_{0}d\bm{p}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

We now evaluate all integrals in all
x
’es except
x_{0}
, in all
p
’s except
p_{n}
and
q_{n+1}
. This gives us

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y-y_{0},p_{n}-q_{n}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q_{0}\rangle}

(11.18)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i}+p_{n}-q_{n},q_{i-1}+p_{n}-q_{n})e^{is_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}((q_{i}+p_{n}-q_{n})^{2}-(q_{i-1}+p_{n}-q_{n})^{2})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}e^{-i\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\Big\}e^{it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(p_{n}+q_{0}-q_{n})^{2}}a(y,\tfrac{1}{2}(p_{n}+q_{n}))\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})e^{-it\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}q_{0}^{2}}\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1}+p_{n}-q_{n},q_{i-1}+p_{n}-q_{n})\,dx_{0}dydy_{0}dp_{n}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}.

By rewriting the phase functions and making the change of variables
p_{n}\mapsto p_{n}-q_{n}
and
y\mapsto y-y_{0}+\tfrac{1}{2}t(p_{n}+q_{n}-q_{n})
we get the expression

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y+tq_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),p_{n}+q_{n}-q_{n}\rangle}

(11.19)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}))^{2}}e^{-i(\tilde{s}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}-s_{\sigma^{1}_{i}})\frac{1}{2}\hbar^{-1}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i}+p_{n},q_{i-1}+p_{n})\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{\beta_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1}+p_{n},q_{i-1}+p_{n})

\displaystyle\times a(y+y_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}t(p_{n}+q_{n}-q_{n}),\tfrac{1}{2}(p_{n}+q_{n}+q_{n}))\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dydy_{0}dp_{n}d\bm{q}d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

We will just consider the integrals in the time variables. Our integrals have the form

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{k}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{r}}f(s_{\sigma_{1}^{1}},\dots,s_{\sigma_{n}^{1}})g(\tilde{s}_{\sigma_{2}^{1}},\dots,{s}_{\sigma_{n}^{2}})d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s}

(11.20)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(s_{n},\dots,s_{1})\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(\tilde{s}_{n},\dots,\tilde{s}_{1})f(s_{1},\dots,s_{n})g(\tilde{s}_{1},\dots,{s}_{n})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!}\frac{(\tilde{s}_{i-1}-\tilde{s}_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}\,d\bm{\tilde{s}}d\bm{s},

We then have the expression

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{2d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle y+tq_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),p_{n}\rangle}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q_{0}\rangle}

(11.21)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{i}-s_{i}))^{2}}e^{-i\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{i}-s_{i})\frac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i}+p_{n},q_{i-1}+p_{n})\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1}+p_{n},q_{i-1}+p_{n})

\displaystyle\times\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(\tilde{s}_{n},\dots,\tilde{s}_{1})\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(s_{n},\dots,s_{1})\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!}\frac{(\ \tilde{s}_{i-1}-\tilde{s}_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}

\displaystyle\times a(y+y_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}tp_{n},\tfrac{1}{2}p_{n}+q_{n})\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dydy_{0}dp_{n}d\bm{q}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}.

Here we make the change of variables
p_{n}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}p_{n}
. As in the proof of Lemma
11.3
we preform a first order Taylor expansion of the function

\displaystyle p_{n}\mapsto\prod_{i=1}^{n}

\displaystyle\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i}+\hbar p_{n},q_{i-1}+\hbar p_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1}+\hbar p_{n},q_{i-1}+\hbar p_{n})

(11.22)

\displaystyle\times a(y+y_{0}-\tfrac{1}{2}t\hbar p_{n},\tfrac{1}{2}\hbar p_{n}+q_{n}).

This will, with a similar argument to that in Lemma
11.3
, produce an error of order
\hbar
. Hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\hbar^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{(n+3)d}}e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{i}-s_{i}))^{2}}

(11.23)

\displaystyle\times e^{-i\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{i}-s_{i})\frac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}\Big\}\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(\tilde{s}_{n},\dots,\tilde{s}_{1})\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(s_{n},\dots,s_{1})

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!}\frac{(\ \tilde{s}_{i-1}-\tilde{s}_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})

\displaystyle\times a(y_{0}-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dy_{0}d\bm{q}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}+\mathcal{O}(\hbar).

Before we proceed we will remove the function
\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(\tilde{s}_{n},\dots,\tilde{s}_{1})
from our expression. To this end we will by
\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi,n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)
denote the expression with this function removed. By the change of variables
\tilde{s}_{n}\mapsto\tilde{s}_{n}
,
\tilde{s}_{i}\mapsto\tilde{s}_{i}-\tilde{s}_{i-1}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
and
s_{n}\mapsto s_{n}
,
s_{i}\mapsto s_{i}-s_{i-1}
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
and afterwards arguing as in previous lemmas we obtain the bound

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)-\tilde{\mathcal{J}}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]|

(11.24)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C\hbar^{-n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\bm{1}_{[t,\infty)}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,n}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,n}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\hbar^{-1}(\bm{\tilde{s}}_{i,n}^{+}-\bm{s}_{i,n}^{+}))^{2}}d\bm{s}d\bm{\tilde{s}}+\mathcal{O}(\hbar),

where the constant
C
is independent of
\hbar
. We now change variables
\tilde{s}_{i}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{i}-s_{i})
for all
i
, and note that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\bm{1}_{[t,\infty)}(\hbar\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,n}^{+}+\bm{s}_{1,n}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,n}^{+})\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\bm{\tilde{s}}_{i,n}^{+})^{2}}d\bm{s}_{1,n}d\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,n}

(11.25)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\hbar}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2n}}\bm{1}_{[t,\infty)}(\hbar\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,n}^{+}+\bm{s}_{1,n}^{+})\bm{1}_{[0,t]}(\bm{s}_{1,n}^{+})\frac{\sqrt{\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,n}^{+}}}{\sqrt{t-\bm{s}_{1,n}^{+}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{-(\varepsilon\bm{\tilde{s}}_{i,n}^{+})^{2}}d\bm{s}_{1,n}d\bm{\tilde{s}}_{1,n}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{\varepsilon^{n+1}}}C\int_{[0,t]^{n}}\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(s_{n},\dots,s_{2})\frac{1}{\sqrt{t-s_{1}}}d\bm{s}_{1,n}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{\varepsilon^{n+1}}}C.

Combining (
11.23
), (
11.24
) and (
11.25
) and the change of variables
\tilde{s}_{i}\mapsto\hbar^{-1}(\tilde{s}_{i}-s_{i})
for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n\}
we obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k+r-n}\pi^{\frac{n}{2}}}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}\varepsilon^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x_{0}-y_{0},q_{0}\rangle}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{e^{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{-2}\frac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma^{1}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})

(11.26)

\displaystyle\times\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\sigma^{2}_{i}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})}\Big\}\bm{1}_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}(s_{n},\dots,s_{1})\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\Big\{\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1})\Big\}a(y_{0}-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})

\displaystyle\times\varphi_{\hbar}(y_{0})\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x_{0})}\,dx_{0}dy_{0}d\bm{q}d\bm{s}+\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}(\sqrt{\hbar}),

where we have also evaluated the integrals in
\tilde{s}
(these are Fourier transforms of the functions
e^{-(\varepsilon\tilde{s}_{i})^{2}}
). We observe that the above expression can be written in the form

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\langle\mathrm{Op}_{0,\hbar}(a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t})\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle+\mathcal{O}_{\varepsilon}(\sqrt{\hbar}),

(11.27)

where the symbol
a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t}(x,q_{0})
can be seen directly in (
11.26
). Moreover it follows from Lemma
11.1
that this symbol is Schwartz class.
∎

Lemma 11.8
.

Assume we are in the same setting as in Definition
3.6
and let
\{\varphi_{\hbar}\}_{\hbar\in I}
be a uniform semiclassical family in
\mathcal{H}^{d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with Wigner measure
\mu_{0}
. Moreover, let

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}.

Then for any
a\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,r=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\int a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0}),

where the symbol
a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})
is given by

\displaystyle a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})=((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{(k+r-n)}(2\pi)^{n}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}\int a(y-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(\tfrac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2}))

(11.28)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1},\infty;V)\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{{\sigma_{i}^{2}}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1},\infty;V)}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1},\infty;V)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1},\infty;V)\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}+\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-2}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}\,d\bm{q}_{1,n}d\bm{s}_{n,1}.

Proof.

As in Setting
11.4
we have for all
k
,
r
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}
and
{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
that

\displaystyle\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle=\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}}\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar),

where
\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\tau,\hbar)
is defined in Setting
11.4
. This gives us that

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]

(11.29)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]

\displaystyle+\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\setminus\{\mathrm{id}\}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)].

Using Lemma
11.5

we have that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\setminus\{\mathrm{id}\}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]\Big|

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}C_{a}2^{k+r}\frac{\hbar\rho(\rho t)^{k+r-n-1}n!}{(k-1)!(r-n)!}(\lambda C\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,5d+5})^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{n+3}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{2d+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

where
C_{a}
depends on the symbol
a
and
C
only depend on the dimension. We have moreover used that
\mathcal{S}_{n}
contains
n!
elements and that the number of elements in
\mathcal{A}(k,n)
is bounded by
2^{k}
and the number of elements in
\mathcal{A}(r,n)
is bounded by
2^{r}
. From arguing as in the previous proofs we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\setminus\{\mathrm{id}\}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]\Big|

(11.30)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle CC^{k_{0}}k_{0}^{3}\hbar|\log(\tfrac{\hbar}{t})|^{k_{0}+3}\sup_{\hbar\in I}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{2d+2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Our assumptions on
k_{0}
and the estimate in (
11.30
) implies that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\Big|\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}\sum_{\kappa\in\mathcal{S}_{n}\setminus\{\mathrm{id}\}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]\Big|=0.

(11.31)

From arguing as in the proof of Lemma
11.6
we can obtain the bound

|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]|\leq C\frac{t^{r+k-n}}{k!(r-n)!}(C\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,2d+2})^{|\alpha|+|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(11.32)

Applying this estimate gives us that

\displaystyle\sup_{\hbar\in I}\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}|(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]|

(11.33)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\hbar\in I}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sum_{n=0}^{k_{0}}\sum_{k,r=n}^{k_{0}}C^{k+r}\frac{t^{r+k+n}}{k!(r-n)!}\leq\sup_{\hbar\in I}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k,r=0}^{\infty}\frac{C^{r+k+n}}{k!n!r!}<\infty.

From this estimate we have that the sums are absolutely convergent. Hence using dominated convergence we get from (
11.29
), (
11.31
) and (
11.33
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]

(11.34)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(-i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)].

Let
\delta>0
then from Lemma
11.6
we have that there exsist
\varepsilon_{0}
such that for all
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{\hbar\in I}|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]|\leq C\delta.

(11.35)

Furthermore, from Lemma
11.7
we get that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]=\langle\mathrm{Op}_{0,\hbar}(a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t})\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle+C_{\varepsilon}\sqrt{\hbar},

(11.36)

where the constant
C_{\varepsilon}
is independent of
\hbar
but will increase polynomial as
\varepsilon
goes to zero. Moreover from Lemma
11.7
we also have that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}|\langle\mathrm{Op}_{0,\hbar}(a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t})\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle-\int a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(y,q_{0})\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0})|=0.

(11.37)

We now compare the integrals of the symbols
a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}
and
a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}
, defined in (
11.14
) and (
11.28
) respectively, against the measure
\mu_{0}
. Here we see that

\displaystyle\int\big(a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(y,q_{0})-a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})\big)\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0})=((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{(k+r-n)}(2\pi)^{n}

\displaystyle\times\int\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}\int a(y-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Big\{\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1},\infty;V)\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{{\sigma_{i}^{2}}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1},\infty;V)}\Big\}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{\frac{1}{(4\pi\varepsilon^{2})^{\frac{n}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{-2}\frac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}-\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(\tfrac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2}))\Big\}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\Big\{\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1},\infty;V)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1},\infty;V)\Big\}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}+\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-2}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}\,d\bm{q}d\bm{s}d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0}).

We know that in a distributional sense we have that

\frac{1}{(4\pi\varepsilon^{2})^{\frac{n}{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}e^{\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon^{-2}\frac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2})}-\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(\tfrac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2}))\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0$}.

In particular this implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\big|\int\big(a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(y,q_{0})-a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})\big)\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0})\big|=0.

(11.38)

To apply all these estimates we note that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]-\int a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0})\big|

(11.39)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)-\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]|

\displaystyle+|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}_{\varepsilon}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\kappa,\hbar)]-\langle\mathrm{Op}_{0,\hbar}(a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t})\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle|

\displaystyle+|\langle\mathrm{Op}_{0,\hbar}(a\circ\Phi_{\gamma,\varepsilon}^{t})\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle-\int a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(y,q_{0})\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0})|

\displaystyle+\big|\int\big(a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,\varepsilon}(y,q_{0})-a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})\big)\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0})\big|.

By first letting
\hbar\rightarrow 0
and the
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
it follows from (
11.35
), (
11.36
), (
11.37
) (
11.38
) and (
11.39
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\big|\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{J}(a,\varphi_{\hbar},n,\sigma^{1},\sigma^{2},\mathrm{id},\hbar)]-\int a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0})\big|=0.

(11.40)

Lastly by (
11.34
) and (
11.40
) the desired result follows and this concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 11.9
.

Assume we are in setting of Definition
3.6
. Let
\{\varphi_{\hbar}\}_{\hbar\in I}
be a uniform semiclassical family in
\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{\hbar}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with Wigner measure
\mu_{0}
.
Let

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}.

Then, for
\gamma>0
,

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k,r=0}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{r}}\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle\Big]=\int f^{\gamma}(t,y,p)\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0}),

where we have used the convention
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(0,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)=U_{\hbar,0}(-t)
and the notation that

\displaystyle f^{\gamma}(t,y,q_{0})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{((2\pi)^{d+1}\rho)^{n}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}\int a(y-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})|\hat{\varphi}_{1}(q_{0})|^{2}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(\tfrac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2}))|\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})|^{2}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}e^{-(2\pi)^{d}\rho(s_{i-1}-s_{i})2\im(\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))}\,d\bm{q}d\bm{s}.

Proof.

From Lemma
9.2
we have that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k=1}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}^{k}_{\neq}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi-\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi\Big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\bigg]^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

This gives us that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k,r=0}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{r}}\langle

\displaystyle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle

(11.41)

\displaystyle-\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle\Big]=0.

Using the definition of the operators and the notation introduced in Notation
11.2
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=0}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{r}}\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}(-i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle].

By arguing as in the proof of Lemma
11.8
we see that the sum is absolute convergent for all
\gamma\geq 0
uniformly in
\hbar
. Hence we obtain that

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k,r=0}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{r}}\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle\Big]

(11.42)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}(-i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle].

We now just need to find the term-wise limits. For the case
k=r=0
we have, by Egorov’s theorem, that

\displaystyle\langle\OpW(a)U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle=\frac{1}{(2\pi\hbar)^{d}}\int e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x-y,q\rangle}a(\tfrac{x+y}{2}-tq,q)\varphi_{\hbar}(y)\overline{\varphi_{\hbar}(x)}\,dydqdx,

and hence

\displaystyle\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\langle\OpW(a)U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle=\int a(y-tq,q)\,d\mu_{0}(y,q).

(11.43)

Lemma
11.3
gives us that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]

(11.44)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}(i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho t)^{k}}{k!}\int a(y-tq,q)\prod_{m=1}^{k}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q,q,\infty;V)\,d\mu_{0}(y,q)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(i(2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k}t^{k}}{k!}\int a(y-tq,q)(\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))^{k}\,d\mu_{0}(y,q),

where we have used the identity (
3.5
), which is

\hat{T}^{\gamma}(p,p_{0})=-i\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(i\lambda)^{n}\Psi_{n}^{\gamma}(p,p_{0},\infty;V),

(11.45)

from Remark
3.3
. Analogously we get that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}(-i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)U_{\hbar,0}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]

(11.46)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-i(2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{k}t^{k}}{k!}\int a(y-tq,q)(\overline{\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q)})^{k}\,d\mu_{0}(y,q).

For the final term we get from Lemma
11.8
that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{r=1}^{\infty}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}(-i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]

(11.47)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}(-i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\int a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})\,d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0}),

where the symbol
a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})
is given by

\displaystyle a\circ\Phi^{t}_{\gamma,0}(y,q_{0})={}

\displaystyle((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{(k+r-n)}(2\pi)^{n}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}\int a(y-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(\tfrac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2}))

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Psi_{\alpha_{\sigma_{i}^{1}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1},\infty;V)\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{{\sigma_{i}^{2}}}}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1},\infty;V)}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{2}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{2}_{i}-1}\overline{\Psi_{{\tilde{\alpha}}_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1},\infty;V)}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\prod_{m=\sigma^{1}_{i-1}+1}^{\sigma^{1}_{i}-1}\Psi_{\alpha_{m}}^{\gamma}(q_{i-1},q_{i-1},\infty;V)\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(s_{i-1}-s_{i})^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}+\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-2}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}\,d\bm{q}d\bm{s}.

We split the sums in (
11.47
) into terms with
n=0
and the rest. More precisely we write

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{n=0}^{\min(k,r)}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}=\sum_{k,r=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k,r=n}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}.

Using this splitting and reordering of the sums and the identity for the Born series (
11.45
) we obtain from (
11.43
), (
11.44
), (
11.46
) and (
11.47
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{k}}\sum_{{\tilde{\alpha}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}}(-i\lambda)^{|\alpha|}(i\lambda)^{|{\tilde{\alpha}}|}\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(k,\alpha;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{\infty}^{\gamma}(r,{\tilde{\alpha}};\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle]

(11.48)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=0}^{\infty}i^{k}(-i)^{r}\frac{((2\pi)^{d}\rho t)^{(k+r)}}{k!r!}\int a(y-tq,q)(\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))^{k}(\overline{\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q)})^{r}\,d\mu_{0}(y,q)

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k,r=n}^{\infty}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{(k+r-n)}(2\pi)^{n}\int\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}\int\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(\tfrac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2}))

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(i(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1}(-i(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\overline{\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q)})^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}

\displaystyle\times a(y-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})\hat{\varphi}_{1}(q_{0})\overline{\hat{\varphi}_{2}(q_{0})}\prod_{i=1}^{n}|\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})|^{2}\,d\bm{q}d\bm{s}d\mu_{0}(y,q_{0}).

If we just consider the sums over
k
,
r
,
\sigma^{1}
and
\sigma^{2}
we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\sum_{r=n}^{\infty}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{(k+r-n)}

(11.49)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(i(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1}(-i(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\overline{\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q)})^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{n}\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\sum_{\sigma^{1}\in\mathcal{A}(k,n)}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(i(2\pi)^{d}\rho(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))^{\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{1}-\sigma_{i-1}^{1}-1)!}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{r=n}^{\infty}\sum_{\sigma^{2}\in\mathcal{A}(r,n)}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\frac{(-i(2\pi)^{d}\rho(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\overline{\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q)})^{\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1}}{(\sigma_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{i-1}^{2}-1)!}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}\sum_{k_{i}=0}^{\infty}\frac{(i(2\pi)^{d}\rho(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))^{k_{i}}}{k_{i}!}\sum_{r_{i}=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-i(2\pi)^{d}\rho(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\overline{\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q)})^{r_{i}}}{r_{i}!}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}e^{(2\pi)^{d}\rho(s_{i-1}-s_{i})(i\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q)-i\overline{\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q)})}=((2\pi)^{d}\rho)^{n}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}e^{-(2\pi)^{d}\rho(s_{i-1}-s_{i})2\im(\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))},

where we in the last equality have used the “optical theorem” (
1.4
). Combining (
11.41
), (
11.42
), (
11.48
) and (
11.49
) we obtain the desired result and this concludes the proof.
∎

12
Proof of Theorem
1.2

We are considering the inner product

\langle U_{\hbar,\lambda}(t)\OpW(a)U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle=\langle\OpW(a)U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle,

(12.1)

where
\{\varphi_{\hbar}\}_{\hbar\in I}
is a uniform semiclassical family in
\mathcal{H}_{\hbar}^{5d+5}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with Wigner measure
\mu_{0}
. From Proposition
3.16
we have for any
\tau_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
that

U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)=U_{\hbar,0}(-t)+\sum_{i=0}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{k=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)+\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar),

(12.2)

where
k_{00}=1
,
k_{11}=3
,
k_{21}=5
,
k_{22}=4
. The operator
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)
is defined in the proposition and
the operators
\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k,\bm{x},\alpha,t;\hbar)
are defined in Definition
3.6
and
3.12
. By applying Proposition
3.16
twice with

k_{0}=\frac{|\log(\hbar)|}{10\log(|\log(\hbar)|)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\tau_{0}=\hbar^{-\frac{1}{3}}.

(12.3)

we get the expansion

\displaystyle\langle\OpW(a)U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar},U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle

(12.4)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{M}(k_{0},\hbar)+\mathcal{E}_{1}(k_{0};\hbar)+\mathcal{E}_{2}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)+\mathcal{E}_{3}(k_{0};\hbar)+\mathcal{E}_{4}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar).

where the main term
\mathcal{M}(k_{0},\hbar)
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(k_{0},\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{k,r=1}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k}}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{r}}\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle,

(12.5)

where we have used the convention
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(0,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)=U_{\hbar,0}(-t)
. The error terms are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}(k_{0};\hbar)=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{r=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\langle\OpW(a)U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar},\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(r,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle,

(12.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)=\langle\OpW(a)U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle,

(12.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}(k_{0};\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{k=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{r=0}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{r}}\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle,

(12.8)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{4}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)={}

\displaystyle\sum_{r=0}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\bm{\tilde{x}}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{r}}\langle\OpW(a)\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar},\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle.

(12.9)

We will consider the asymptotics for each term separately. We start with
\mathcal{E}_{1}(k_{0};\hbar)
.
By using the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{1}(k_{0};\hbar)
, linearity of the integral and Cauchy-Schwarz for the inner product in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
we get the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{1}(k_{0};\hbar)|]\leq{}

\displaystyle\lVert\OpW(a)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{r=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\Big]

(12.10)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\lVert\OpW(a)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{j=\min(1,i)}^{i}\sum_{r=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}},

where we in the last inequality have used Jensen’s inequality. From Lemma
10.3
we get that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\sum_{r=k_{ij}}^{k_{0}}\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal{Q}_{k,i,j}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{k-i}}\mathcal{I}_{i,j}(k,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

(12.11)

for all
(i,j)\in\{(1,1),(2,1),(2,3)\}
. Since we have that both
\lVert\OpW(a)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
and
\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
are uniformly bounded for
\hbar\in I
it follows by combining (
12.10
) and (
12.11
) that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{1}(k_{0};\hbar)|]=0.

(12.12)

For the second term we get from the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{2}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)
, linearity of the integral, Cauchy-Schwarz for the inner product in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and Jensen’s inequality we get the estimate

\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{2}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)|]\leq\lVert\OpW(a)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(12.13)

Lemma
10.1
gives us that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\mathcal{R}(\tau_{0},k_{0}:\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}=0.

(12.14)

Hence from combining (
12.13
) and (
12.14
) it follows that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{2}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)|]=0,

(12.15)

since
\lVert\OpW(a)\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
and
\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
are uniformly bounded for
\hbar\in I
. To obtain that

\lim_{\hbar\rightarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{3}(k_{0};\hbar)|]+\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{4}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)|]=0,

(12.16)

we can argue as above. Except when we use our assumption to say that
\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
is uniformly bounded for
\hbar\in I
we now need to use Lemma
10.2
to get the uniform bound

\displaystyle\sup_{\hbar\in I}\sum_{r=0}^{k_{0}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\lVert\sum_{\bm{x}\in\mathcal{X}_{\neq}^{r}}\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(r,\bm{x},\iota,t;\hbar)\varphi_{\hbar}\big\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\Big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C,

where we use our assumptions on
k_{0}
. We now turn to the main term
\mathcal{M}(k_{0},\hbar)
. We will in the following let
\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}(k_{0},\hbar)
be defined as in (
12.5
) but with
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
and
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)
replaced by
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(k,\bm{x},t;\hbar)
and
\mathcal{I}_{0,0}^{\gamma}(r,\bm{\tilde{x}},t;\hbar)
respectively. Then by Lemma
9.1
it follows that

\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\sup_{\hbar\in I}\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{M}(\infty,\hbar)-\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}(\infty,\hbar)|]=0.

(12.17)

Moreover for
\gamma\geq 0
we let

\displaystyle f^{\gamma}(t,y,q_{0})={}

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{((2\pi)^{d+1}\rho)^{n}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}\int a(y-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})|\hat{\varphi}_{1}(q_{0})|^{2}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{i=1}^{n}\delta(\tfrac{1}{2}(q_{i}^{2}-q_{i-1}^{2}))|\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q_{i},q_{i-1})|^{2}\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}e^{-(2\pi)^{d}\rho(s_{i-1}-s_{i})2\im(\hat{T}^{\gamma}(q,q))}\,d\bm{q}d\bm{s}

and we will use the convention
f(t,y,q_{0})=f^{0}(t,y,q_{0})
. Note that by definition we have that

\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}f^{\gamma}(t,y,q_{0})=f(t,y,q_{0}).

(12.18)

Then using (
12.4
) we get that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle-\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}(x,p)]|

(12.19)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{M}(\infty,\hbar)-\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}(\infty,\hbar)|]+\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{1}(k_{0};\hbar)|]+\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{2}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)|]+\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{3}(k_{0};\hbar)|]+\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{E}_{4}(k_{0},\tau_{0};\hbar)|]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}[|\mathcal{M}^{\gamma}(\infty,\hbar)-\int f^{\gamma}(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}(x,p)|]+|\int f^{\gamma}(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}(x,p)-\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}(x,p)|.

Equations

12.12
,
12.15
,
12.16
,
12.17
,
12.18
and
11.9
gives us that by first taking
\hbar
to zero and then taking
\gamma
to zero the bound in (
12.19
) will go to zero. This implies the desired convergence and concludes the proof.

13
Proof of Theorem
1.3

Before we give a proof of Theorem
1.3
we will make the following observations regarding the solution to the linear Boltzmann equation when the collision kernel is as in Theorem
1.2
.

Observation 13.1
.

Firstly recall that from Remark
3.3
we have that

\hat{T}(p,p_{0})=-i\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(i\lambda)^{n}\Psi_{n}(p,p_{0},\infty;V).

(13.1)

Applying Lemma
3.2
and under the condition
\lambda C_{d}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,2n+2}<1
we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\hat{T}(p,p_{0})|\,dp_{0}\leq\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\lambda^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\Psi_{n}(p,p_{0},\infty;V)|\,dp_{0}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(\lambda C_{d}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,2n+2})^{n}=C

(13.2)

\displaystyle\sup_{p,p_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\hat{T}(p,p_{0})|\leq\sup_{p,p_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\lambda^{n}|\Psi_{n}(p,p_{0},\infty;V)|\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(\lambda C_{d}\lVert\hat{V}\rVert_{1,\infty,2n+2})^{n}=C,

where
C
is some constant. Secondly recall that the solution of the linear Boltzmann equation (
1.9
) can be expressed as the collision series

f(t,x,p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}f^{(n)}(t,x,p),

(13.3)

where

\displaystyle f^{(n)}(t,x,q_{0})=\int_{[0,t]_{\leq}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{nd}}

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Sigma(q_{i},q_{i-1})\prod_{i=1}^{n+1}e^{-(s_{i-1}-s_{i})\Sigma_{\text{\rm tot}}(q)}

\displaystyle\times a(x-tq_{0}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}s_{i}(q_{i}-q_{i-1}),q_{n})\,d\bm{q}_{1,n}d\bm{s}_{n,1},

where we assume the initial data
a
is Schwartz class and

\Sigma(p,q)=(2\pi)^{d+1}\rho\,\delta(\tfrac{1}{2}p^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}q^{2})|\hat{T}(p,q)|^{2}.

Applying the two estimates from (
13.2
) and using that
\Sigma_{\text{\rm tot}}(q)
is positive for all
q
it follows that

\displaystyle|f^{(n)}(t,x,q_{0})|\leq\frac{(tC^{2})^{n}}{n!}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int|a(x,q_{n})|\,dq_{n}.

Combining this estimate with (
13.3
) we obtain that

\sup_{x,p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|f(t,x,p)|\leq C

(13.4)

for some constant
C
. Moreover we also have that
f(t,x,p)
is continuous in
x
and
p
.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Since Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.2
are equivalent we will only prove that the assertion of Theorem
1.2
is still valid. Hence
let
A_{\hbar}(t)=U_{\hbar,\lambda}(t)\,A_{\hbar}\,U_{\hbar,\lambda}(-t)
be the Heisenberg evolution of
A_{\hbar}=\OpW(a)
, where
a\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{2d})
. Then by applying Cauchy-Schwartz we get that

\displaystyle|\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar}^{n},\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\rangle-\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|\langle A_{\hbar}(t)(\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}-\varphi_{\hbar}),\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\rangle-\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}-\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\OpW(a))\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}-\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}(\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}).

By assumption this inequality implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow 0}\sup_{\hbar\in I}|\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar}^{n},\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\rangle-\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle|=0.

(13.5)

We have that for each
n
the families
\{\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\}_{\hbar\in I}
will satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
. Hence we get the convergence

\mathbb{E}\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar}^{n},\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\rangle\to\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}^{n}(x,p)\qquad\text{as $\hbar\rightarrow 0$}.

(13.6)

We note that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle-\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}(x,p)|

(13.7)

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar},\varphi_{\hbar}\rangle-\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar}^{n},\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\rangle]|+|\mathbb{E}\langle A_{\hbar}(t)\varphi_{\hbar}^{n},\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\rangle-\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}^{n}(x,p)|

\displaystyle+|\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}^{n}(x,p)-\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}(x,p)|.

It follows form (
13.5
) that the first term in this upper bound can be made small and that the second term can be made small follows from (
13.6
). Finally since the sequence
\{\mu_{0}^{n}\}
converges weakly to
\mu_{0}
and
f(t,x,p)
is a bounded continuous function in
x
and
p
as observed in Observation
13.1
it follows that the third term in the upper bound in (
13.7
) can be made small. Hence we have obtained the desired convergence and this ends the proof.
∎

We end this section by giving two examples of well approximated states. These are two particular cases of Lagrangian states. These two types of states are

\varphi_{1,\hbar}(x)=w(x)e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,p_{0}\rangle}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varphi_{2,\hbar}(x)=\hbar^{-d/2}w(\hbar^{-1}(x-x_{0})),

(13.8)

where we will only assume that
w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The associated Wigner measures are

\mu_{0}^{1}(x,p)=|w(x)|^{2}\delta(p-p_{0})\,dx\,dp\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mu_{0}^{2}(x,p)=\delta(x-x_{0})|\hat{w}(p)|^{2}\,dx\,dp.

(13.9)

To find the approximating states we let for all
\varepsilon>0

\psi_{\varepsilon}
be a standard mollifier. That is
\psi_{\varepsilon}(x)=\varepsilon^{-d}\psi(\varepsilon^{-1}x)
, where
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, positive with support contained in the unit ball and integrating to 1. We set

w_{\varepsilon}(x)=\psi_{\varepsilon}*w(x)=\int\psi_{\varepsilon}(x-y)w(y)\,dy.

It is standard results that
w_{\varepsilon}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and that

\lVert w_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C\lVert w\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\lVert w_{\varepsilon}-w\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0.

(13.10)

Moreover for each fixed
\varepsilon>0
we have that
w_{\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{H}^{5d+5}_{1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This implies that for each fixed
\varepsilon
we will have that the Lagrangian states

\varphi_{1,\hbar}^{\varepsilon}(x)=w_{\varepsilon}(x)e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,p_{0}\rangle}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varphi_{2,\hbar}^{\varepsilon}(x)=\hbar^{-d/2}w_{\varepsilon}(\hbar^{-1}(x-x_{0}))

(13.11)

will satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
1.2
. Moreover, we have in particular that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{\hbar\in(0,1]}\lVert\varphi_{\hbar}-\varphi_{\hbar}^{n}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0,

(13.12)

where we have chosen
\varepsilon=\frac{1}{n}
. What remains is to verify that the measures will converge weakly.
The two associated Wigner measures
\mu_{0,\varepsilon}^{1}
and
\mu_{0,\varepsilon}^{2}
are given by

\mu_{0,\varepsilon}^{1}(x,p)=|w_{\varepsilon}(x)|^{2}\delta(p-p_{0})\,dx\,dp\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mu_{0,\varepsilon}^{2}(x,p)=\delta(x-x_{0})|\hat{w}_{\varepsilon}(p)|^{2}\,dx\,dp.

(13.13)

With this note that for any continuous and bounded function
f
we get that

\displaystyle\big|\int f(x,p)\,d\mu_{0,\varepsilon}^{1}(x,p)-\int f(x,p)\,d\mu_{0}^{1}(x,p)\big|

(13.14)

\displaystyle\leq\big|\int f(x,p_{0})(|w_{\varepsilon}(x)|^{2}-|w(x)|^{2})\,dx\big|\leq C(\lVert w_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}-\lVert w\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}).

This inequality implies that
\{\mu_{0,\varepsilon}^{1}\}
converges weakly to
\mu_{0}^{1}
. Before we consider the measure
\mu_{0,\varepsilon}^{2}(x,p)
we note that

\hat{w}_{\varepsilon}(p)=\widehat{\psi_{\varepsilon}*w}(p)=\hat{\psi}(\varepsilon p)\hat{w}(p)\to\hat{w}(p)\quad\text{as $\varepsilon\rightarrow 0$},

(13.15)

where the convergence result follows since
\psi
integrates to one. With this in mind we see that

\displaystyle\big|\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0,\varepsilon}^{2}(x,p)-\int f(t,x,p)\,d\mu_{0}^{2}(x,p)\big|

(13.16)

\displaystyle\leq\big|\int f(t,x_{0},p)(|\hat{w}_{\varepsilon}(p)|^{2}-|\hat{w}(p)|^{2})\,dp\big|\leq C\int|(\hat{\psi}(\varepsilon x)|^{2}-1)|\hat{w}(p)|^{2}\,dp.

Since we have now constructed these two approximating sequences it follows form Theorem
1.3
that the statement of Theorem
1.2
is indeed valid for states of the form

\varphi_{1,\hbar}(x)=w(x)e^{i\hbar^{-1}\langle x,p_{0}\rangle}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\varphi_{2,\hbar}(x)=\hbar^{-d/2}w(\hbar^{-1}(x-x_{0})),

(13.17)

where we only assume
w\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.

Appendix A
Proofs of integral estimates

Proof of Lemma
2.4
.

Recall that we have
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
given and want to establish the estimate

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{d+1}|x-y|}\,dx\leq\frac{C}{\langle y\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

(A.1)

We split into the two cases if
|y|>4
or
|y|\leq 4
. For the latter case we have that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{d+1}|x-y|}\,dx\leq C.

(A.2)

This estimate is true for a suitable choice of constant since the integral is finite for any value of
y
. Hence we are left with the case where
|y|>4
for this case we split the integral in the following way

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{d+1}|x-y|}\,dx=\int_{B(y,\sqrt{|y|})}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{d+1}|x-y|}\,dx+\int_{B(y,\sqrt{|y|})^{c}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{d+1}|x-y|}\,dx.

(A.3)

For the second integral in the right hand side of (
A.3
) we have that

\int_{B(y,\sqrt{|y|})^{c}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{d+1}|x-y|}\,dx\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{|y|}}\int_{B(y,\sqrt{|y|})^{c}}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{d+1}}\,dx\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{|y|}}.

(A.4)

For the first integral of the left hand side of (
A.3
) we have by switching to spherical coordinates that

\displaystyle\int_{B(y,\sqrt{y})}\frac{1}{\langle x\rangle^{d+1}|x-y|}\,dx

\displaystyle=\int_{B(0,\sqrt{y})}\frac{1}{\langle x+y\rangle^{d+1}|x|}

(A.5)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\sqrt{y}}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{r^{d-2}(1-z^{2})^{d-3}}{(1+r^{2}+|y|^{2}+2|y|rz)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}\,dzdr.

We observe that for any positive
r
and
z\in[-1,1]
we have that
r^{2}+|y|^{2}+2|y|rz\geq(|y|-r)^{2}
. Moreover, on the domain of integration and since
|y|>4
we have that
|y|-r\geq\sqrt{|y|}
. Using this we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\sqrt{y}}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{r^{d-2}(1-z^{2})^{d-3}}{(1+r^{2}+|y|^{2}+2|y|rz)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}\,dzdr

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{\sqrt{y}}\frac{r^{d-2}}{(1+|y|)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}\,dr

(A.6)

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{|y|^{\frac{d}{2}}}{(1+|y|)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}}\leq C\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{\frac{1}{2}}}

From combining (
A.2
), (
A.3
), (
A.4
) and (
A.6
) we obtain the desired result.
∎

Proof of estimate (
2.13
).

Recall that
\zeta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
and we want to prove

\sup_{p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\langle\nu\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,d\nu\leq C|\log(\zeta)|.

(A.7)

We observe that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\langle\nu\rangle|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\nu^{2}}\sqrt{(\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu)^{2}+\zeta^{2}}}\,d\nu

(A.8)

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+\nu^{2}}\sqrt{\nu^{2}+\zeta^{2}}}\,d\nu

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+\nu)(\nu+\zeta)}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=2C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\nu+\zeta}-\frac{1}{\nu+1}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=2C[\log(\nu+\zeta)-\log(\nu+1)]_{\nu=0}^{\infty}=2C|\log(\zeta)|.

This gives us the estimate in (
A.7
).
∎

Proof of equation (
2.14
).

Recall that
\zeta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
and we want to prove the estimate

\sup_{\nu\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp\leq C|\log(\zeta)|.

(A.9)

We first do a change of variables to spherical coordinates this gives us

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{1+\nu^{2}}r^{d-1}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}\sqrt{(\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu)^{2}+\zeta^{2}}}\,dr,

(A.10)

where
C
only depend on the dimension. Set
u=r^{2}
by a change of variables we get

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{1+\nu^{2}}r^{d-1}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}\sqrt{(\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu)^{2}+\zeta^{2}}}\,dr

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{1+\nu^{2}}u^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}{(1+u)^{\frac{d+1}{2}}\sqrt{(\frac{1}{2}u+\nu)^{2}+\zeta^{2}}}\,du

(A.11)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+|\nu|}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{(\frac{1}{2}u+\nu)^{2}+\zeta^{2}}}\,du

Using the inequality
\sqrt{a^{2}+b^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(|a|+|b|)
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+|\nu|}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}\sqrt{(\frac{1}{2}u+\nu)^{2}+\zeta^{2}}}\,du\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+|\nu|}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u+\nu|+\zeta)}\,du

(A.12)

We now divide into the two cases
\nu\geq 0
and
\nu<0
. For the first case we have that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+|\nu|}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u+\nu|+\zeta)}\,du

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+\nu}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{1}{2}u+\nu+\zeta)}\,du

(A.13)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{1}{2}u+\nu+\zeta)}\,du+\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\nu}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{1}{2}u+\nu+\zeta)}\,du

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+u)(\frac{1}{2}u+\zeta)}\,du+\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,du

\displaystyle\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C

In the case
\nu<0
we will assume
\nu>0
and write
-\nu
in the equation. That is we consider the integral

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+\nu}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u-\nu|+\zeta)}\,du

(A.14)

First we consider the case where
\nu\in(0,1]
. For this case we have that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+\nu}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u-\nu|+\zeta)}\,du

(A.15)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{2\nu}^{\infty}\frac{2}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{1}{2}u-\nu+\zeta)}\,du+\int_{0}^{2\nu}\frac{2}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(\nu-\frac{1}{2}u+\zeta)}\,du

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{2}{(1+u+2\nu)^{\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{1}{2}u+\zeta)}\,du+\int_{0}^{2\nu}\frac{2}{(1+2\nu-u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(\frac{1}{2}u+\zeta)}\,du

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{2}{(1+u)(\frac{1}{2}u+\zeta)}\,du+\int_{0}^{2}\frac{2}{\frac{1}{2}u+\zeta}\,du\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C

Lastly we assume
\nu>1
. Here we do the change of variables
u\mapsto\nu^{-1}u
. This gives us

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1+\nu}{(1+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u-\nu|+\zeta)}\,du=\frac{1+\nu}{\nu^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u-1|+\frac{\zeta}{\nu})}\,du

(A.16)

To estimate this integral we split the integral up in 4 parts and estimate each separately. We have that

\displaystyle\frac{1+\nu}{\nu^{3/2}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u-1|+\frac{\zeta}{\nu})}\,du

(A.17)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+\nu}{\nu^{3/2}}\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\zeta}{\nu}}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,du=\frac{4(1+\nu)}{(\nu+2\zeta)\sqrt{\nu}}\big[\sqrt{\nu}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu^{-1}+1}}\big]\leq\frac{8(1+\nu)}{\nu+2\zeta}\leq\frac{16}{1+2\zeta}

\displaystyle\frac{1+\nu}{\nu^{3/2}}\int_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u-1|+\frac{\zeta}{\nu})}\,du\leq\frac{1+\nu}{\nu^{3/2}}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+1)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{1}^{2}\frac{1}{1-\frac{1}{2}u+\frac{\zeta}{\nu}}\,du

(A.18)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(\nu+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big[\log(\tfrac{1}{2}+\frac{\zeta}{\nu})-\log(\frac{\zeta}{\nu})\big]\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C

\displaystyle\frac{1+\nu}{\nu^{3/2}}\int_{2}^{3}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u-1|+\frac{\zeta}{\nu})}\,du\leq\frac{1+\nu}{\nu^{3/2}}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+2)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{2}^{3}\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}u-1+\frac{\zeta}{\nu}}\,du

(A.19)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{(2\nu+1)^{\frac{1}{2}}}\big[\log(\tfrac{1}{2}+\frac{\zeta}{\nu})-\log(\frac{\zeta}{\nu})\big]\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C

\displaystyle\frac{1+\nu}{\nu^{3/2}}\int_{3}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\frac{1}{2}u-1|+\frac{\zeta}{\nu})}\,du

(A.20)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\zeta}{\nu}}\int_{3}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(\frac{1}{\nu}+u)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,du=\frac{4(1+\nu)}{(\nu+2\zeta)\sqrt{\nu}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu^{-1}+3}}\leq\frac{4(1+\nu)}{(\nu+2\zeta)}\leq\frac{8}{(1+2\zeta)}

Combining all of these estimates we get that (
A.9
) is valid.
∎

Proof of estimate (
2.15
).

Again we have that
\zeta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
. Here we consider the integral

\sup_{q\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\nu\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{|p-q|}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.21)

We start by changing to spherical coordinates, where we measure the angular variable against the fixed vector
q
. This gives us the following expression ofr the integral.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{|p-q|}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.22)

\displaystyle=c\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{r^{2}-2r|q|\theta+|q|^{2}}}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle r^{d-1}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,d\theta dr

\displaystyle=c\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle r^{d-1}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{r^{2}-2r|q|\theta+|q|^{2}}}\,d\theta dr

Evaluating the
\theta
integral yields

\displaystyle\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{r^{2}-2r|q|\theta+|q|^{2}}}\,d\theta

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{r|q|}[\sqrt{r^{2}-2r|q|\theta+|q|^{2}}]_{\theta=-1}^{\theta=1}=\frac{r+|q|-|r-|q||}{r|q|}.

(A.23)

Using this we split the integral over
r
into two parts according to if
r\geq|q|
or
r<|q|
. This gives us that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{|p-q|}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.24)

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{0}^{|q|}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle r^{d-1}}{|q|(1+r^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}dr+c\int_{|q|}^{\infty}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle r^{d-2}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}dr

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle r^{d-2}}{(1+r^{2})^{\frac{d+1}{2}}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}dr\leq C|\log(\zeta)|,

where the last inequality is obtained similar to the estimates obtained in the two previous proofs. This gives us the desired estimate.
∎

Proof of estimate (
2.16
).

We now consider the fourth estimate of Lemma
2.5
. That is the estimate

\sup_{q\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\nu\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp\leq C|\log(\zeta)|,

(A.25)

where again
\zeta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
. For the case where
|q|=0
we get the estimate from (
2.14
) so we may assume
|q|>0
. We will consider the three cases
\nu\geq 0

\nu\in(0,-1]
and
\nu<-1
. For
\nu\geq 0
we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.26)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{|p|\leq 1\}}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+i\zeta|}\,dp+\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}+\zeta}\int_{\{|p|>1\}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}}\,dp

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{1}\frac{r^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}{\frac{1}{2}r+\zeta}\,dr+\frac{C}{\frac{1}{2}+\zeta}\leq C[\log(\tfrac{1}{2}+\zeta)-\log(\zeta)]+\frac{C}{\frac{1}{2}+\zeta}\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C

For the case when
\nu\in(0,-1]
we will write
-\nu
in he equation and think of
\nu
being in
(0,1]
. We have here that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.27)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\{|p|\leq 2\}}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp+\frac{1}{1+\zeta}\int_{\{|p|>2\}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}}\,dp

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{4}\frac{r^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}{|\frac{1}{2}r-\nu|+\zeta}\,dr+C\leq C\int_{0}^{2\nu}\frac{1}{\nu-\frac{1}{2}r+\zeta}\,dr+C\int_{2\nu}^{4}\frac{1}{\frac{1}{2}r-\nu+\zeta}\,dr+C

\displaystyle=C[\log(\nu+\zeta)-\log(\zeta)+\log(2-\nu+\zeta)-\log(\zeta)]+C\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C.

What remains is the case where
\nu<-1
. So We are considering the integral

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp,

(A.28)

where we again think of
\nu>1
. We will here consider the two cases where either
\nu\leq|q|^{4}+2
or
\nu>|q|^{4}+2
. We start with the second case and we let
\tilde{\nu}
be the number
\tilde{\nu}=\nu-|q|^{4}-2
. So when
\nu
is large is it either because
\tilde{\nu}
is large or
|q|
. We define the set

A_{\nu}=\{p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,|\,\sqrt{2\nu-2}\leq|p|\leq\sqrt{2\nu+2}\}
. With this set we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.29)

\displaystyle=\int_{A_{\nu}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp+\int_{A_{\nu}^{c}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp.

For the last integral we have that

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\nu}^{c}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp\leq\frac{C}{1+\zeta}\int_{A_{\nu}^{c}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}}\leq\frac{C}{1+\zeta}.

(A.30)

For the second integral we note that for
p\in A_{\nu}
we have that

|p-q|\geq\min_{p\in A_{\nu}}(|p|)-|q|=\sqrt{2\nu-2}-|q|=\sqrt{2\tilde{\nu}+2|q|^{4}+2}-|q|>0

(A.31)

With this observation we get that

\displaystyle\int_{A_{\nu}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp\leq\frac{1}{\langle\sqrt{2\tilde{\nu}+2|q|^{4}+2}-|q|\rangle^{d+1}}\int_{A_{\nu}}\frac{1}{|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu|+\zeta}\,dp

(A.32)

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\langle\sqrt{2\tilde{\nu}+2|q|^{4}+2}-|q|\rangle^{d+1}}\int_{\sqrt{2\nu-2}}^{\sqrt{2\nu+2}}\frac{r^{d-1}}{|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}-\nu|+\zeta}\,dr

\displaystyle=\frac{C}{\langle\sqrt{2\tilde{\nu}+2|q|^{4}+2}-|q|\rangle^{d+1}}\int_{\nu-1}^{\nu+1}\frac{r^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}{|r-\nu|+\zeta}\,dr

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C(\tilde{\nu}+|q|^{4}+2)^{\frac{d-2}{2}}}{\langle\sqrt{2\tilde{\nu}+2|q|^{4}+2}-|q|\rangle^{d+1}}\Big[\int_{\nu-1}^{\nu}\frac{1}{\nu-r+\zeta}\,dr+\int_{\nu}^{\nu+1}\frac{1}{r-\nu+\zeta}\,dr\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\Big[\log(1+\zeta)-\log(\zeta)\Big]\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C

What remains is the case where
\nu\leq|q|^{4}+2
. Here we will further divide into the two cases
|q|\leq 1
and
|q|>1
. For the first case we divide the integral into one part over the ball of radius
5
centred at zero and the rest. Then each of these integrals is estimated as above. In the case
|q|>1
we start with the change of variables
p\mapsto p-q
. This gives us

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{1}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}(p+q)^{2}-\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.33)

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{r^{d-1}}{\langle r\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+r|q|\theta-\nu+i\zeta|}\,d\theta dr,

where we in the last equality have change to spherical coordinates, where the angular variables is measured against the fixed vector
q
. We now do the change of variables
r\mapsto r^{2}
and
\theta\mapsto\sqrt{r}|q|\theta
This gives us

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{r^{d-1}}{\langle r\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+r|q|\theta-\nu+i\zeta|}\,d\theta dr

(A.34)

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\sqrt{r}|q|}^{\sqrt{r}|q|}\frac{r^{\frac{d-3}{2}}}{|q|\langle\sqrt{r}\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+\theta-\nu+i\zeta|}\,d\theta dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\sqrt{r}|q|}^{\sqrt{r}|q|}\frac{1}{|q|(1+r)^{2}|\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+\theta-\nu+i\zeta|}\,d\theta dr

Next we do the change of variables
\theta\mapsto\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+\theta-\nu
. With this change of variables the bounds on our integral in
\theta
becomes
\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+\sqrt{r}|q|-\nu
and
\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}-\sqrt{r}|q|-\nu
. Let
g(r)=\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+\sqrt{r}|q|+|q|^{4}+2
. Then we have that

\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+\sqrt{r}|q|-\nu\leq g(r)\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}-\sqrt{r}|q|-\nu>-g(r).

(A.35)

Hence we get that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-\sqrt{r}|q|}^{\sqrt{r}|q|}\frac{1}{|q|(1+r)^{2}|\frac{1}{2}r+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}+\theta-\nu+i\zeta|}\,d\theta dr

(A.36)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{|q|(1+r)^{2}}\int_{-g(r)}^{g(r)}\frac{1}{|\theta|+\zeta}\,d\theta dr=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{|q|(1+r)^{2}}\int_{0}^{g(r)}\frac{1}{\theta+\zeta}\,d\theta dr

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{|q|(1+r)^{2}}\big[\log(g(r)+\zeta)-\log(\zeta)\big]dr

\displaystyle\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C\frac{\log(g(r)+\zeta)}{|q|\sqrt{(1+r)}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(1+r)^{\frac{3}{2}}}dr\leq C|\log(\zeta)|+C,

where we have used the definition of
g(r)
to conclude the the fraction is bounded recalling that
|q|>1
. Combing the estimates from above we get the desired conclusion. This ends the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.6
.

Recall that
\zeta\in(0,1/2)
and we want to prove the following estimate

\sup_{\nu,\tilde{\nu}\in\mathbb{R}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta||\frac{1}{2}(p-q)^{2}+\tilde{\nu}+i\zeta|}\,dp\leq\frac{C\log(\zeta)^{2}}{|q|+\zeta},

(A.37)

First we assume that
|q|>0
. Then we do a change of variables to spherical coordinates, where the angular momentum is measure against the fixed vector
q
. This gives us

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta||\frac{1}{2}(p-q)^{2}+\tilde{\nu}+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.38)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{-1}^{1}\frac{r^{d-1}\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle}{\langle r\rangle^{d+1}\langle\sqrt{r^{2}+|q|^{2}-2r|q|\theta}\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu+i\zeta||\frac{1}{2}(r^{2}+|q|^{2}-2r|q|\theta)+\tilde{\nu}+i\zeta|}\,d\theta dr

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|q|}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{r^{d-2}\langle\nu\rangle}{\langle r\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|dr}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle}{\langle\sqrt{\theta}\rangle^{d+1}|\theta+\tilde{\nu}+i\zeta|}\,d\theta,

where we in the last inequality first have extended the integration domain to the whole line for
\theta
and then done the change of variables
\theta\mapsto\frac{1}{2}r^{2}+\frac{1}{2}|q|^{2}-r|q|\theta
. Applying Lemma
2.5
we get that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta||\frac{1}{2}(p-q)^{2}+\tilde{\nu}+i\zeta|}\,dp\leq\frac{C\log(\zeta)^{2}}{|q|}.

(A.39)

For any
q\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle\langle\tilde{\nu}\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}\langle p-q\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta||\frac{1}{2}(p-q)^{2}+\tilde{\nu}+i\zeta|}\,dp

(A.40)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\zeta}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{\langle\nu\rangle}{\langle p\rangle^{d+1}|\frac{1}{2}p^{2}+\nu+i\zeta|}\,dp\leq\frac{C|\log(\zeta)|}{\zeta},

where we again have used Lemma
2.5
. Combining the two estimates we obtain the estimate in (
A.37
).
∎
